# Room review list

One section per room type in `src/roomplanner/data/rooms/*.yaml` (203 rooms).

Process: a review agent first designs 10 instances of the room from its own knowledge (no rules, no generator),
then generates rooms with the generator and compares both. Findings go into the room's entry below.

Purpose: find optimizations that make the generated rooms look more realistic. Findings are concrete, prioritised
improvements (layout, furniture choice and counts, grouping, spacing, orientation, walkways, missing details),
phrased generally ("these must be arranged in rows", "this must face the door"), not as code or YAML changes.
Status: `todo` / `done`.

## casino.yaml
### gaming_floor
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order (details below): (1) fill the hall like a real casino: many more slots, as aligned back-to-back islands with 1:1 stools, instead of a sparse perimeter ring plus a few random strips (YAML: `slot_machine per: 8, count: [6, 120]`, `slot_bank` as `grid`/row placement with `aisle: 3`, count scaled by area); (2) table games in pits: tables in rows with real aisles between sets, stools or rail at roulette/craps, correct (smaller) roulette footprint (YAML: put roulette/craps on the same grid as `card_table_set`, `aisle: 3`); (3) a believable bar: short wall bar or island with stools (6-8 cells, not a 18x4 slab) (YAML: cap bar length, `placement: wall`, gate fridge/shelf by wealth); (4) door area: guard beside the door not in the aisle, ATM/change booth near the exit, second emergency exit; (5) details: cameras, pit-boss stand, poker/sports-book/stage/lounge by wealth, plants/pillars for luxury.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x8 m dive to 30x20 m luxury): slot rows along walls plus back-to-back slot islands (stool each, ~1.5 m deep, 1.5 m side aisles, 3 m main aisle from the door); table games in pit clusters (BJ/roulette/craps/baccarat, 1 m behind the players, dealer side inside a pit, a pit-boss stand); a bar island or corner bar with stools; cage window and ATMs on the wall; cameras every ~6 m; 2+ exits far apart, guard at the door; luxury adds a baccarat salon, poker room, stage, pillars, plants; Shadowrun flavour: trode/jack-in booths, drones, sentries. Generated 12 floors (16x12 to 60x40 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] Bar is wrong: a `bar_set` came out as a 9x2 m (18x4 cell) bar counter with a fridge and shelves on the wall, standing as a huge slab with only 4 stools (g1, g11, g6: 15x3 cells counter in the middle of the room). It is the biggest object on small floors and eats the aisle. Cap the bar to 6-8 cells long, put it against a wall or make it a short island with stools on both sides; scale its count with area.
  - [high] Roulette set is oversized (about 9x5 cells = 4.5x2.5 m, real table about 3.3x1.6 m with the dealer 1 m behind) and appears with no player stools in several results (g1, g2, g4, g6: roulette/craps without seats, or the seats of another table). Check the footprint of `roulette_set`/`craps_table`; roulette and craps are standing games, but roulette should have 4-6 stools or a standing rail; the table should carry a dealer spot.
  - [high] Table placement is ad hoc: tables touch each other with no aisle (g4: craps and roulette abut; g10: four tables in a plus cluster, one table's seats against another table's back), a card table is jammed under the north slot row (g11, no walkway between table and slots) and seat rows sit directly against neighbours. Needs a real aisle rule between table sets (>= 2 cells, 3 on the main route) and a pit layout: tables in rows with a common dealer-side aisle, instead of `placement: center` plus `grid` with random leftovers. `margin: 4` / `aisle: 3` on card_table_set does not hold for roulette/craps, which are placed independently.
  - [high] Slot machines are sparse for the floor size: 30x20 cells (15x10 m, 150 m2) gets about 30 slots, a 60x40 floor (600 m2) only 40 (cap `count: [6, 40]`). A real hall has 1 slot per 2-3 m2 (a 600 m2 floor holds 100+). Raise the cap (to ~120), use `per: 8`, and add proper slot islands: `slot_bank` is only `scatter` with `count: [0, 4]` and is scattered at random as 1x4 strips (g10: three islands in odd positions with stools on both sides, one island crammed against the east wall) with no alignment to the perimeter rows. Use grid/row placement for islands: rows of 4-6 back-to-back machines with 3-cell aisles between them, parallel to the entrance axis.
  - [medium] Slot stools are placed unevenly: several machines have no stool, others 2 stools stacked in corners (g1 north-west, g2 south-west corner), and stools beside a machine in the corner block the aisle. Stools should be 1:1 with machines, none in corners.
  - [medium] Guard desk (7x3 cells) sits right in front of the door, in the entry line and 3 cells deep, shrinking the door aisle on small floors (g1, g6, g11). A casino has the door guard beside the door, not in front of it; use `placement: wall` next to the door and make it smaller (a 1-cell stand) unless it is a security post for a metal detector.
  - [medium] No cameras (the surveillance room exists, but the floor has no camera objects at all), no pit-boss/floor-supervisor stand, no cashier or change booth (the cash_cage is a separate room that is not always adjacent), no pillars. Add `camera` (ceiling/wall, count by area, always), a `change_booth`/ATM next to the exit, and for luxury a baccarat salon or high-roller door.
  - [medium] Missing Shadowrun flavour and style differences: dive casino and luxury casino look the same apart from table count; no carpet/theme change, no stage, no poker table type, no sports-book screens, no trode/jack-in booths. Add wealth-gated lines: `wall_screen` (sports book/odds board), `poker_table_set` (middle+), `stage` + `bar` for high/luxury, `sofa_set` lounge, `jackpot` signs; a `rug`/pillar line for luxury.
  - [medium] Exits: floors only have the one entrance (generated alone; in a building the engine must give it a second exit as above 100 m2 / more than 50 persons need 2 exits, ASR A2.3/MVStättVO, and it is a large assembly hall). Make `exits: 2` (or an emergency door on the opposite side) a required property of this room so the validator checks it. Also `door_width: 4` (2 m) is good for the main door; the fire exit must be >= 1.2 m.
  - [low] Plants only in some seeds (1-4 per floor but in corners only) and trash bins are tiny; both fine, but ATMs are placed along walls near random slots (g1: left side) rather than near the entrance/cage. Prefer `near_exit`.
  - [low] Walls get fridge/shelf from the bar_set on the east wall (g1, g11) reading as a storeroom; fine for a dive, not for luxury. Gate by wealth or reduce.
### high_roller_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m suite to 20x12 m mega salon): one double entrance door plus staff door; 1-3 felt tables (baccarat/poker/BJ) with 6-9 seats around a dealer crescent and 1.5 m walkway behind the chairs; private bar with 2-6 stools and drinks cabinet/humidor; one or two lounge groups (sofas facing a low table on a rug); chip kiosk/cashier window by the door; 2 bodyguards flanking the door; cameras; plants; larger salons add stage/piano, wine wall, second bar. Generated 10 rooms (12x10 to 48x28 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Rooms scale badly: a 14x10 m or 24x14 m salon contains the same one table, one lounge corner and nothing else, leaving 80 % of the floor empty. A real salon fills up with size: more tables, a second and third lounge group, a bar, art/wine wall, stage, so the item count must grow with area (and with wealth).
  - [high] No private bar: a high roller room is defined by a host's bar with stools and a back shelf/drinks cabinet; here only sofas and side tables stand around. Add a bar with 2-4 stools against a wall, always.
  - [high] The card table is seated wrongly: only 4 chairs in one row on one side, and a single dealer chair opposite, in the middle of the floor. A real baccarat/poker table has 6-9 seats around a curve (or both long sides) with the dealer inside, and is a bit bigger; seats should not form a straight line like a bench.
  - [high] Sofas stand back against the wall with a lone armchair-like square (coffee table) pushed beside them and not facing anything; the rug is two disconnected purple rectangles in the middle of the floor, one above and one below the table, so they read as carpets lying around, not under a seating group. Seating should face a low table on a rug that sits under the group, and lounge groups should face the table or the room, not the wall corner.
  - [medium] Everything floats in the room centre or clings to the corners: the table has no relationship to the door or the lounge (not centred on the axis, no clear route door to table to bar). Arrange table, lounge and bar in clear zones with a 1.5 m aisle between them and free approach from the door.
  - [medium] Door area: wall screen and a plant sit at the entrance, no hosts' desk, chip/cashier kiosk, or two guards flanking the door, no coat/weapon-check. Exactly the place where a salon would have its staff.
  - [medium] Small variants (12x10 cells) squeeze a sofa group, bar-like block and two tables against each other with no 1 m path; the tiny size should drop items (one table, wall bar, two armchairs) rather than cram them.
  - [medium] Only one door; a luxury salon should have a second (staff/service or emergency) door, ideally to the cage or kitchen, not on the same wall as the main door.
  - [low] Missing details: cameras, ashtrays/cigar humidor, curtains/art on walls, a plant at every corner reads fine but is repetitive; add a wall of bottles, mirrors and a trode booth/holo screen for Shadowrun flavour. Dive/low wealth should look visibly cheaper (fewer plants, no rug).
### cage_complex
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m dive to 24x16 m megacorp): a public-facing cage with 1-6 teller windows (counter on the window wall, chair/terminal behind, safe under the counter, manager desk, key cabinet); a windowless count room with 2-4 long count tables, one chair row per table, counting machines, carts, cameras; a vault deepest in the chain with a heavy door, safe wall, cash racks and a cart; mantrap between cage and count room, guard post, cameras everywhere; the vault is only reachable through cage/count room; Shadowrun flavour: matrix node terminal, drone dock, armed guard station. Generated 10 complexes (16x12 to 40x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared; layout is always the same: cage as the big southern strip with the entrance, count room NW/N, vault NE/E, three rooms in a 2x1 arrangement.
  - [high] The cage is a huge empty hall (e.g. 36x24 cells: ~70 % bare floor, only 3-4 counters on the walls, 1-2 stools, a few carts and safes). A real cash cage is a compact room behind a long teller window; here the counters are scattered along random walls (some on the back wall, one on the side wall) and face nothing. Counters must form one continuous window line on the wall facing the public side/door, with staff chairs behind and a clear customer side; the rest of the room must be the staff area (desks, safes, key cabinet), not a ballroom. Scale the number of windows with size (a 20 m wide cage with 3 counters of 3.5 m is not enough) or make the cage smaller and let the entrance zone be a distinct lobby.
  - [high] Count room: the count tables are crossing the room with chairs 1-2 cells away from them, floating in the open (c5/c9: two chairs side by side detached from the tables), tables in the middle and also against the wall; real count tables stand in a row, parallel, with a 1 m aisle between, chairs tucked directly at the long edge of each table, all on the same side. Also tables stand against each other in a 3x3 block at the east wall (g3, g9, c5 style: a column of tables against the wall with no chairs at all). Enforce a chair per table seat and equal alignment.
  - [high] Door/opening problems: a hard warning "door overlaps another opening" in several seeds (1, 4, 6, 7, 10) at the count-room/cage wall; the count-room door sits in the corner next to the wall end and the door arc runs into the corner counters. The doors of the complex are plain single doors; a cash cage needs a mantrap/airlock (two doors in a short vestibule) between cage and count room and a heavy vault door; the vault should not have a door in the middle of the shared wall with cabinets right in front of it.
  - [high] Chain of rooms is wrong: the vault is entered from the count room and the count room from the cage, but the vault, count room and cage all lie in a plain row with the long corridor-like cage beside them; the vault must be the deepest room (only access via cage or count room, no window, no second door) and the count room must not be directly accessible from the public entrance hall. Here the staff area is open to the whole cage hall.
  - [medium] Vault contents: only wall-aligned deposit boxes (safety box rows), 1-2 crates and a safe in the corner; looks like a bank safe-deposit room, not a casino vault. Add cash racks/pallet shelves, a cart in the middle with a 1.2 m aisle, a counting/weighing table, and floor drains/alarm panel; keep the vault small (not 120-160 cells) with a lot of contents instead of an empty room (luxury vault 8x20 cells is mostly bare). Crates in the middle of the vault (3x3 blocks) read oddly; they should stand in groups next to a shelf.
  - [medium] Cash carts line up in threes along the wall at the entrance of the count room and cage (mmm mmm), in the middle of the entry walkway; carts should be parked in one bay next to the vault/count door, not scattered; a cart parked in front of the main entrance blocks the route.
  - [medium] Missing details: no security: guard post/desk at the cage entrance, cameras in each room (especially count room and vault), key/lock cabinet, cash-in-transit drop (armoured car side door) and an alarm panel; no desk for the cage manager, no terminals at the counters (only one 'terminal' in two seeds). Count rooms need counting machines on the tables and bins/shredder.
  - [low] Stools at the counters are placed in the room middle, 5+ cells away from the counter (c3/c8), or two stools together under a counter 7 cells long; 1 chair per window, directly behind it.
  - [low] Wall screens/suppression tanks and mismatched 'wall' pieces appear in the cage on high/luxury and not in the other rooms; fine but should sit beside the vault/count door, not at the top of the public wall.
### cash_cage
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) one coherent teller line, not scattered counters: a single run of windows with a teller stool behind each, facing the public side; (2) back-office kit that makes it read as a cage: till/PC per teller, chip racks, counting table, supervisor desk, vault/safe wall; (3) a compact, secured room instead of a hall with a huge empty middle; (4) a believable entrance and cameras.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m dive to 20x10 m luxury, incl. corner L-cage, VIP window, ticket-redemption, Shadowrun corp with deck jack and guard post): a long window counter along ONE wall (public side), 1 teller per ~1.5 m with stool, till drawer, PC and chip tray directly behind the window; 1 m working aisle behind the tellers; chip racks and shelving on the back wall; 1-3 safes or a vault alcove on the far wall; counting table and cash cart in the middle-back; supervisor desk at the side with sight over all tellers; one secured door (mantrap) in a side wall; cameras in corners; no windows. Generated 10+ rooms (10x8 to 30x16 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-11) and compared.
  - [high] Teller counters are scattered over several walls (g3/g4/g7/g8: strips on north wall, east wall and south wall at once, 1 on the west wall) and are not one window line. Real cages have all windows in one continuous row on one wall with the stools behind them; arrange them in a row with at most a narrow gap (a pillar/partition) between windows, same orientation, none on the entrance wall or the wall beside the safe.
  - [high] Room reads as an empty hall: 24x14 or 30x16 cells with 5-8 objects around the edges and a completely empty middle (c4, c8). A cage is a tight working room (about 20-50 m2) with equipment; large sizes need more equipment (more windows, counting table, vault, chip racks) rather than empty floor. Size/count must scale: more tellers and safes for big rooms.
  - [high] Missing everything that makes a cage: no teller desks/PCs/till drawers, no chip racks or cabinets, no counting table, no supervisor desk, no shredder, no shelving. Only counter, stool, safe, cash cart (and a terminal/camera in some). Add these, grouped by function: chip racks and shelves along the back wall, counting table in the middle with chairs on both sides, supervisor desk at the end of the teller line.
  - [high] Stools are not tied to the counters: several counters have no stool (g1: 2 counters, 1 stool; g8 two counters without), stools stand 3-4 cells from the counter (c3: stools hanging in the room) or in front of a short strip. Each window needs its own stool directly behind it, centred, about 1 cell from the counter, facing the wall of windows.
  - [high] Safes sit alone in corners (g1/g4/g7/g10: safe in a corner, sometimes two on opposite corners). A vault area should be a group: 1-3 safes side by side on one wall (or in an alcove) with a 1 m aisle in front and no other furniture blocking the door swing; never lone safes in far-apart corners.
  - [medium] Entrance: the door is the full width of an 11-cell (5.5 m) opening in the wall, like a hall gate; a cage has a single narrow secured door (1 m, ideally mantrap) and the interior beside it should not open directly onto the counters. The entry wall should be free; counters directly beside the door (g1 bottom-left, g10) block it.
  - [medium] Cash carts lined up 3 in a row along a wall (g1 bottom, g5, g7 east wall) look like parked trolleys; 1 cart near the vault/count table is enough, cart stored by a wall in a free niche.
  - [medium] Cameras appear in corners only in some seeds and sometimes the triangle marker is the only sign; a cage must have cameras covering every teller and the safe, always, plus the terminal/deck jack placed by the supervisor (c3/c11 terminal on the far south-west corner by the door is not logical).
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour missing: no armed-guard post inside, no keycard reader beside the door, no drone; wealth changes nothing visible (low and luxury differ only in room size). High/luxury: more windows, VIP window with sofa outside, vault alcove with several safes.
### count_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m dive to 14x10 m luxury): one narrow secured door (mantrap/keycard, guard chair); 1-6 long count tables in parallel rows in the room middle, a chair per counter on both long sides, a bill counter every ~1 m; drop-box rack, bill-strapping/weighing station, coin sorter alcove, 1-2 carts parked in a niche, safe/vault door, shelves with bags, shred bin; supervisor desk with terminal/monitor wall in big rooms; 2-6 ceiling cameras over the tables; Shadowrun flavour: deck jack, drone dock, armed sentry. Generated 10 rooms (10x8 to 24x16 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The count table is a huge slab (3 cells deep x 9-11 cells long, 4.5-5.5 m, up to 9 cells tall) pushed against a wall, and counters sit on only ONE side, only 2 chairs on a 5 m table (g2, g4, g7, g9). A count table must be free-standing in the room with chairs on both long sides, about one chair per 1 m, and 1.2 m walkway all around; against the wall it reads as a counter/bar, not a count room.
  - [high] Rooms are nearly empty: a 24x16 cell room has two tables and 4 chairs and a vast void in the middle (c7), 20x14 only tables and carts. Contents must scale with area: several tables in parallel rows, more chairs, shelves, drop-box racks, safes, supervisor desk, cameras. Small rooms should drop to one table rather than cram.
  - [high] Missing the core equipment of a count room: no bill counters/coin sorters on the tables, no drop-box (slot box) racks, no strapping/weighing station, no cash trolleys properly parked, no shred bin, no supervisor desk with terminal/monitor. Tables look like empty counters.
  - [high] Cash carts are placed at random: 3-in-a-row along walls (g1, g6, g10 east and south walls), single carts in corners, carts in the aisle in front of the door (c4, c9). One or two carts parked together in a niche next to the table/vault; never several across the room.
  - [medium] Door: a wide 5.5 m hall-style gate opening (11 cells). A count room has one narrow secured door (1-1.2 m, mantrap), with a guard chair/desk beside it and a keycard reader; the wall around the door should be free and the interior should not open directly onto the tables.
  - [medium] Safe appears in only some seeds and tiny in a corner; a count room is built around the vault/safe door: a safe or vault door on the wall opposite the entry, with approach aisle, must be present at every wealth level above dive.
  - [medium] Cameras: only one triangle in a corner or none; add 2-4 ceiling cameras above the count tables, always. Wall screens (monitors) hang in a row on the north wall with no desk or terminal below (g4, g6); they must belong to a supervisor/monitoring desk.
  - [low] Wealth has no visible effect (low and luxury differ only in size): high should add more tables, keycard/biometric pad, drone dock, armed sentry post; dive could be one table, a safe and a trash bin.
### surveillance_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x4 m dive to 12x8 m luxury): one video wall of screens on a single wall (6 to 20 monitors), operator consoles (2-4 monitors each) in 1-4 rows all facing that wall, 1 m aisle behind and between rows, supervisor desk at the back facing the wall, 1-3 server/DVR racks in a corner or alcove, door at the back so staff do not cross the view; extras with size: coffee/fridge corner, lockers, whiteboard, sofa, drone dock, rigger/jack-in chair (Shadowrun). Generated 10 rooms (8x8 to 30x20 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] No composition: consoles and screens are scattered along random walls (west, east, north, south) in every result, with no common facing direction. A control room must have ONE screen wall, and all operator desks must be in rows in front of it, facing it, sharing one aisle behind them (s5, s6, s10: desks on three different walls looking in different directions).
  - [high] Wall screens (blue panels) are separate 1-2 m strips spread over all walls, some beside the door, in corners or at the south wall next to the entrance (s3, s6, s8, s9). Group them into one contiguous video wall (a grid of monitors) at the wall opposite the door; no screens on the door wall.
  - [high] Consoles are single desks with one chair, stuck to the wall with their back to the screens or facing a blank wall; no desk has its own monitors in front of the operator. Each operator station needs a visible monitor group directly in front, a chair behind it and a keyboard/joystick; desks in the middle of the room (rows) instead of only on the perimeter.
  - [high] Large rooms are nearly empty (s6 28x20 and s10 30x20: 3-4 desks and a few screens, 80% empty carpet in the centre). Desk and screen count must scale with room size (about 1 operator per 6-8 m2 up to 4 rows) or the room is simply smaller; luxury/corp should have many more stations plus a supervisor desk.
  - [medium] No supervisor/shift lead desk at the back with a view over all consoles, and no rack/server group: only tiny racks/stripes in corners (grid-like objects, 1-2 per room) that read as radiators. Add one or more server/DVR racks together as a group in a corner, plus a UPS/AC.
  - [medium] Missing comfort and service details for a 24/7 room: coffee machine/fridge, locker, bin, whiteboard/watch list, a small sofa in big rooms, and a drone dock/rigger chair for Shadowrun style. Only a bin-like pair of circles in one corner appears. These should be grouped in one rest corner away from the screens.
  - [medium] Wealth does not change the look: low and luxury (s1 vs s10) differ only by count; style should vary (dive: one desk, few screens; corp: stepped rows, glass supervisor booth, rack room).
  - [low] Single door in the middle of the south wall with the whole room open; the aisle from the door to the stations is not defined (desks near the entrance). Keep an aisle from the door to the back and to the supervisor, and place desks not in front of the door.
### sky_lounge
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m bar-lounge to 20x12 m luxury): a bar (wall bar or island, 4-8 stools, back shelves) near the service side; seating zones facing the glass (window chairs in pairs, bistro tables along the windows, sofa groups of sofa + armchairs + low table on a rug); larger rooms add a stage/DJ corner with dance floor, VIP booths along the glass, planters as dividers, host stand and coat check at the door, wall screens, drone waiter dock; 1.5 m route door -> bar. Generated 10 rooms (10x8 to 40x24 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The bar is a slab, not a bar: a 3-cell-deep, 12-15 cell long counter (about 6-7 m) with only 4 stools, in the middle or at a wall with the fridge/shelf strip behind it (s2, s3, s10). On small rooms it is the only thing in the room. It must be shorter, stools must run along the whole customer side (1 stool per ~1.2 cells), and it should sit against a wall or end at a wall, never float as a block in the room centre (s3, s8).
  - [high] Seating does not use the room: big rooms are rows of identical 4-chair tables in a neat grid (s9: 10 tables, s4: 7) with a huge empty hall around, no relation to windows, bar or door. A lounge needs variety and zones: tables along the glass first, lounge groups, a dance floor/stage in large rooms, and counts that scale with wealth (luxury looks the same as middle).
  - [high] Small/low rooms are empty: a 12x10 cell room has only a bar, one sofa and a row of stools floating in the middle (s2, s8: stools in a row with nothing at the bar); no table at all. Even a tiny lounge needs 2-3 small tables with chairs or a window counter plus a bar with stools directly at the counter.
  - [high] Stools are not at the bar: in several results the stool row stands 2-3 cells away from the counter or beside it (s10: four stools in a free line under a counter, s8 stools in a column off the counter). Stools must touch the bar front, evenly spaced, facing it.
  - [medium] Sofas stand against the wall with the coffee table in front but there are no armchairs, no rug, and the groups are isolated and pushed into corners or onto one wall, often facing nothing (a sofa facing east wall, back to the room, s9, s3). Seating should face the view (glass) or the room centre, form a group (sofa + 2 armchairs + low table on a rug) and not sit in corners next to plant/side tables only.
  - [medium] The door has a free clear space but nothing welcomes: no host stand, coat rack/check (only in one result), no bouncer; wall screens appear in odd corners; no stage, music corner, drinks stations, pillars. Add door staff and a service door near the bar.
  - [medium] Tables have no relation to each other: chairs are 2 per side with no spacing to the next row (fine) but the first table row sits against the sofas (s4, s9: tables within 1 cell of the sofa coffee tables), so walkways between table rows and lounge groups are too tight while the middle of the room is empty. Keep a 1.5 m aisle everywhere and spread zones evenly.
  - [low] Plants and side tables are placed only in corners; add planters as dividers between zones and along glass, and decorative items (candles, ash trays, glassware) on tables are absent. Shelf/fridge strip behind the bar dominates a whole wall.

## clinic.yaml
### waiting_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a waiting room needs a reception/triage counter with a clerk and a visible door to the clinic interior; (2) seating must be real seating groups (rows facing the counter or screen, chair clusters with side tables), not a perimeter ring of benches around an empty hall; (3) fill the centre or shrink the room feel; (4) amenities and a second door; (5) wealth/style differentiation.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m squatter/ripperdoc to 14x9 m ER and luxury private clinic): reception counter with glass hatch next to the entrance, seats in 2-3 back-to-back rows (4-6 chairs, 1.2 m aisle) facing a wall screen/ticket window, or L along two walls in small rooms; magazine/side tables between chairs, kids corner with mat and toys, water cooler, vending, bins by the door, plant corners, wheelchair bay 1.5 m, queue-number screen, kiosk; luxury uses sofa groups, armchairs, coffee tables, rug, concierge, art; ER adds a guard and gurney bay; ripperdoc is a bench, barred window and mismatched chairs. Generated 10 rooms (10x8 to 28x18 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] No reception/triage desk and no staff in any result (all 10); the room has no focal point and no place to check in. Every real waiting room is organised around a counter or hatch near the entrance, and chairs face it.
  - [high] Seating is only single-bench strips pressed against the walls, all facing into an empty middle. A 20x12 or 28x18 cell room (up to 250 m2) is a vast empty floor with 8-14 short benches on the border (w5, w6, w9, w10). Real rooms put chair rows or island rows in the middle (back-to-back or facing the counter/screen), with 1.2 m aisles, and benches only as a wall supplement. Seat count must scale with floor area (about 1 seat per 1.5-2 m2 in a clinic) and is far too low now (w6: 8 benches in 96 m2).
  - [high] Benches are placed arbitrarily: several face a side wall, some in corners, some isolated in the middle of a long wall with a gap of 5+ cells to the next. They should be grouped contiguously into runs of 3-6 seats along one wall, oriented towards the focal point (counter/screen/door), not scattered one per wall segment (w4, w6).
  - [medium] No tables: no magazine/side tables between seats, no kids corner (mat, toys, low chairs), no coat stand, no brochure rack. Add them in all wealth levels above squatter; they also break up the empty floor.
  - [medium] Amenities are random and sparse: vending machine, water cooler and bins appear in some seeds only and are stuck in corners or alone on a wall (w4: water cooler between benches, wall screens stacked 4 in a row on one wall). One wall screen or a queue-number display facing the seats is right; four in a row is not. Hand sanitiser, info board, kiosk, and a bin by the door are missing.
  - [medium] Exits: only the entrance door, no second door to the clinic interior (patients are called into exam rooms through a separate door, usually beside the counter). Add a staff/inner door on a different wall; keep the area between both doors free as a walkway and keep a 1.5 m wheelchair space beside the door.
  - [medium] Wealth/style is almost invisible: squatter, low, middle and high look the same (plants appear, a bench count changes). Squatter should have mismatched chairs, trash and a barred hatch; middle/high chairs plus tables; luxury sofas, armchairs, coffee table, rug, plants, a concierge desk and an aquarium or art; corp medical a scanner at the door.
  - [low] Plants only in some seeds, in a corner; fine, but better 1-2 per room by wealth and not in the entry corner. Shadowrun flavour (ad screens, SINless/trauma-team posters, a drone or guard at the door for rough areas) is missing.
### exam_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a doctor's desk with terminal and chair(s) (patient chair beside it) is missing in every room; (2) exam couch placement: head against a wall, reachable from both long sides, the doctor's stool beside it, not an isolated slab floating mid-room or squeezed in a corner; (3) clutter and size scaling: the rooms are mostly empty floor, so add the usual small items per wealth; (4) sink/cabinet grouped into one counter run.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 2.5x2.5 m ripperdoc to 5x5 m luxury): couch with head to the wall and 0.8-1 m free on both long sides; stool at the foot or side; one wall counter run (sink + base cabinets + glove/sanitizer dispenser + sharps bin + waste bin) near the door; desk with terminal and a doctor chair plus a patient chair near the door, so the doctor sees both patient and door; privacy curtain; exam lamp; wealth gates: squatter = salvaged couch, bin, crate; middle = standard set; high/luxury = armchair, wall screen, diagnostic arm, plant; Shadowrun: scanner/autodoc screen, cyberware cabinet, trode rig. Generated 12 rooms (5x5 to 14x8 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] No desk, computer/terminal or chairs in any of 12 rooms: the room reads as a store room with a bed, not a place for examination and consultation. Needs a small desk with terminal against a wall near the door, a doctor chair and a patient chair; larger rooms (>= 50 m2... i.e. 10x8 cells and up) can hold two patient chairs.
  - [high] Exam table placement is arbitrary: in e5, e7, e9, e11 and e12 it stands in a far corner or against one wall with its long side parallel to the wall and 0 cells free beside it, in e6 it sits in the middle of the room with its short end to the south, and in large rooms (12x8, 10x10, 14x8) it is lost in a big empty hall. A couch must have its head to a wall, at least 1 m (2 cells) free on both long sides, and be roughly central on that wall, with the free foot end toward the room, so the doctor can walk around it.
  - [high] Rooms are far too empty and uniform across wealth: only couch, a stool, 1-3 cabinets, a sink and sometimes a bin. A 10x10 high-wealth room has the same objects as a 5x5 low room. Big rooms (>= 80 cells) should not exist as plain exam rooms or must be filled with a second couch, a screen/curtain, a wall screen, plant, diagnostic equipment; wealth must visibly change the content.
  - [medium] Cabinets and sink are scattered individually: a lone 1x3 sink strip in a far corner (e2, e3, e8, e10, e12), medical cabinets as unrelated strips on random walls (e6, e10 run down a whole 9-cell wall of 8 cabinets in a row, looks like a locker room). They should form one counter run: sink flanked by base cabinets, 2-3 m long at most, on one wall, with soap/towel, glove dispenser and waste/sharps bin directly beside it.
  - [medium] Waste/sharps bins appear only sometimes (e5, e8, e9, e10), and then far from the sink; every exam room needs a waste bin and sharps container at the sink and a hand sanitizer by the door.
  - [medium] Stool is often missing (e2, e4/e10 sometimes, e5) or stands loose in open floor away from the couch (e3, e7, e9, e12); the doctor's stool must sit at the couch side or foot.
  - [medium] Items are too far from the door / jammed in the door line: the doors are 3-7 cells wide (large door wall) so the room reads as a corridor opening; an exam room should have a normal 1 m door and wall space beside it for the desk and sanitizer.
  - [low] Missing details: privacy curtain around the couch, exam lamp, wall screen or anatomy chart, height scale; Shadowrun flavour (autodoc/scanner arm, cyberware cabinet, trode rig) for middle and above.
  - [low] Size: rooms like 14x8 cells (7x4 m) are too big for single exam rooms; real ones are 3x3.5 to 4x5 m (6x7 to 8x10 cells).
### treatment_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m minor-injury room to 5x5 m twin-bed room, street-doc to corp luxury): door in a short wall; one exam couch with head to the wall and 1 m access on both long sides; doctor desk with terminal and a stool/chair at the door side plus a patient chair; wash basin by the door with soap/towel; instrument trolley and exam lamp beside the couch; wall cabinets, sharps and waste bins; vitals monitor at the bed head; big rooms add a second couch behind a curtain rail, medicine fridge, scale; Shadowrun flavour: street-doc cot with a parts crate, cyberware diagnostic rig, autodoc and holo screen for corp. Generated 10 rooms (8x7 to 16x12, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Not a treatment room but a ward: it contains only beds, iv stands, vital monitors and cabinets. No doctor desk/terminal, no stool or patient chair, no instrument trolley, no exam lamp, no waste/sharps bin. Add the desk-and-chair work spot and the treatment kit; a room without a desk or seat for the clinician reads as a hospital bay.
  - [high] Number of beds scales with area: 2 to 4 beds in rooms of 8x7 to 11x11, often spread over the room (luxury 11x11 has 3 beds). A treatment room is one couch (two at most in a big room, with a curtain); several beds in different corners looks like a ward. Keep it to one couch, add other furniture with size instead.
  - [high] Bed placement is arbitrary: some beds sit with the side against a wall, some in mid-floor, some head free, facing in mixed directions (seeds 5, 6, 7, 10 beds on east/west wall sideways, seed 3/4 two beds in a row at the north wall). The couch must stand with the head against a wall, free on both long sides and at the foot, and be oriented consistently, away from the door so the doctor sees patient and door.
  - [high] Sink is missing in most seeds (1, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8) and when present stands in a far corner or a 3-cell strip far from the door (seeds 2, 5, 9, 10). A treatment room always has a wash basin near the door (hygiene on entering); it must be mandatory and wall-mounted next to the door.
  - [medium] Rooms look empty: 8x7 to 16x12 cells with 4-6 small objects, vast bare floor in the middle and near the door (seeds 2, 7, 9). Furniture must grow with room size: second cabinet wall, desk group, fridge, scale, chairs, and wealth-based details (monitor screens, autodoc/diagnostic rig, plants and art for luxury, crate and bucket for squatter).
  - [medium] Cabinets are 3-cell strips crammed in corners (seeds 1, 3, 4) or alone against random walls; they should line a wall in a short run as supply storage, near the couch/desk, not in the entry corner where the door swing is.
  - [medium] Iv stand and vital monitor sit at the bed corners but are tiny and the monitor is a stray square on the wall away from the head (seeds 1, 7, 8: monitor in the room corner meters from the bed). They should be attached to the bed head (monitor at head, iv stand beside).
  - [low] Wealth hardly changes the room: squatter and luxury differ only in count of beds. Low should read as street clinic (cot, folding table, crate), luxury as corp clinic (adjustable bed, diagnostic scanner, holo screen).
### trauma_bay
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m street-doc bay to 10x10 m hospital bay and a corp/ripperdoc variant): wide double door to the ambulance corridor; trauma stretchers stand free in the room (head to the wall with headwall unit: monitor, gas/suction, light), at least 1.2-1.5 m free on three sides, foot end toward the door; 1 bed (small), 2-3 in a row with curtains (mid), 4 bays (large); crash cart, defibrillator and instrument trolley beside the bed head/door side; sink, PPE and sharps near the door; supply cabinets in a block on one wall; IV poles and a vent/monitor at every bed; luxury adds a ceiling boom, wall screens, blood fridge, portable x-ray and cyberware diagnostic rig; slum/ripperdoc a single worn bed with mismatched cabinets. Generated 10 rooms (12x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Bed placement is random and does not read as a trauma bay: beds are scattered at different orientations (some head north, some head west, some head south), partly in corners and at the walls, with no row or common direction (t3, t4, t7, t9). Beds must stand in a tidy row (or two parallel rows), all with the head to the same wall and the foot toward the door, with equal spacing and a curtain/aisle between them.
  - [high] The room is mostly empty and does not scale: 30x20 cells (15x10 m) holds the same 3-4 beds plus a few carts, leaving most of the floor bare (t7, t4). Count must grow with area (more bays, a central supply/nurse station, a second wall of cabinets), or the room should be rejected as too large for a bay.
  - [high] Missing the defining resuscitation set-up: no crash cart/defibrillator next to the door or bed head, no ventilator, no surgical/exam light, no instrument trolley at the bed. The "medical carts" float alone in the middle of the room (t2, t4, t7), not related to any bed. Each bed needs its own monitor, IV pole, light and a trolley at the side; the crash cart belongs near the door.
  - [high] Beds are ordinary hospital beds ("hospital_bed" in the ward style, rows of pillow and blanket), not trauma stretchers; a trauma bay centres a narrow gurney/trauma table free on three sides with a headwall unit. Gurneys (g) are parked in a corner or along a wall like storage (t3, t4, t9) with no relation to the door; they should be at the door/ambulance entry.
  - [medium] Monitors, IV poles and vitals are placed per bed inconsistently: some beds have the monitor and IV pole at the head/foot, some none (t7: two beds with a pole between them, the third has nothing; t1: monitors in room corners far from the beds). Always bind monitor + pole to each bed at the head side.
  - [medium] Sink is a corner element (t2, t4, t7, t9) far away from door and beds, and is the only hygiene item: no hand disinfectant dispenser, glove/PPE box, sharps bin or biohazard bin near the door. A bay needs a sink with dispenser right by the entrance and a sharps bin at each bed.
  - [medium] Cabinets are 3-cell blocks pushed arbitrarily to any wall or placed in the middle of the south wall near the door (t7, t4), with no grouping; supplies should be grouped as one coherent storage run on one wall (not next to the door swing), plus a medication fridge/blood fridge for middle+ wealth.
  - [medium] Wealth barely shows: low, middle, high and luxury have the same inventory with a few more objects (t4 luxury = more of the same beds). Squatter/low should be one worn bed and mismatched cabinets; high/luxury should add ceiling booms, wall screens, portable x-ray/ultrasound and an autodoc/cyberware rig (Shadowrun flavour), plus a curtain track between bays.
  - [low] Door is a double door on one wall but there is no second (staff/ambulance) door and no clear straight route door-to-bed (a gurney would be blocked by carts in t2 and t7); keep a 2 m wide lane from the door to the bed row clear.
### operating_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x5 m street-doc backroom to 10x8 m hybrid OR / teaching theatre): one central table (about 0.6x2 m) with a twin-arm surgical light above, anaesthesia machine and cart at the head end, Mayo/instrument tables and a back table at the sides and foot, 2+ wall monitors, wall cabinets, scrub sink next to the door, bins and kick buckets, 1.5 m free ring around the table; larger/richer rooms add C-arm or robot console, crash cart/defibrillator, observation window, second (clean) door; Shadowrun flavour: cyberware tray, tech bench with diagnostic rig, drone arm, organ cooler; a low ripperdoc room is scavenged and messy. Generated 9 rooms (10x12 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10; the 10x8 one was refused as too small) and compared.
  - [high] Room contents do not scale and the room looks empty: a 24x16 or 30x20 cell room has the same single table, one monitor and one cart as a 12x12 room, with 3 or 4 cabinets stuck on random walls. A real OR is dense around the table: surgical light(s), anaesthesia machine at the head, Mayo/instrument tables, back table, several monitors, sink, bins. Add these always and scale equipment with size and wealth; cap the room size or add zones (scrub/prep, sterile storage) instead of leaving 60 % bare floor.
  - [high] The surgical light is missing, as is any anaesthesia machine/cart at the head end, instrument (Mayo) and back tables at the foot/side, and a scrub sink at the door. The table has only a vital monitor and a lone cart beside it; without the light and the instrument tables the room reads as a hospital bed in a hall, not a theatre.
  - [high] The table looks like a bed (pillow-like head, dark body) and is wide (3 cells = 1.5 m); an operating table is a narrow (about 0.6 m) straight table with a split head/leg section and arm boards. Make it narrower and sheet/steel-like, clearly distinct from a ward bed.
  - [high] Cabinets (medical_cabinet) are scattered at random on any wall, some lying along the door wall right at the door, some in corners, in 1-4 cell stubs; real OR storage is a continuous run of wall cabinets (one or two walls, not at the door) plus pass-through/sterile storage. Group them in one or two runs, keep them clear of the door swing, and orient them to face the room.
  - [high] Machinery (the orange boxes with two round dials, luxury and some middle rooms) is clipped half-way through the west wall (or4) and floats in corners with no relation to the table; equipment should stand against a wall within reach of the table (head end), fully inside the room and with a service gap. Make sure no object overlaps a wall.
  - [medium] Table-side layout is always the same: cart(s) in a column on the east side touching the table (the cart and table overlap/abut) and monitor at the head; carts must have 0.6-1 m clearance so staff can work between table and cart, and the monitor should stand at the head or on a boom, not on the floor beside the patient. Staff aisle >= 1.5 m on both long sides and at the foot must stay free.
  - [medium] Table position is the exact centre of the room with the head toward the north in every instance, with the door directly under the foot end: the first thing staff sees entering is the sterile field. Better: table slightly offset, head to the wall that carries the gas outlets/anaesthesia, and the foot turned to the double door so the bed can roll in; the approach from the door to the table must be a clear corridor.
  - [medium] The entrance is a 7-cell (3.5 m) double door wide open in every room; fine as a bed door but there is no second (clean/dirty or scrub-room) door and no sink next to it. One door to the scrub room and one to the corridor is realistic; the door should not be wider than the wall run left beside it.
  - [medium] Wealth difference is minimal: squatter/low and luxury rooms differ only by cabinet and machinery count. Low should look improvised (one lamp, tray table, bucket, rusty cooler, bare), high/luxury should add a second light, wall monitor bank, robot/imaging unit, observation glass, tech bench with cyberware tray and diagnostic rig, organ cooler (Shadowrun flavour).
  - [low] Missing small details: waste bin and linen bin, kick bucket, wall clock, X-ray view box, IV stand only in some seeds (and it stands at the table without a pole base), gas outlets/pendants, floor drain mark.
  - [low] Very wide rooms (18x10, 30x20 cells) have no reason to be that size; either subdivide into prep and OR or use the surplus for a second table in a hybrid/trauma variant.
### scrub_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x2 m street clinic to 6x4 m luxury): one continuous trough sink (2-6 taps, 60 cm per tap) along one wall, ideally the wall shared with the OR (viewing window), with soap/brush dispensers and a paper towel dispenser above or beside it, a waste bin at its end, 1.5 m clear aisle in front of the sink; a glove/gown/mask cabinet or shelf, scrub-suit and shoe-cover dispensers by the entry plus a bench; two doors (corridor side and OR side, hands-free); larger rooms add UV/sterile cabinet, clock, eyewash, wall screen with OR status. Generated 10 rooms (10x8 to 20x12 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Sinks must form one trough: individual basins stand 1 cell apart in a broken row (or even separate groups with gaps, in 3 + 3 pairs), reading as scattered washbasins. They need to be butted together as one continuous wash line along one wall with the taps all on one side, and sinks must never be split into two groups or placed in a corner next to a locker.
  - [high] Sink line must be tied to the room function, not the entry wall: in several results the sinks sit directly beside the entrance door on the door wall (s1, s4, s7), so people washing block the entry and the OR door; in others they run along a short side wall. The wash line belongs on a long wall away from the door (preferably the OR wall), with the approach from the door free.
  - [high] The room is nearly empty: a 20x12 cell (10x6 m) scrub room contains 6 sinks, 2 lockers and 2 bins on the edge and a huge dead floor; counts hardly grow with size and rooms of 12x10 cells have only 2-3 sinks and one locker. A scrub room is a small, tight room (about 3x2 to 5x3 m); oversized rooms should get more equipment (gown/glove shelf, supply cabinet, bench, dispensers), not just more empty floor.
  - [high] Missing the second door: only a single wide door on the corridor side exists. A scrub room is a pass-through (corridor in, operating room out); the OR door should be on the opposite wall (or at least another wall), with a straight walk from the sink line to it, and the door should be a normal single/double door, not 3.5 m wide.
  - [medium] Lockers and bins are placed in random corners and mid-wall (including one locker alone on the east wall, lockers in a pair at the top, bin in the opposite corner to the sinks), so there is no consistent logic. The bin and the paper towel dispenser must sit right at the end of the sink line, the cabinets/lockers together on one wall (a row) as clean-side storage.
  - [medium] Missing details: no paper towel/soap dispenser, no scrub-suit/mask/cap dispenser, no bench or shoe-cover station at the entry, no clock, no sterile supply shelf, no eyewash; wealth does not change anything visible (squatter and luxury look the same besides count). Higher wealth should add a UV/sterilising cabinet, wall screen with OR status, hands-free sensor taps.
  - [low] Lockers (personal-style) are the wrong storage type for sterile gear; a closed cabinet or open shelf with gowns/gloves is what a real scrub room has, and a cyberware/trauma-team gear shelf fits the Shadowrun flavour.
### sterilization
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the room must read as a work-flow line (dirty to clean to sterile) with a central work table, not as 4-6 unrelated objects scattered along the walls of an empty hall; (2) it needs far more equipment, scaled with room size; (3) every object needs a role relation (sink beside washer, autoclave beside packing table, storage at the clean end); (4) basic details (hand basin at the door, bins, cart, terminal, eyewash).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x4 m dentist closet to 12x8 m hospital CSSD, shabby to luxury): dirty zone near the entry (receiving counter, 2-bay sink, ultrasonic cleaner, washer-disinfector in the dividing wall), clean zone behind (packing/inspection table with 1 m aisles, 2-4 autoclaves on a wall with service clearance, drying cabinet, pouch sealer, sterile storage racks/cabinets, sterile hatch), hand basin and biohazard bin at the door, eyewash, trolleys, tracking terminal; luxury adds UV cabinets, robot cart, glass partition; ganger back room = one pressure-cooker autoclave, steel table, bucket sink. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 24x16, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10, PNG + ASCII) and compared.
  - [high] Rooms are almost empty: 4-6 objects in 100-380 cells (2x 10x10 squatter room: one sink row, one machine block, one counter; 24x16 middle: one sink, one counter, one counter, one machine, 380 cells of bare floor). A sterile processing room is one of the densest work rooms in a clinic: equipment lines the walls nearly continuously and the middle is occupied by a work table. Object count must grow with area (a counter run per wall, several autoclave/washer units, etc.).
  - [high] There is no central work table at all. The packing/inspection table is the defining object of the room; without it the room is a big void with items stuck on the walls. Add one packing table (island, about 1 m aisles all around) in larger rooms, and for big rooms two, in a row.
  - [high] Objects are scattered randomly with no flow: sink in the north-east corner, counter beside the door, machine in the opposite corner, shelf mid-wall (seeds 3, 6, 8, 10). Must be arranged as a sequence along the walls: sink and ultrasonic cleaner next to the entry (dirty), washer and autoclaves together in one block, then packing table, then sterile storage at the far end, all near each other. Sinks and the counter must not be spread to opposite ends.
  - [high] Autoclaves/machinery are placed in corners or alone (seeds 6, 8, 10, 9): equipment must stand in a row along one wall with service clearance, several units together (a bank of 2-4 in mid/high rooms), not one machine in a corner. Always pair a washer-disinfector with a sink or counter at its loading side.
  - [medium] Counters are short stubs (7 cells) put at arbitrary spots, e.g. right inside the door beside the door swing (seeds 2, 3, 6, 7, 8) or the middle of a wall. They must form a continuous work counter run (sink | counter | machine) along a wall with wall shelves/cabinets above it, with the loading counter next to the washer.
  - [medium] Missing sterile storage: rarely any shelves/cabinets (none at 10, 12, 20 cell seeds 1, 2, 3, 7, 8), and when present they stand alone in corners or the room centre-line. Closed cabinets or racks must group on the clean end wall, close to the packing table.
  - [medium] Missing standard details: hand basin at the door, biohazard/waste bins by the sinks, instrument trolley/cart, computer/label terminal at the packing table, eyewash, drying cabinet, pouch sealer. None appear in any of the 10 rooms.
  - [medium] Door/entry: the door sits in the middle of the south wall and the first objects are beside or in front of it (counter stub, sink); the dirty-side items should be at the door while the clean items are far from it. There is only one door; large rooms (> 20 m2) should have a second (clean-side) door or a pass-through hatch.
  - [low] Wealth barely changes the room: squatter and luxury look alike except for object count. Low wealth: one tabletop autoclave and a bucket sink on a steel table; high/luxury: several washers, UV cabinets, glass partition, tracking terminal, drone cart.
### ward
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x4 m squatter back room to 14x8 m barrack ward, plus isolation and luxury single suite): beds in rows with heads to the long walls, 1 m between beds, 1.5-2 m central aisle, a bedside table/locker plus monitor/IV per bed, chair per bed, curtains/screens, a washbasin and hand sanitizer by the door, bin, wardrobe, wide door; luxury adds sofa/TV/ensuite, ripperdoc ward adds med cart, drone dock; squatter variants use cots/mattresses and crates. Generated 10 wards (12x10 to 30x16 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Bed count does not scale with the room: 14x10, 24x16 and even 30x16 cells (15x8 m, 120 m2) hold 1-2 beds, 16x12 holds 4 at most. A ward is defined by a row of beds; a 12x8 m room with one bed looks like an empty hall. Beds must grow with area (about one per 7-8 m2) and fill the room.
  - [high] Beds must be arranged in rows, heads against the long walls (or one wall), spaced about 1 m apart with a common central aisle of 1.5-2 m. Now they are scattered at random: some head to the wall, some turned 90 degrees, one floating mid-room with its head into the room (seed 4, two beds side by side in the south wall but another alone at the north), none paired or aligned.
  - [high] Each bed needs its own bedside kit placed directly at the head: bedside table/locker, monitor and IV stand next to the bed, chair at the side. Currently monitors (green squares) and IV stands drift a few cells from beds or sit in a corner alone (w1: monitor and cabinet at the bed, w8: monitor and IV on opposite walls, beds unaccompanied), and one bed often has no IV/monitor at all. Beds and their equipment must read as one unit.
  - [high] Missing the defining ward details: washbasin/hand sanitizer near the door, bin, privacy curtains between beds, medicine cart/trolley, wardrobe or locker, visitor chair per bed; wall-mounted TV/screen only in a few; nothing for an ensuite or toilet. Chairs appear as one or two stray round stools in the middle of the floor, not at a bed.
  - [medium] Furniture clings to corners and sits in the open (cabinets in corners, round stool mid-room w3/w9), while the door-side wall is empty. Keep the walkway from the door free, put sink and cabinet along the wall near the door, and nothing in the foot space of beds (1.2 m free for transport).
  - [medium] No style variation by wealth: squatter, low and luxury look the same, only beds count differs a little. Squatter: cots/mattresses on the floor, crate for a table; middle: curtains, TV; luxury: single/double rooms with sofa, desk, fridge, flowers, ensuite; ripperdoc flavour: drip stands, med cart, cyberware tray, drone dock, autodoc bay at the end.
  - [medium] Large wards (24x16, 30x16) leave a huge empty middle and a lone wall screen on the north wall; a big room should be divided into bays (4-6 beds each) or get a nurse desk/station at the door, not stay empty.
  - [low] Windows alternate evenly around all walls including the door wall, but beds do not use them (bed heads should stand under or between windows). Fine, but a window opposite the door and bed heads on the side walls would look more natural.
### nurse_station
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the room reads as a station, not as scattered pieces: a counter that faces the approach with staff chairs behind it, workstation items on it, medical wall behind; (2) fill the room proportionally; (3) clinical details; (4) wealth differences.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x4 m squatter closet to 12x8 m trauma/ICU hub): an L- or U-shaped (high wealth: curved) counter as a hub or at the opening facing the corridor/door, 1-4 staff chairs behind it with a PC/monitor and phone each, a 1 m working aisle behind, 1.5 m clear in front, locked medicine cabinet/fridge, shelves and printer on the back wall, sink or hand sanitizer at the entrance, sharps bin, whiteboard/patient chart, a parked crash cart, wheelchair and gurney, 2 waiting chairs for ICU/clinic reception; squatter version is a folding table, one chair, crates and a cooler; luxury adds glass screens, holo display, med dispenser, plants, a lounge corner.
  - Generated 10 rooms (10x8 to 20x14 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The counters are plain slabs pushed against a wall (north or west), so the staff chair ends up on the room side and the counter is no hub or desk facing the door; nothing stands on or behind it. A nurse station counter must stand where the visitor or corridor reaches it (opposite or beside the door, or as an island/L-shape), the staff chair on the inside, with a free walkway on the visitor side.
  - [high] Rooms are mostly empty: a 14x10 or 18x10 room holds one or two counters, a bed and a cart, and 70% of the floor is bare (seeds 3, 5, 7, 10). A real station has computers/monitors at every chair, a printer, filing or supply shelving, a med fridge, a sink, wall chart, bins. Either fill the room with these or have the room shrink to the equipment it needs.
  - [high] Chairs are not paired with counters: the counter count and chair count do not match (seeds 4 and 10: a chair stands alone in a corner or beside an unrelated counter, a counter has none). One chair per workstation segment, centered behind it, facing the counter.
  - [medium] The medical cabinets, beds and carts are dropped at random along the walls and the middle (seed 9: a bed or cart-like slab stands alone in the centre and another directly in the door line, 2 cells from the entrance), blocking the entry. A station keeps the door area clear; gurney/wheelchair/cart park along a wall near the entrance, in a row, not in the middle.
  - [medium] No hand sanitizer/sink at the door, no sharps bin, no crash cart or defibrillator next to the station, no whiteboard; the one flavour item (terminal) appears in corners only. Add them near the counter and the door.
  - [medium] Wealth barely changes the result: squatter, low and luxury look the same (counter + chair + cabinet). Low should be a table with crates and a cooler, luxury a curved or longer counter, several chairs, a waiting lounge, plants and screens.
  - [low] Medical cabinets are sometimes single cells in the corner and at the counter's end (seed 5, 10), reading as leftovers; cabinets must line a stretch of wall behind the staff chairs as one run.
### pharmacy
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a real counter/service-window front that separates customers from staff, with the stock behind it; (2) the stock as shelf/cabinet rows only in the staff zone, not filling the whole room; (3) the mandatory pharmacy details (fridge, safe, workstation, sink, bin); (4) a customer area with queue space and waiting seats; (5) wealth and Shadowrun flavour.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m autodispenser kiosk to 14x10 m luxury): a counter/hatch across the front with 1-2 service windows (armored glass in street/black-market variants); behind it 2-4 parallel shelf/drug-cabinet rows 1 m apart, a medicine fridge (cold chain), a narcotics safe/vault, terminals and label printer, sink and bin, staff door; outside the counter a queue zone, 3-4 waiting chairs, optionally a consultation corner, OTC gondolas in retail styles, a robot dispenser for corp/luxury, camera and guard in black-market, crates and plank shelves in squatter. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 20x14 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The room is only shelf rows: 3-4 identical rows of 4-cell shelf blocks span the room like a warehouse or library, in every size and wealth (low to luxury look identical). A pharmacy is a counter front plus a back stock zone. Customers must not be able to walk between the shelves; the shelf rows belong behind the counter, and the shelves should hug the walls or form a few short parallel rows in the staff area.
  - [high] The counter is wrong: a thin 1-cell strip glued to a side wall (west or east, sometimes in a corner), perpendicular to the door and unrelated to it, with shelf rows running through it (seeds 2, 4, 6, 7). A dispensing counter must face the entrance and stretch across the room (or an L), with the public zone between door and counter and the staff side behind it. A counter on the long side wall behind shelves makes no sense. Seed 5 (squatter) puts the counter in a corner at the far end with no function.
  - [high] Missing pharmacy-defining objects: no medicine fridge, no safe/narcotics cabinet, no terminal/PC with label printer, no sink, no waste bin, no scale/BP station, no waiting chairs. Only counter, shelf, one stool and one round object (stool/bin, in a corner by the counter) appear. A room with these objects missing reads as an empty storeroom.
  - [high] No customer side: no queue space in front of the counter, no waiting chairs or bench, no info shelf or OTC display; the big empty carpeted area between door and first shelf row looks unused, and walking from the door leads into shelves with nothing to approach. Provide a waiting row (2-4 chairs) and a clear 2-3 m queue strip in front of the counter.
  - [medium] Counts scale with room area only: bigger rooms (20x14) get 4 rows of 6+2 blocks; shelf count should be capped, large rooms should instead gain a back store/compounding area and a customer lounge. Rows are far too long (up to 12 m unbroken) with no cross aisle.
  - [medium] Shelf rows are left floating in the middle with 4-cell gaps and a free strip at the wall (seeds 2, 6), so the wall stays empty while the room centre is cluttered; real stock sits against the walls, with floating rows only as gondolas in the customer area and then with price-sign orientation facing the aisle. Row ends should touch a cross wall or leave a 1 m passage consistently; seed 7 (north-south rows) is cut by the central row and a counter at the east wall.
  - [medium] No wealth or setting differences: squatter/low should look improvised (crates, plank shelves, cooler box, barred window), middle a regular counter pharmacy, high/luxury a robot dispenser, consultation booth, wall screen, plants and seating; the Shadowrun touch (armored counter glass, guard, camera, stimpatch/medkit display, bioware cooler) is absent.
  - [low] The only orientation cue is the door at the south; the one stool/round object next to the counter faces nowhere (no clerk workstation behind the counter). Add a staff chair at a terminal behind the counter, and a second (staff) door on the stock side for larger variants.
### lab
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m mobile lab to 10x6 m hospital lab, ghetto to corp/biotech): continuous wall benches (L or U shape) with analyzer, centrifuge, microscope in a row on top; a sink plus eyewash/hand wash close to the door; fridge and freezer side by side; 1-2 islands with a stool per workplace and >=1.2 m aisles; PC desk and label printer; reagent cabinet, sharps/biohazard bins; larger labs split into a reception/sample-intake zone at the door and an analysis zone; Shadowrun flavour (DNA sequencer, cyberware diagnostic bench, drone). Generated 10 labs (12x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The lab is nearly empty. Only wall counter fragments and a few stools: a 30x20 cell (150 m2) lab holds 3 bench pieces and 3 stools, a 24x16 lab the same. No analyzers, centrifuges, microscopes, freezers or fridges appear in most results (middle/high/luxury look the same as squatter). Needs far more equipment, scaled with area, and the wealth levels need to look different (more and better machines, not just more bare counters).
  - [high] Benches are short isolated stubs scattered on random walls (1-2 cells wide, with gaps and on all four walls, e.g. one on each side of a room), not a continuous working bench. A lab must have long continuous wall benches (one or two walls, L or U shaped, corner-to-corner) with equipment in a row on top, and the rest of the walls left to cabinets or fridges. A bench in the middle of an otherwise empty wall with nothing next to it reads as a leftover.
  - [high] Missing islands: big rooms (20x14 and bigger) have a huge empty centre with no work island. Add 1-3 parallel islands/tables with stools on both sides and 1.2-1.5 m (2-3 cells) aisles to the wall benches, oriented along the long room axis, so the floor is used the way a real lab fills it.
  - [high] Stools do not belong to a workplace: they float 1-2 cells beside a bench end, sit in front of a counter stub, or stand alone in a corner or in the middle of the floor. Each stool must be pulled up to a bench/island, in front of a working position, facing it, one per workplace, none at benches with nothing to work on.
  - [medium] Core lab furniture is missing or lone: fridge (cooled sample storage), -20 C freezer and reagent/chemical cabinet should stand together in a storage group (usually near the sample reception or the back), not as a single fridge in a corner. Only one of ten rooms had a fridge/two sinks. A sink with eyewash and a hand wash are expected next to the door and in a larger lab also at the far end; here a lone sink floats at the north wall (seed 9) or in the middle of the north wall far from the door (seed 7).
  - [medium] Terminals (the cyan squares) are lone and in corners or the middle of an empty wall with no desk or chair (seeds 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9). A PC/LIMS station should stand at a bench or a small desk with a chair, near the analyzers, and a second one at the intake desk by the door.
  - [medium] No sample intake: real labs have a reception counter or pass-through at the door for incoming samples plus a label printer; and a cleaner entrance zone. A short counter right beside the door (not in the door line) would anchor the room and give it a clear front and back.
  - [medium] Door area and walkways are fine (nothing blocks the door), but the open floor is so large that everything feels pushed to the walls. Fill from the walls inwards instead of random positions, and keep a clear main route door-to-islands.
  - [low] No safety details: wall-mounted eyewash/shower, biohazard and sharps bins, first-aid kit, fire extinguisher, hazard sign; a fume hood or safety cabinet in higher wealth. A trash bin beside the sink and bench ends would also be expected.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour absent: a lab here could carry a DNA sequencer, cyberware/biosign diagnostic bench, specimen drone or tissue tank in the high/luxury version; the squatter back-alley version should be a mismatched table with a mini fridge and crates instead of the same bare counters.
### xray
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the room needs a control zone: a lead-glass booth or screen with console, operator chair and window onto the table, next to the door side; (2) the imaging equipment must be a recognisable X-ray setup (table with tube arm over it, wall bucky/upright detector, apron rack) and in the right place; (3) patient support items (changing cubicle, chair, bench, aprons, sink); (4) wealth/Shadowrun variation.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x8 dental to 20x14 luxury CT suite): a central table with at least 1 m free on all sides and a ceiling tube; wall bucky on a side wall; control booth (about 1.5-2 m) in a corner behind lead glass with its own door, PC with 2 monitors; lead apron rack and hooks beside the door; changing cubicle; patient chair/wheelchair parking; sink; trash bin; cassette/contrast cabinet; red "X-ray on" lamp above the door; luxury adds CT/MRI gantry plus a second bay; street clinic adds jury-rigged console, cyberware scanner arch, drone and crates. One 1.2 m+ patient door (bed width), booth with a second door.
  - Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 20x14 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10): all are the same four objects in a near identical arrangement (a bed-like table in the centre, one 3x5 machine in a wall corner, a terminal+chair pair, a small marker). Wealth and size change nothing except empty floor.
  - [high] No control booth/operator position: a lone terminal with chair stands against a wall, often in a corner or far side, with no screen, no lead glass, no wall between operator and table. It must form a booth or screen directly beside the door side of the room, with the chair facing the table/window and the terminal close to the chair (here chair and terminal are 1 cell apart but pointing nowhere; seeds 3, 7, 10 put it far from everything).
  - [high] The "machine" (a 3x5 block with two round lenses) is read as a generic machine; it reads neither as a tube/detector nor as a CT. A wall-standing upright detector (thin, flush to a side wall, patient stands facing it) is missing. The table must be paired with a ceiling tube/arm centred over it, aligned with the table axis, and the upright detector must face the middle of the room with free standing space of about 1 m.
  - [high] The machine position is random (back wall, east wall, beside the door in seeds 1 and 4/6) and sometimes jammed beside the entrance, where a patient would bump into it; it should stand on a wall opposite or beside the table, not in the door swing/entry line.
  - [high] The table is oriented the same way everywhere and is placed in the geometric centre, so that in small rooms (10x10) the 1 m clearance and the door walkway get tight, and in big rooms (20x14, 18x12) it floats alone in an empty hall. Table length axis should follow the longer room axis, head away from the door, and the room should be sized/filled around it (ceiling rails, bucky, booth on one side).
  - [medium] Missing patient flow items: lead aprons on a rack/hooks beside the door, changing cubicle or screen (for larger rooms), wheelchair/stretcher parking spot, a chair or bench for waiting patients. Currently the whole room is bare except the table.
  - [medium] No hygiene and storage: sink and soap dispenser next to the door, trash bin, a cabinet for cassettes/contrast agent/positioning pads, and an emergency/crash cart in higher wealth. A wall cabinet along the side wall fills the dead space.
  - [medium] Wealth does not matter: low and luxury look identical. Luxury and high should add a CT gantry with its own table, a second imaging bay, two monitors in the booth, and a wall screen. Squatter/low version should look improvised (old machine, crates, cables, a mismatched chair).
  - [medium] Large rooms (20x14) have 80% empty floor; they should either shrink or contain a second imaging station (bays separated by curtain or the booth in between), as real radiology suites have 2-3 modalities.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour absent: cyberware scanner arch, a monitor with ghost-in-the-bones overlays, a drone cart, hazard/radiation signs, warning lamp over the door.
  - [low] Only one door: a booth should have its own second door; the main door should be wider (1.2 m+ for stretchers), which it appears to be (7 cells).
### morgue
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the morgue must read as a working room: autopsy table(s) with a sink/wash station at the head and a drain, instead of empty floor; (2) cold-storage drawers as ONE bank (or two facing banks with an aisle) along a wall, not 2-6 separate blocks scattered over all four walls; (3) furnish for the size: large rooms must get more tables/banks/equipment, not just more empty floor; (4) clear gurney route and door area; (5) small-room details (desk, scale, cart, shelves, bins) and wealth differences.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x8 to 24x16 cells, ripper backroom to hospital/corp morgue): double door in a short wall, gurney parked near the door; one cold-drawer bank (4-12 drawers) along one wall with about 2.5 m free pull-out space in front; 1-3 steel autopsy tables in a row mid-room with about 1.2 m free around, each with lamp, scale and a sink at the head; floor drain; hand-wash sink by the door; instrument cart/workbench; writing desk with terminal; shelves for body bags; biohazard bin; luxury adds viewing alcove, lockers, dispensary, evidence cabinet; low-wealth is one table, a small fridge/ice chest and crates. Generated 12 morgues (10x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] Many morgues contain no autopsy table at all, only drawer blocks and a lone gurney or nothing (m3 low 14x12, m6 squatter 10x10, m4 luxury 20x14, m8 high 30x20: table-less). A morgue without a table, sink and drain is just a cold store. A table is mandatory (more with area), with a sink at its head and a drain; the cause of "o" tables is an operating table, which has headrest at the north and no instrument tray, lamp or scale: mark it as an autopsy table.
  - [high] Drawer blocks are scattered: 2-6 separate 3x7-cell blocks on all four walls, some at the room corners, some in odd positions (m8 30x20: four blocks one per wall edge, each far from the others; m4: blocks on 3 walls). Real cold storage is one continuous bank (or two facing banks) on one wall with a common pull-out aisle (about 5 cells deep) in front, and no furniture in the pull-out zone. Group them, and flip them so the doors face the room.
  - [high] Large rooms are nearly empty: 20x14 and up have 100+ cells of bare floor (m8 30x20 = 600 cells, 4 blocks + 2 gurneys; m3, m6 and m11 mid-size, one or two blocks only). Count of tables, drawers and gurneys must scale with area, with a working zone (tables in a row, 1.2 m around) and a storage zone; otherwise the room is better made smaller or turned into a multi-purpose lab/prep room.
  - [high] Sinks are rare and misplaced: several rooms have none at all (m3, m4, m6, m8, m11), others have a sink in a far corner (m7, m9) or at a drawer block. A morgue needs a wash station at the table head and a hand-wash sink next to the door (hygiene when leaving); plus a floor drain under the table.
  - [medium] Gurneys are parked randomly: touching the door wall beside the entrance (m4, m8), crosswise at a wall (m9, m12: head pushed against the wall with feet in the room), two in a row next to each other with nothing (m12), or lone in the room middle. A gurney must be parked along a wall near the door (long side against the wall, free beside it) or placed directly at a drawer bank/table, never blocking the door swing or the central aisle.
  - [medium] Door area and walkway: the drawer block sits right against the door wall in several rooms (m1, m12 at the door-side corner; m7 block in front of the door), narrowing the entry. Keep 2 m free in front of a double door and a straight route from door to table and from table to drawer bank.
  - [medium] Missing details: no desk/terminal for the autopsy report, no instrument cart at the table (the medical cart floats in the room centre/at a wall in m5, m9), no scale, no shelves for body bags and supplies, no biohazard bin, no ceiling lamp over the table, no lockers/changing for staff, no X-ray lightbox. Medical cart/workbench must stand beside a table, not randomly.
  - [low] Wealth reads little: luxury (m4, m11) differs only by more drawers; add a viewing/identification alcove or window with chairs, lockers, evidence cabinet; low/squatter should be one improvised table, an ice chest or 2 small drawers and crates, not the same drawer blocks.
### doctor_office
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x8 cell ripperdoc back room to 20x14 cell luxury private doctor): desk (3x2 cells) with doctor chair behind it and 1-2 patient chairs facing the doctor across the desk, terminal on desk; exam couch along a wall with 1 m free on its open side, curtain or screen; sink with cabinet near the couch; instrument cart beside the couch; file cabinet/shelf, scale, waste bin, plant, wall posters; bigger rooms add a lounge or a consult/exam split, rug, diagnostic scanner; squatter version = scarred desk plus improvised surgery table. Generated 12 rooms (10x8 to 20x14 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] The room is only a desk and sometimes a bed: 6 of 12 results contain nothing but desk plus 3 chairs (and at most one tiny corner box), so a doctor's office has no exam couch, sink, cabinet or shelves. Every doctor_office must have an exam couch, a sink with a small cabinet, and storage (file/medicine cabinet); the couch must not be random.
  - [high] Content does not scale with size: 14x12 to 20x14 cell rooms (about 12 x 9 m) hold the same desk and chairs and are 85-90 percent empty carpet. Larger rooms must add things (second couch is not it) - consult corner with sofa/armchairs, rug, shelves, scanner, cabinets, plants - or the room should stay small.
  - [high] Furniture is scattered in the middle of the floor and not anchored to walls: desk plus chairs float away from the walls (d3, d6, d11, d12: desk in the open with chairs behind it), the couch stands unrelated to anything.
