Keep as a row but tie count to the number of guards (about one per bed/chair), place near the door or on a short wall, and keep clear floor in front (1 m) where guards change.
  - [medium] Bunks (g4, g6, g8) are placed anywhere on a wall, often at the door side or with a locker/white item right next to them, and singly in a huge room; real guard bunks come in pairs/rows along one wall with a nightstand or locker at the foot and are not near the door. Number of bunks must match the shift size and some guard rooms have none (day post).
  - [medium] Weapon rack/armory (olive stacked block) is tucked in a corner far from the door (g1, g3, g5, g8) or against a wall segment away from the post. It should be on a wall near the desk/door, visible to the guard, ideally with a locked cabinet look; at most one.
  - [medium] Wall screen (blue strip) is placed at random: on the side wall far from the table (g2, g5, g9), by the door on the entry wall (g3). It should face the guards' seating/desk: a monitor wall above the post desk or a TV opposite the couch/table, not behind the bunks.
  - [medium] Small white items scattered on walls and in corners (toilet/sink/coffee-like) are single and isolated, e.g. one on the left wall and another 3 cells below (g6, g7), or one in the middle of the east wall (g9, g10). Group wet/kitchen items together into a kitchenette (counter, sink, coffee machine, fridge) in a corner, and keep them away from the bunk side.
  - [low] Details missing: no desk with paperwork, key cabinet, whiteboard/duty roster, extinguisher, first-aid, bin, camera/panic button; no wealth-dependent style (squatter vs luxury looks identical apart from item counts).
### reception
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches: corp/law/hotel/clinic/factory gatehouse/police/flophouse/arcology/dental/ER, 8x8 to 16x12 cells): a counter 4-10 cells wide facing the door, anchored to a wall or with a back-office wall behind it, staff chair(s) and terminal(s) behind; a waiting group (2-6 chairs in a row or sofa plus low table) on a side wall facing the counter; plants in corners, bin, coat stand/water cooler; type-specific extras (key rack and luggage cart in hotels, armored glass and bench in police, lockers/PPE and time clock in factories, vending in clinics); a door onward behind or beside the counter; 1.5 m free walkway from entrance to counter and a 1 m staff aisle.
  - Generated 12 receptions alone (office, hotel, clinic, police, factory, corp_office, hospital, street_doc, data_centre; 8x10 to 20x14, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] The room contains essentially only the desk block plus a lone chair and 1-3 corner items. There is no waiting area at all: no chairs, sofa or low table for visitors. A reception without seating is not a reception; every room must have a visitor group (row of 2-6 chairs or sofa with table) facing the counter or along a side wall, scaled with area.
  - [high] 70-85 percent of the floor is empty carpet (20x14 squatter, 18x12 luxury, 16x12 corp are nearly bare). Furniture count must scale with room size and wealth: more seating groups, plants, shelves, notice boards, water cooler, vending, bin, coat stand; poor rooms should look worn and mismatched, luxury ones with lounges and art, not just emptier.
  - [high] The counter is the same size and shape (about 8x3, only rotated) in every room regardless of room size, wealth or building type, and it floats 2-3 cells off the wall (often with the chair squeezed between desk and wall) instead of being anchored. It should be a proper counter or desk island: longer in wide rooms, anchored to the wall or with a back-office wall behind it, a visible staff side with a 1 m aisle, optionally L-shaped or with a second workstation, and a clear transaction side toward the visitor.
  - [high] The desk is often turned sideways (against a side wall, front toward the room centre) and the staff chair is on the wall side, so staff sit between the counter and the wall with their back to nothing and the layout does not face the entrance. The counter should face the door/visitors; staff stand behind it, with the chair and monitor on the staff side.
  - [medium] Nothing behind the counter: a real front desk has file cabinets, shelves, a printer, a key rack (hotel) or lockers, and a door to the back office/inner area. The room has no inner door at all, so the reception has no purpose as a gateway; there should be a second door near or behind the counter, and the entrance-to-counter path must be clear.
  - [medium] No type specific content: hotel, clinic, police, factory, data centre and street doc look identical. Expect key rack, luggage cart, bell (hotel); sign-in, magazine table, vending (clinic); glass partition, bench, metal detector (police); lockers, PPE rack, time clock, visitor badges (factory); security gate/turnstile and camera (corp, data centre).
  - [low] Corner details (plant, small cube, camera-like items) are sparse and often placed at the entrance wall corners only; add wall details (screen/logo behind the counter, notice board, clock, fire extinguisher, sign) and a rug in the waiting zone.
### staff_room
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 sketches: office kitchenette 12x10, corp lounge 20x14, hotel staff room with lockers, low-wealth back room, guard room, canteen-style 24x16, clinic, luxury lounge, factory break room, narrow 16x8): a contiguous kitchen run on one wall (sink, fridge at the end, microwave, coffee machine) with a bin; 1 table per ~4-6 people with 4-6 chairs and 1.2 m clearance; a sofa group facing a TV/screen; a row of lockers; vending machines in a row; notice board by the door; door side kept clear; wealth sets quality (mismatched chairs and clutter when poor, espresso bar, sofas and plants when rich).
- Findings (seen in 10 solo rooms 12x10 to 30x16 over all wealth levels, plus a hotel; the result is nearly identical everywhere):
  1. Biggest problem: the room is one 2x2-chair table in the middle plus a few loose 1-cell grey boxes scattered along walls and corners. It does not read as a break room. Furniture count and type do not grow with room size: a 30x16 room has the same single table, so it looks empty. Larger rooms need more tables (a grid of tables with aisles), or distinct zones (dining, lounge, kitchen).
  2. There is no recognisable kitchenette. Sink, fridge, microwave, coffee machine and bin must form ONE contiguous counter run on a single wall (fridge or sink at the end, appliances side by side on the worktop). Right now these items are single cells spread over different walls and corners, some next to the door.
  3. Wall items must be grouped, not scattered: lockers in a row, vending machines side by side, bins next to the kitchen and door. Pairs of items pushed into corners or at random wall spots look accidental.
  4. The sofa (when present) is stuck against a wall or corner with nothing to face. It needs a low table and a TV/screen opposite, forming a lounge corner. Rich rooms may have several sofas, but each needs its own group, not one sofa per wall.
  5. Table and chairs: the single table stands dead centre, aligned with nothing. Place tables so that aisles of at least 1 m remain to the walls, the kitchen and the door. Chairs must stand tightly against the table with a clear approach, and 4 chairs for a lone table is fine but an odd number or a bench is welcome in poor places.
  6. The door side is OK, but items right at the door jamb (a box in the corner next to the door, in the door swing area) should not be there. Keep a clear walkway from door to table and kitchen. A notice board or fire panel belongs on the wall beside the door.
  7. Wealth/style variation is almost absent: poor, middle and luxury rooms differ only by an occasional sofa or blue panel. Poor rooms should have mismatched chairs, fewer and older appliances; rich rooms a coffee bar with stools, plants, art and a sofa group.
  8. The blue glowing item (screen/aquarium?) appears alone on a wall with no purpose. Screens belong opposite seating.
  9. Missing details a real staff room has: lockers or coat hooks, notice board, bin(s) and recycling, wall clock (not needed in a top-down map), first-aid or fire extinguisher, and in hotels/factories a locker row with a bench.
### electrical_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (apartment meter room 6x8 up to arcology switchgear hall 30x20): boards flush on walls in continuous lineups (4-8 cabinets side by side), 2-3 cells clear aisle in front, door outward with panic bar, extinguisher and signs next to the door, overhead cable trays; large rooms add transformers (caged, 1 m clearance), UPS/battery cabinets, capacitor bank, back-to-back double rows with an aisle between, a small maintenance table/schematic; squatter rooms: a single rusty box with illegal taps, no extinguisher; luxury: neat labelled lineup.
- Findings (12 rooms viewed, office/hotel/hospital/factory/data centre/apartment/corp office/warehouse, squatter to luxury):
  1. Cabinets must form a continuous lineup. Today every room scatters 3-4-wide cabinets as isolated pieces with random gaps along all four walls, and some stand alone in corners; reads as furniture sprinkled around, not a switchgear row. Group them into one or two tight rows (touching, same depth, same front side) on one or two walls.
  2. Cabinet fronts must face a clear aisle and the door side; keep one wall (the door wall's opposite or the long wall) as the main lineup. Do not spread boards over all four walls including the door wall.
  3. No boards directly beside the door (hinge/latch side, e.g. next to the door opening) - they block the swing and the access path; keep a free zone in front of and around the door.
  4. Large rooms (data centre, factory, hospital, 20+ cells) are mostly empty floor with a handful of boxes; size and equipment count do not scale. Add bigger heavy gear (transformers with a keep-out clearance, UPS and battery cabinets, capacitor bank), back-to-back rows with an aisle, and cable trays/floor ducts linking them; or accept smaller rooms.
  5. Equipment variety is almost nil: orange-stripe boards, a few green and dark ones. Differentiate main switchgear (large, centre of the lineup), sub-distribution (small wall boxes), meter cabinets (apartment/hotel: a row of meters), UPS/battery, and per-building flavour (data centre = UPS A/B pairs, factory = transformer and capacitor bank, hospital = isolated IT panels).
  6. Fire extinguisher and warning triangle sit in far corners, unrelated to the door; extinguisher belongs beside the door inside (or just outside), warning sign on the door/lineup.
  7. Wealth/style barely shows: squatter or low rooms should look improvised (one old box, loose cable bundles, junk, no extinguisher, maybe a bypass), luxury/corp neat and complete (labelled lineup, key box, maintenance table with schematic, security keypad).
  8. Missing small details: maintenance table or workbench with diagrams in larger rooms, floor cable ducts/tray, ventilation grille, spare-parts crate; no beds/chairs obviously, correct already.
  9. Door swing is outward and door is wide enough; double doors on a 10-cell room are plausible for big gear, but a single door fits small rooms; keep a second exit in big switchgear halls (escape at both ends of the lineup).
### network_closet
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 variants, office IDF to hospital MDF to Shadowrun slum closet): racks (19", ~1x3 cells incl. door swing space) in a straight row against one wall, all fronts facing the same side with a ~2-cell front aisle and ~1 cell behind; UPS under/next to the racks, overhead cable tray running from rack to the door, wall patch field, wall cabinet in tiny closets, AC split in larger ones, small work table/stool, extinguisher, spare-parts shelf; slum variant with jury-rigged gear and a terminal.
- Findings (12 renders: office, hotel, hospital, corp_office, corp_lab, clinic, data_centre; 1-3 racks each):
  1. Racks are scattered over random walls and corners (top-left, right wall, bottom corner next to the door) instead of one aligned row; they must stand together in a row/lineup with all fronts facing the same direction.
  2. Rack fronts face different directions and some sit with their back to the open room; fronts must face an open aisle (about 2 cells deep) and the room should be laid out around that aisle.
  3. Rooms are far too empty: 1-3 racks in a 10x12+ room reads as an empty hall. Either the room is smaller (a closet) or it needs more equipment; count should scale with area (a 14x12 room holds a row of 4-6 racks).
  4. Rack placed beside or across from the door at the room edge (bottom corner by the hinge) blocks or crowds the entrance; keep the door and a straight path to the rack front free.
  5. Missing supporting equipment: UPS/battery cabinets beside the racks, a ceiling cable tray or floor duct linking the racks and the door, wall-mounted patch panel/cabinet, AC/cooling unit (heat is the defining feature), fire extinguisher.
  6. Missing human details in larger rooms: small work table or crash-cart with stool, spare-parts shelf, labelled cables; none exist, so the room looks unused.
  7. Double doors on a small locked closet look wrong; a single door (or a single door with a bigger room) fits better, double only for large MDF halls.
  8. Racks have no variety in kind (some show amber slot lines, some plain); a mix of racks, a patch panel board and a UPS in the row would look more purposeful. Wealth should change density and tidiness (squatter/low: jury-rigged, mismatched, loose; luxury/corp: neat symmetric rows, cooling, second UPS).
  9. Small closets (under ~6x6) should be filled in a different pattern (wall cabinet plus a single rack) than big halls; currently all sizes use the same sparse scatter.
### mechanical_room
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 sketches: apartment boiler room, office HVAC, hotel plant, hospital N+1, data-centre cooling, factory compressor/steam, luxury rooftop plant, slum single boiler, corp arcology, heat-pump closet): equipment in rows along walls facing a central aisle, boilers/chillers/AHUs as duty/standby pairs, pump pairs, buffer tank and expansion vessel beside the boilers, electrical panel by the door, floor drain mid-room, pipework overhead, 1 m clearance in front of everything. Renders checked: 10 office mechanical rooms (12x10 to 36x20, squatter to luxury), saved in scratchpad `mech/`.
- Findings (prioritised):
  1. Equipment is scattered, not organised: big units sit in random corners and on random walls (some with a gap, some wedged in corners, some on 3 sides). A real plant room has machines of one kind lined up in a row on one wall with equal spacing, all service sides facing the same aisle; boilers together, chillers/AHUs together, pump sets together.
  2. Rooms look empty and cavernous: in the large rooms (24x14 and bigger) 4-5 units sit on the perimeter and the middle 60 percent is bare floor. Real plant rooms are dense: more units (duty/standby pairs, N+1), second row or island, with aisles only where needed. Size should drive equipment count; or an oversized plant room should read as a hall with rows, not a few lone boxes.
  3. Missing the supporting kit that makes it read as a mechanical room: buffer/storage tanks, expansion vessels, pump sets (small pump pairs in front of boilers/chillers), manifolds/header pipework, water softener, control/VFD cabinet bank, floor drain and sump, workbench or tool cabinet, spare-parts shelf. Currently only big two-fan units, a few small glowing units, one small panel and a red alarm item appear.
  4. Little variety: wealth/condition/building type do not change the plant (the same unit types and look in squatter and luxury). Expect one old boiler plus a water tank in a slum, redundant paired plant in hospital/data centre/luxury, dirty or rusty clutter and leaks when run down.
  5. Electrical panels are tiny and randomly placed (even mid-wall far from the door, or two stacked on a wall alone); a panel needs a free 1 m zone in front and normally sits next to the door as a group (switchboard plus VFDs).
  6. Units backed into walls and into each other with no maintenance space: pairs touching with only a hairline gap (3 stacked units on one wall) and units flush to walls on the service side. Each machine needs front access clearance (about 1 m) and at least a hand's gap behind/between; nothing may block the door swing or the straight path from the door.
  7. Units placed right next to the double door (immediately beside the doorway) make entry cramped; keep the first 2 cells in front of and beside the door free as a lobby/aisle, with the aisle running through the room to the equipment.
  8. Orientation: units show the same fan face regardless of which side faces the aisle; rotate so the service/front face points into the room and fans or louvres point to the exterior wall where there is one.
### generator_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the genset must be placed like a real one: lengthwise along the room, with its radiator end towards an exterior louvre wall and service aisles (about 1 m) on both long sides and at the control end, not as a lone slab floating in the room centre; (2) size must drive equipment: big rooms hold 2-4 gensets in a row (N+1), a small room one; (3) the supporting kit must be grouped with the genset (day tank beside it, ATS/switchgear and control panel together next to the door); (4) louvres, exhaust and fuel-line details; (5) variety by building type and wealth.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x6 m hospital N+1 to 5x4 m slum): one genset (or a row of 2-4 in hospital/data centre) with its long axis parallel to the long wall, radiator end at an exterior louvre wall (intake louvre opposite, discharge louvre at radiator), exhaust silencer and flue leaving through the same wall, belly tank or day tank on or next to the skid, ATS and paralleling switchgear in a row on the wall by the door with 1 m clear in front, battery charger and fuel pump on the wall, bund or drip tray around the fuel tank, floor drain, extinguisher and eye wash at the door, spare-parts shelf or workbench in industrial buildings, hoist rail in factories, jerry cans and drums plus a manual switch in slums, luxury and corp buildings clean with sound enclosure and battery rack. Generated 12 rooms (hospital, data centre, hotel, corp office, factory, office, prison, corp lab, warehouse, police station; 14x10 to 36x18 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] The genset is always the same single small unit (about 11x6 cells, two fans) dropped in the centre of the room, whatever the room size (36x18 and 16x12 look alike). A real generator hall is built around the machine: it stands on one side with its radiator end against an exterior wall, the long axis along the room. Floating in the middle, with 8+ cells of bare floor all round, it reads as a stage prop. Move it to a wall/end and make it face the louvre wall.
  - [high] Rooms are cavernous: in 24x14 and larger rooms (g3, g4, g7, g12) 60-80 percent of the floor is empty and the few other items hug the perimeter. A real generator room is sized around the engine plus 1 m aisles; large rooms need several gensets in a row (N+1 redundancy in hospital, data centre, luxury) with equal spacing and a common aisle, plus a fuel and switchgear zone, rather than empty floor.
  - [high] No fuel supply near the engine: the red rounded blocks (tanks/drums?) stand in random corners and on random walls, far from the genset, with no pipes and no relation to it. A day tank must sit directly beside or under the genset with a visible fuel line; the larger storage tank belongs in a bunded corner or the adjacent room. Counts also vary wildly (0 to 3), sometimes right by the door (g8, g9 beside the entry line).
  - [high] Electrical gear (green striped switchgear, orange striped panels) is scattered over random walls (north wall, west wall, east wall, next to the door), often several metres from the genset and from each other. ATS, control panel and switchgear must be one row on a wall near the door (or beside the genset's control end) with a free 1 m zone in front, so one person can reach the panel and still see the engine.
  - [medium] Missing the details that sell the room: exhaust silencer/flue and intake and discharge louvres on the exterior wall, battery charger/start batteries, fuel pump or manifold, spill bund or drip tray, floor drain, hoist or lifting eye, eye wash, spare-parts shelf or workbench, warning signs. At least louvres or ducts on the radiator side and a visible bund under the fuel items.
  - [medium] The door is always on the south wall and the equipment (panels and tanks) are tucked against the door wall in several rooms (g4, g6, g8, g12), so the first steps into the room are crowded while the open middle stays unused. Keep 2 cells free beside the door and put gear along the walls and where the aisle leads: entry, aisle, panel, engine control end.
  - [medium] Little variety: the same layout appears for hospital, prison, hotel, warehouse and luxury lab. Expect redundant paired gensets with a bunded tank in hospital and data centre, a clean enclosure with battery rack in corp/luxury, rusty old unit with jerry cans and drums and a manual changeover in slums, a workbench and oil drums in factory and warehouse.
  - [low] Extinguisher (red circle) and a small marker in the corner are tiny and at the far corner of the room; the extinguisher belongs next to the door (and one by the engine). A second exit is missing in large rooms, and emergency stop button/ventilation controls beside the door are absent.
### host_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) racks as proper hot/cold-aisle rows (paired front-to-front across a wide cold aisle, back-to-back hot aisle between pairs), not identical rows all facing one way; (2) cooling and power equipment attached to the rack rows and in believable shapes/places; (3) one clear entry zone with console, extinguisher and access control; (4) variety by size, wealth and building type.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m slum/closet host to 10x8 m data hall, luxury caged row): racks 0.6x1 m in rows facing each other across about 2.4 m cold aisle, 1 m hot aisle behind, in-row or CRAC cooling at row ends or on a wall, UPS/battery cabinet and PDU, technician desk with crash cart beside the door, EPO button, gas extinguisher bottles, camera, card+biometric reader at the door, spare-parts shelf, cable tray; poor hosts have mismatched shelves and a window fan; luxury has a glass cage and a second operator booth. Generated 12 host rooms (corp_office, corp_lab, data_centre, hotel, office, hospital, police_station, casino; 10x10 to 20x16 cells; squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] All rack rows are identical and face the same way (3 to 4 rows of 4-cell-wide blocks, h1, h3, h4, h6, h10). Real rooms pair rows front-to-front with a wide cold aisle (about 3-4 cells) and then a narrow hot aisle behind, so rows come in pairs (2+2), with aisle widths alternating. Equal 3-cell gaps between all rows look like a library, not a server hall.
  - [high] Rack rows are floating in the room with the same margin on all sides and a dead strip at one end (h4: gap column then a short separate stack of racks at the east; h1/h3: unused wide side margins). Rows must run full length between walls with a clear 1 m side passage, row ends capped by a cooling unit or door, and no orphan short stacks.
  - [high] Racks and room size do not scale to the building: tiny rooms (10x10, 12x10) still get 2 to 4 rows of blocks, and a cramped hospital/hotel/office host looks the same as a corp data floor. Small hosts should have 2 to 4 racks in one row on one wall; only data centre/corp lab get multi-row halls. Slum/squatter hosts (h9) show the same neat rack rows as luxury; they should have mismatched shelves, a loose deck, cables and a fan.
  - [high] Cooling is poorly shaped: the red slatted blocks are tiny wall strips dropped at random on any wall (north, east, door wall, even in a corner with the entry marker) and sometimes two touch in an L (h11). Cooling must stand at the row ends or along the hot-aisle side, near the racks it cools, with one clear front; room has at most one or two, not at random corners.
  - [medium] The big grey units with two circles (h2, h7; UPS/generator/battery) are 9x6 cells, huge and packed against the door wall right next to the entry, blocking the first steps. Put UPS/battery as a compact cabinet row along a side wall away from the door, with a free front, and show it only in bigger or higher wealth rooms.
  - [medium] The technician console sits right at the door (bottom corner, back to the wall facing south wall) with the chair behind it, or in the far corner (h2, h7: top). A real console desk faces the racks or the door so the technician sees the room, with the chair turned to the desk, is about 2x1 m (here 6x3 cells reads like a counter), and has a free pull-out space. It also should not sit in the door swing line.
  - [medium] Missing details that make it a server room: extinguisher/gas bottles and EPO button at the door, camera and card/biometric reader at the entry, cable tray or raised floor marks, patch panel/KVM crash cart, spare parts shelf, warning signs. Nothing besides racks, cooling and console is present in any of the 12 renders.
  - [medium] Door: single door at the south wall always, no vestibule/mantrap even in corp/aaa buildings; big halls (h4, h10) have one narrow door at one end only. Big data halls need a second exit at the far end and a mantrap/security lock zone at the entry.
  - [low] No Shadowrun flavour in the room: no jackpoint/deck terminal, no spider's remote console, no maintenance drone parking, no faraday-cage marker; rooms look like a bare rack hall.
### spider_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (small office to casino/mega-HQ). Core: a row of 3-6 desks/jack-in couches all facing one central wall of monitors (opposite the door or on the long wall), 2-cell aisle behind chairs, rack/cable tray along a side wall with front clearance, supervisor desk near the door, break corner (coffee, cot/fridge) away from the screens; low wealth = mismatched desks, cot, clutter; luxury = curved video wall, lounge, minibar.
- Findings (from 10 renders: corp_office low/middle/high at 16x12 and 24x14, casino, data_centre, corp_lab, hospital; all near-identical):
  1. Rooms are far too empty: 2 desks, 1 cocoon, a few wall screens and a rack in 190-340 cells; the middle is a big void. A spider room should be densely equipped (4-8 workstations/couches, rack bank, supervisor desk, break corner).
  2. No shared focus: screens are scattered on random walls (south wall next to door, west, east, north) and desks sit on different walls facing different ways. Workstations must be a row (or two rows) facing ONE monitor wall; the video wall must be centred opposite the door or on the long wall, large and contiguous, not single 3-cell stubs.
  3. Wall screens are placed with nobody looking at them (south wall behind the door side, west/east walls beside nothing). Every wall screen needs seats/desks facing it with a viewing distance of 2-4 cells; screens behind operators' backs read as wrong.
  4. Seating: jack-in seats (couch/recliner/cocoon) are lone items stuck to the north wall, not paired with decker desks. Deckers need couches/recliners in a group (side by side, with cable/trode details) and a rigger console with drone control beside them.
  5. Desks are pushed against a wall with the chair in the room, so the operator faces the wall and has no sight of the room or door; prefer desks in the room facing the monitor wall, with the operator's back toward the door side being acceptable only with a supervisor desk watching the door.
  6. Missing support gear for a 24/7 watch room: coffee machine/table, mini-fridge, cot or bunk, lockers, whiteboard/host map, printer, bin, power/UPS and cable trays. Only a weapon locker appears (in one variant).
  7. Rack/server unit appears as a thin 4-cell strip jammed in a corner or at the door; racks need a proper bank along a side wall with an aisle in front and cabling to the desks.
  8. Door clearance: the door area is clear, but items cluster right beside the door (screens along the entry wall); keep a clean entrance zone and put the supervisor desk or coffee nearby instead of screens.
  9. Size/wealth scaling does nothing: low, middle and high look the same apart from an extra screen; low should be cluttered/mismatched with a cot, high should have a curved wall display, ergonomic chairs and lounge.
  10. Building type does not change it (casino should have a long surveillance monitor wall with a row of operators; data centre more racks; hospital a modest 2-3 desk set).
### drone_bay
- Status: done
- Blind design (summary): flyer cradles in a row along one wall (1-cell spacing), ground-drone pads in a row with a 2-cell lane to a launch door/hatch on an exterior wall, repair bench plus tool chest and parts shelving on the opposite wall, caged ammo/munitions corner away from the chargers, rigger console near the entry with a view of the dock row and hatch, spare-battery rack, fire extinguisher, floor lane markings. Ten variants from 10x6 up to 24x14 (corp, lab, casino, factory, data centre, gang, luxury, military). Full sketches in the scratchpad (drone_bay_blind.md).
- Findings (10 renders, corp office/lab, low to luxury, 10x8 to 24x16, all very similar):
  1. No launch door/hatch or vehicle-sized opening: the only exit is the single double door in the south wall, so there is no place for drones to go out; the bay reads as a plain room with a door.
  2. The ground-drone/charging pads are scattered in an evenly spaced grid over the whole floor, like furniture on a showroom floor. Real bays dock units in a row (or two rows) along a wall, with a clear lane in front, not floating in the middle with the same gap everywhere.
  3. In large rooms the middle and the whole south half stay empty (24x16, 20x14): the count of pads grows but they hug the north half, leaving a big dead zone. Add more variety instead of only more pads (bench, shelving, second row), or keep the room smaller.
  4. Missing repair/maintenance bench with tool chest and parts shelving; only appears rarely and not near the pads (a sofa-like block and a crate block appeared in two renders and look out of place).
  5. Missing rigger/control console with chair, which must sit near the door looking over the pads; only a tiny red dot (alarm/camera) and small markers at the door are visible.
  6. Missing ammo/munitions storage, ideally a cage or lockable cabinet in a separate corner away from the battery chargers; a lone small rack on the east wall appears only sometimes and without context.
  7. The wall-mounted charger/rack units along the north wall are good, but sit in a row with gaps and no relation to the pad row below; pads should line up with the racks (same spacing, same axis) so one unit corresponds to one dock.
  8. Pads should be oriented with a clear aisle toward the door/hatch; currently the first row sits 3 cells from the wall and the front row blocks nothing, but nothing says where drones travel. Mark a lane.
  9. Low/squatter wealth looks identical to luxury; poorer variants should have fewer, mismatched docks, clutter, crates, tool mess; richer ones tidy rows plus glass control booth.
  10. Safety details missing: fire extinguisher (Li-ion batteries), power/cable runs, floor lane markings, ventilation.
### vault
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the middle of the vault must not be an empty hall: a real strongroom is dense (box walls, racks, a work table), and 20x14 to 30x20 cell vaults here are 90% bare floor; (2) the contents must read as a vault (deposit-box walls, cash racks, bullion, counting/viewing table), not as identical shelf strips plus random crates; (3) wealth/size must change the look; (4) security details (cameras, mantrap/anteroom, panel, laser grid).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m branch strongroom to 10x7 m luxury private bank, plus casino cash vault, gun-shop strongroom, bullion, corp data vault, mob backroom safe, museum): single heavy door into an anteroom or corridor; deposit-box walls in contiguous runs on 1-3 walls with a 1.2 m aisle in front; cash racks in parallel rows with 1.2 m aisles and a pallet trolley; a counting or viewing table in the middle with chairs on both sides; camera in a corner facing the door; keypad/time-lock panel outside; small vaults = one wall of boxes plus one safe; Shadowrun flavour = laser grid, turret, biometric panel. Generated 10 casino vaults (12x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Far too empty for the size: only wall strips and 0-3 crates. A 30x20 cell (15x10 m) vault has about 5 shelf strips and 2 crates, the middle 70% is empty floor (v6, v10, v9). Large vaults need interior structure: free-standing rack rows or box islands in parallel rows with aisles (running from the door inward), a central counting/viewing table with chairs, trolleys. Alternatively large rooms should not be generated as one huge vault; they should look like a vault plus an anteroom or sorting area.
  - [high] The wall strips are all the same grey shelf with yellow ticks; deposit boxes, cash racks, weapon racks and bullion look identical, so no vault "type" is visible. Make the contents tell what is stored: a bank = continuous deposit-box walls with viewing booths; cash vault = racks with cash bundles + trolley + counting table; bullion = pallets/cages on the floor; corp = server/locker rows. One vault should be one type, not a mixed bag.
  - [high] Box walls are fragmented: runs of 6-8 cells with arbitrary gaps and corners left empty (v1, v2, v9: wall runs broken in the middle for no reason, a gap at the SE corner). Deposit-box walls should be continuous along the whole wall (except for the door), flush into corners, with an even aisle (>= 2 cells) in front.
  - [high] Crates are scattered randomly in the middle or jammed into a corner (v3, v8, v10: crate in a corner next to a shelf, v1: two crates floating diagonally, 3-4 cells from anything). Crates/pallets should be stacked together in a storage corner or in a row against a wall/in a cage, with aisles, never single and floating.
  - [medium] No table or chair anywhere: a vault has a counting table / viewing table (box is carried to it) with chairs or at least a stool, usually near the door. Add one in the middle section; it also fills the empty centre.
  - [medium] No wealth or building-type difference: squatter, middle and luxury vaults look the same (same strips, same crates), and no sprites are visible for safes (the red strip appears in v3/v5/v10 only, apparently a different item). A low-wealth one should be a small safe + crates, a luxury one tidy rows, glass viewing room, bullion cages.
  - [medium] Door side is plain: the door opens straight into the open floor with no anteroom/mantrap, no keypad/panel, and the corner objects (cameras?) are tiny dark squares in corners that are hard to read; the camera should clearly face the door and also cover the aisle. Add the vault door as a visibly heavy door (wider/thicker), a panel beside it outside, and a second camera in large vaults.
  - [medium] Door aisle is not respected: strips start right beside the door jamb (v4, v7 south wall strip ends 1 cell from the door; v10 shelf next to the door) which blocks the swing path and leaves no walkway; keep 2 cells free on both sides of the door and a clear straight lane from the door to the centre.
  - [low] Shadowrun details missing: laser grid or tripwires, turret/drone, biometric panel, alarm lights; and a fire/extinguishing detail (gas flood vent) that a real vault has.
  - [low] Rooms are generated with very large aspect/size for a vault (10 m x 8 m is a big bank vault, 7.5 x 6 m common): sizes above ~8 m should be the exception.
### safe_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x6 to 12x8 cells: apartment closet, corp executive, safehouse bunker, mage flat, hotel suite, lab, gang bunker, vault): one reinforced door in a corner, bunks/cots in a row along the long wall (1 per occupant, head to wall), supply shelving and water canisters on the opposite wall, chemical toilet behind a curtain in a far corner, comms/monitor desk with chair facing the door, weapon locker beside the door, air filter and first aid on a wall, 1 m aisle between rows. Generated 24 renders (corp_office/safehouse/mage_flat/hotel x low/high/luxury x 2 seeds, 14x12 cells, saved in the scratchpad subfolder safe_room) and viewed 12; the room looks the same whatever the building type.
  - [high] The room is nearly empty: only 4 to 8 objects in a 7x6 m room (84 cells of floor, 10 or fewer objects, all on the walls, the whole middle bare). A safe room is a densely packed shelter; it needs far more content (several bunks/cots, shelving racks, supply crates, water barrels, a table with chairs) and a size that matches its content, not a big hall with a few things on the walls.
  - [high] Core safe-room equipment is missing or unreadable: no comms/monitor desk with chair facing the door (CCTV monitors), no weapon locker/rack beside the door, no toilet or curtained corner, no air filter/vent unit, no medkit, no generator/battery; the cyan boxes are the only recognisable non-bunk items. Without them it reads as an empty storage cell, not a panic room.
  - [high] Bunks are scattered individually rather than arranged: bed blocks (4-cell strips) stand alone on random walls (west, east, north), sometimes one beside the door in the corner (corp_office_luxury_2: a bunk tucked at the door corner), sometimes a single bunk on the north wall. Bunks must form one or two parallel rows on the long wall(s) with equal spacing and heads to the wall, count matched to occupancy, and never next to or in the door swing.
  - [high] Nothing is arranged in relation to the door: nothing faces the entrance, the area just inside the door is empty or has one small cyan box (a stray item in the door line, corp_office_low/luxury_1), the door swings into the room. Door should be in the short wall, the guard/comms desk and weapon locker next to it, the sleeping area at the far end, and a clear 1 m walkway from door to bunks.
  - [medium] Items on the walls are placed at random heights along the walls with big gaps (single blue unit at the NE corner, a grey locker alone mid east wall, a blue slab alone in the middle of a wall); shelving/storage should be continuous runs along one wall with water and supplies beside each other, so the supplies read as one stockpile.
  - [medium] Wealth and building type produce no visible difference: luxury, low, corp, safehouse and hotel safe rooms have the same pieces. Expect: low = cots, barrels, bucket; corp = monitor wall, filtered air, bunks, locker; luxury = sofa, mini-fridge, screens, bathroom; mage flat = ward circle and focus locker; hotel = hardened suite.
  - [low] Double doors with a wide opening on some variants (the 3 m double door, corp_office/mage_flat) are unusual for a panic room; a single narrow steel door (about 1 m) is the norm; and no second exit/escape hatch or vent for bigger rooms.
### vending_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches from 6x5 hotel ice/vending niche to 16x6 transit row. Core: 2-6 machines (snack, drink, coffee) contiguous in ONE row on a wall, fronts to the room, 1 m+ (3 cells in hospitals) clear aisle in front, bins beside the machines/by the door, microwave and water cooler on a counter/wall opposite, optional small table + stools in bigger/richer rooms, luxury = espresso counter + fridge instead of snack machines, low wealth = one battered machine + overflowing bin.
- Findings (rendered 12 stand-alone rooms plus the room inside a 60x40 office; stand-alone rooms in 18x14..40x20 cells are filled absurdly):
  1. Machine count must be capped by purpose, not by room area: big rooms get dozens of machines (up to ~100 in a 40x20 room: wall-to-wall perimeter ring plus 4x5 grids of double blocks). A real vending room has 2-8 machines; excess space must stay empty or become a break/seating area with tables, counter, microwave, water cooler.
  2. Machines must stand in one or two rows against walls, backs to the wall, fronts to the room. Free-standing double-row blocks in the room centre are wrong (machines do not stand back-to-back in islands; they would show a back side). Perimeter ring is also wrong: machines on all four walls, including both sides of the door, make it a warehouse.
  3. Door relation: machines directly flank the door and crowd it; keep the wall around the door and the arc free (at least 1 cell each side) and leave a clear aisle in front of the door.
  4. Equipment variety is missing: only one kind of block is shown (all identical vending machines). Needs mix (snack, drink, coffee), plus bins next to them, a water cooler, a microwave on a counter; at most a stray bin/cooler appears now (1 round object in a random spot, not at a wall).
  5. The lone bin/round object is placed in the middle of the walkway (e.g. 3 cells from any wall); bins belong against walls next to machines or beside the door.
  6. Aisle widths are fine in the grid layouts but only because everything is spaced evenly; the cluster looks synthetic. Real clearance in front of a row is about 3 cells, not a 6-cell void with a second row far away.
  7. No seating: bigger rooms (office, hospital, factory) need one or two small tables with chairs or a bench; hotel/luxury versions need ice machine and espresso/fridge rather than snack grids.
  8. Wealth/condition has no visible effect: squatter/low rooms should have fewer, mismatched machines and an overflowing bin; luxury fewer machines and a counter with espresso.
  9. In the building context (office floor 1, 5x11 cells) it is acceptable: machines alternate along the two long walls with a 3-cell aisle, a bin and a coffee machine appear; but machines are staggered on both walls and wedged to the room's corners, and the door-end bottom row of three crowds the aisle. Prefer a tidy row on one wall.
  10. Door of stand-alone rooms is always a wide double door; a small vending room should have a single door.
### garage
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m squatter 1-car to 20x10 m staff garage, plus rigger workshop, biker garage, valet, mechanic): car on the axis of the door, 0.8-1 m door-opening gaps between cars, drive lane straight from the garage door, workbench + tool chest + shelves on the back wall (wall opposite the door), drums/bike rack/charging/fire extinguisher, personnel door on the side; workshop variants add rigger bench, drone cradles, lift. Generated 10 garages (12x10 to 40x24 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] The garage is only cars, a tool rack and a fire extinguisher. A real garage has a workbench with vise, a tool chest, shelves and a few barrels/crates; the working side (back wall, opposite the vehicle door) is empty in all 10 renders. Add a bench + chest + shelf group on one wall, never in the drive lane, more of it for squatter/low and rigger/workshop style.
  - [high] Cars are packed side by side with about 0 gap (1 cell or less between big grey slabs, g2-g9). Nobody can open a door or walk between them; parked cars need at least 0.8 m (2 cells) between them and a walkway along one wall. Fewer cars in the same width, or a gap after every car.
  - [high] Cars are plain featureless grey rectangles of identical size and colour, all same orientation; no motorcycles, vans, bikes or drones, no mix of vehicle sizes. A Shadowrun garage should mix a van/truck, car, bikes and drones (rigger), and the cars should not look like blank slabs (wheels/windscreen/hood).
  - [high] Layout does not read as a garage: cars stand in a row in the middle of the room (g5-g9 with big empty strips north and south of the block), the vehicles are not nose-to-wall and have no obvious lane. Park them against the back wall (or in two rows facing one lane) and leave one clear drive lane from the garage door to the bays, 5+ m turning room in front.
  - [medium] Doors: there is a wide double door on the north wall and also a regular door on the south wall; it is not clear which is the vehicle door. The vehicle door must be the widest opening, in line with the lane; the personnel door must be on the other side/short wall near the building core. The north door is much narrower than the car row in small garages (g1, g10), where cars stand beside it instead of in front of it.
  - [medium] Small garages (g1, g10): two cars in a 12x10 room leave no room for a bench; g10 rotates cars to stack three, leaving a 1-cell gap, reading like a parking lot. A small garage should hold 1-2 vehicles and the bench.
  - [medium] Wealth makes no visible difference: squatter and luxury garages look the same. Low: junk, barrels, tyres, oil stains; high/luxury: fewer cars with display lighting, clean bench, cabinets, EV chargers, wash drain.
  - [medium] The tool rack is placed at random: it sits in a corner or in the middle of the row (g10, tool rack and extinguisher jammed between the wall and cars). Wall equipment must sit on a free wall, extinguisher beside the personnel door.
  - [low] Missing details: floor drain, oil stains, wheel chocks, charging posts, rigger drone cradle/bench (Shadowrun), light switches, a hidden stash/locker for safehouse garages, bike rack.

## data_centre.yaml
### data_hall
- Status: done
- Blind design: rows of 8-12 racks (1x2 cells) in cold/hot-aisle pairs (cold aisle ~3 cells, hot ~2), containment doors and PDU/UPS cabinets at row ends, CRAC/CRAH units on the long walls facing the row ends, a 4-cell main aisle plus cross aisles in big halls, entry through a mantrap, NOC glass room in a corner, EPO + extinguishers + gas-suppression bottles at the exit, cable trays overhead; variants: cheap colo, cage colo, low-wealth backroom, luxury pods, matrix hub with a console.
- Findings (10 renders, 20x14 to 60x36, low to luxury):
  1. Rack rows are all identical: double rows, equal spacing, no alternation of cold and hot aisles, no containment, nothing at row ends. Real halls pair rows (face-to-face cold aisle, narrow, with doors/curtains at both ends) and separate pairs with a hot aisle; PDU/UPS/network cabinets must close the row ends.
  2. Rows have random holes and a short "orphan" row (single-depth, partly missing racks) in front by the door wall; looks like leftovers. Gaps in a row should be deliberate (cross aisle, pillar, CRAC) or filled; the front row is a tight strip that blocks the entry view and should be an aisle/staging zone instead.
  3. CRAC units are scattered on any wall, some jammed against row ends or with a rack row running into them, others facing a wall. They must sit in a row along one or two walls, blowing along the cold aisles/into the raised floor, with free front clearance and service access, evenly spaced, not 2 in a corner and 1 far away.
  4. Missing functional areas: no NOC/control window or desk, no mantrap/second door inside the hall, no crash cart/work bench, no cage/spare-parts rack, no cable trays, no UPS/battery cabinets, no gas-suppression bottles in the hall. At least a monitor station near the door and a staging/tool spot are expected.
  5. Only one door, in the middle of a long wall. A 30 m hall needs at least two exits (opposite ends), and the door should open into a main aisle that leads along the rows, not straight into a rack row end.
  6. Fire gear is sparse and random (extinguishers in corners, a red cabinet in odd places, one row of 3 extinguishers). They belong next to exits and at the main aisle, evenly spread; EPO button next to every door.
  7. Large halls leave empty bands (east edge, corners, central cross aisle that is sometimes 4 cells wide, sometimes absent). Aisle widths should be consistent: main aisle widest, rows' aisles uniform; fill wall margins with the CRACs, PDUs or cable risers.
  8. Wealth barely changes the look: low looks the same as luxury. Low should have mismatched/fewer racks, loose cables, a fan or single AC, clutter; luxury should have containment, biometric mantrap, glass NOC, pod layout.
  9. Rack rows run only horizontally; fine, but they should run perpendicular to the CRAC wall so the cooling reaches every aisle, and row length should be consistent (equal-length rows, last one not shorter).
  10. Nothing Shadowrun-specific: no host console / jackpoint, no glowing core, no ICE-looking hardware or guard post. A hall cabinet or terminal for decking in and a guard desk would sell the setting.
### mantrap
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x4 to 12x6 cells): small rectangle with an outer and an inner door on opposite walls (interlocked), a straight 2.5-3 m walkway between them, card/biometric reader and intercom at each door, ceiling camera in a corner covering both doors, emergency release; larger versions add a glazed guard booth in a corner with a window to both doors, a search bench, 4-6 personal lockers on a side wall, tray rack, metal detector/body scanner or turnstile at the inner door, floor weight mat, and Shadowrun flavour (gas vents, armoured doors, tazer). Generated 12 mantraps (10x8 to 24x16 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] The room has only ONE door (south wall) in every result and is a dead end. A mantrap is a vestibule with two interlocked doors: the second door must exist, on the opposite wall (or at least a different wall) so the path runs straight through it; and the doors must be a full room length apart, not side by side.
  - [high] 9 of 12 renders are completely empty (m1, m2, m4, m5, m7, m8, m10, m11 to m12 mostly): no cameras, no card readers or intercom, no scanner or turnstile, no booth, no lockers. A mantrap must always have at least cameras and door readers; luxury/high add a body scanner or metal detector gate, turnstile, guard booth with window, lockers and search bench. Currently it reads as an empty box.
  - [high] The only object that appears is a lone narrow bar (about 1x6 cells with a red end) standing free in the room, in the middle of a wall or sticking out of the wall, unrelated to the door (m3, m6, m7, m9, m12). It does not form a recognisable station: it should be a scanner/gate placed in line with the door path and the red end oriented at the door, or a booth/bench flush against a wall with a clear aisle in front. As placed it is random and in m9 it sits in the middle of the west wall, in m12 in a corner behind the door.
  - [high] The room is far too big: 16x10, 20x14, 24x16 cells (8x5 to 12x8 m) are a hall, not an airlock. A real mantrap is about 2x3 to 4x6 m (4x6 to 8x12 cells) and should be narrow and long (door-to-door path), not square (m8, m10, m12 are almost square and empty). Large sizes only make sense with a guard booth and search area filling them.
  - [medium] Door clearances: with a single door and no inner door, there is no controlled flow. Walkway between door and inner door must be kept free (straight 2-cell lane); equipment should sit to the sides of that lane (scanner and gate across it only where intended).
  - [medium] No cameras or sensors in the corners, no signage/status lights over the doors, no emergency release or call panel beside the doors, no floor weight mat - the cheap details that make a mantrap read as a mantrap.
  - [low] Wealth has no visible effect: low, middle, high and luxury look the same. Low: bare box, camera, reader; middle: plus lockers and bench; high/luxury: booth, scanner, turnstile, second camera, trap/gas vents (Shadowrun).
### noc
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (12x10 to 30x20, low to luxury). Common core: one big video wall on a short wall, 3-5 m from the first row; tiered rows of operator desks (2-3 monitors each) all facing the wall; ~2 cell aisles between rows and a side aisle; shift-lead desk at the back with view of wall and door; door at the back; glass wall to hall; whiteboard and break corner (coffee, fridge) near door; small KVM rack; wealthy variants add lounge, meeting/crisis table, plants; 24/7 variants add lockers, clocks, nap couch.
- Findings (rendered 10 NOCs, 14x12 to 30x20):
  1. There is no video wall: the room only has 1-2 small wall screens (2-3 cells wide) scattered on random walls (often the door wall or a side wall behind or beside the operators). A NOC must have one large, wide display wall (or a row of screens) across the whole short wall, which every desk faces.
  2. Desks must all face that wall in one common direction, in straight rows. Now desks face west/east or north and their facing often points at a bare wall; chairs sit beside the desk end instead of between desk and aisle.
  3. Operator seats should be one tight row/tier per wall-parallel line (desks side by side 3 cells apart, shared long benches), not isolated single desks with ~3 cell gaps; in the larger rooms the 2x2 desk blocks stand touching back to back with a wide empty strip between blocks and big empty margins at the far end.
  4. Missing the shift-lead / supervisor desk (raised or at the back, facing the wall, near the door) and any crisis/meeting table; every desk currently looks identical.
  5. Missing break/coffee corner, whiteboard/handover board, fridge/water; at most a tiny rack stub and a plant-like symbol in a corner. Add them near the door, out of the viewing axis.
  6. Missing KVM/patch rack or a few network racks along a side wall, and a printer; the room has no sign of being a technical control room besides the desks.
  7. No glass wall/window to the server hall or corridor, and the door lands in the middle of the back wall with no entry aisle structure; the door should lead into the aisle behind the last row, not into a desk gap.
  8. Desk count should scale with the room (about 1 desk per 3 cells of width per row, 2-4 rows tiered), and the whole room should be filled with a plan, not leave one third empty (e.g. 26x14 and 24x16 have empty right/left thirds).
  9. Wealth barely changes anything: luxury/high should add lounge seating, espresso bar, extra screens; low should show a single TV and mismatched desks.
### cooling_plant
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 plants (12x10 to 36x16 cells): chillers in ONE row along a long wall with a 3-4 cell service aisle in front, a pump row (duty/standby pairs) parallel in front of them, header/manifold pipes between, buffer tanks at one end, BMS/MCC panels next to the door, floor drains in the aisle, N+1 chillers, fire extinguisher/eyewash at the door, second exit when big.
- Findings (10+ renders, 17-48 cells wide):
  1. No plant logic: chillers and pumps are scattered on all four walls (and some in the middle of walls) with random gaps. They must form a single aligned row (or two facing rows with a central aisle), evenly spaced, same orientation, all fans/faces toward the aisle.
  2. Pumps (small amber-lit units) are placed alone against random walls, far from any chiller. They must sit as duty/standby pairs directly in front of or beside their chiller group, in line with the row.
  3. Nothing connects the equipment: no header pipes, manifolds, valves or CRAH feed lines. A chilled-water plant needs visible piping runs along the chiller row (supply and return), floor trays/pipe racks, and risers heading to the data halls.
  4. Large empty floor in most rooms (the bigger the emptier, e.g. 30x14 has 5 units and a void). Counts do not scale with size: add more chillers (N+1) and pumps, buffer tanks, expansion vessels, air separators, dosing/glycol station, spare parts rack.
  5. Missing buffer/storage tanks entirely; the thermal buffer tank is the most recognisable piece besides chillers.
  6. Electrical: panels are tiny and lonely, and not grouped. Need an MCC/VFD cabinet bank on one wall with front clearance, plus a BMS control desk or panel near the door.
  7. Door relation: a chiller or pump often stands right by the door (jamming the entrance); the door should open onto a clear aisle leading along the row, with the control panel and extinguisher beside it.
  8. Missing safety/maintenance details: floor drains and sump, eyewash/shower, hose, leak marks, warning striping on walkways, service clearance in front of chillers.
  9. Equipment sits oddly: units have different orientations and some are wedged in corners with the extinguisher/odd items; keep corners tidy and clearances consistent. Only one door even in 40+ cell wide plants; large plants need a second exit at the far end.
### ups_room
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 sketches, 12x10 to 30x16, squatter to luxury): UPS modules as one row of 1-3 large cabinets with a bypass/paralleling panel beside them; battery cabinets as separate strings (front-to-front pairs with a shared aisle, or along a wall) clearly distinct from the UPS; distribution board and ATS near the door; eyewash, spill kit, extinguisher and EPO button by the door; hydrogen/smoke detector and exhaust vent; wealth shows as modular racks/lithium/gas suppression (rich) vs. mismatched shelf batteries and one old UPS (poor).
- Findings (rendered 10 rooms, 12x12 to 30x16, all wealth levels, seeds 1-10):
  1. The room is a field of identical cabinets (always 3-6 rows of 4-ish segments, green strips) with no UPS modules. A UPS room must contain two clearly different groups: a few big UPS/inverter cabinets plus bypass/static-switch panel in ONE row, and battery cabinets as the bulk. Currently it reads as a generic server hall or battery wall only.
  2. All rows face the same direction and every row has the same spacing, so aisles are all back-to-face. Battery strings should be arranged front-to-front (pairs with a shared service aisle) or against walls, with the narrow back-to-back gaps between pairs; rows should not all be uniform stripes.
  3. Rows are pushed to the middle with wasted floor on both sides, and a vertical gap splits the room into two blocks with no purpose. Equipment should stand along walls or in deliberate blocks aligned to the room; leftover space should be a clear walkway, not a ragged margin.
  4. Quantity does not scale sensibly: large rooms just get more rows (up to 24-32 cabinets, a 30x16 room is packed), small rooms look the same. Counts should follow a plausible ratio (a UPS block per battery string group), and the biggest rooms should split into A/B sides with separate panels rather than uniform repetition.
  5. Wealth/style variation is almost absent. Poor rooms should show a single old UPS with stacked/mismatched batteries on shelves, patched panels; rich rooms modular racks, gas-suppression bottles, monitoring console. Presently low and luxury look identical.
  6. Distribution boards are scattered randomly (north wall, east wall, west wall, corner) in a count of 1-3. They should cluster by the UPS group and/or the door, with working clearance in front, and be on the wall the UPS row's output side points to.
  7. Door-area safety details are thin: eyewash/spill kit and extinguisher should sit visibly next to the door; EPO button beside the door inside. Only a red extinguisher/alarm marker in a far corner appears; the eyewash appears rarely. Hydrogen/ventilation (vent unit or fan on a wall, detector) and a small maintenance table/laptop/terminal spot are missing.
  8. A distribution board sometimes sits right in front of the door in the first aisle, narrowing the entry; the entry zone must stay free.
  9. Label text overlaps cabinets in the middle (cosmetic, with --labels only).
### suppression_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: cylinders upright in 1-2 strapped rows against a wall (6-14 per bank, banks per protected zone), manifold/header pipe above them, release panel + abort/manual release beside the door, warning signs at the door, 1 m service aisle in front for weighing/exchange, spare cylinders, workbench/scale, extinguisher, vent grille; rich variants add a mirrored backup bank and per-zone selector valves; slum variants loose cylinders and a cart.
- Findings (10 renders, 10x10 to 20x12, low to luxury; all nearly identical):
  1. Room is almost empty: only red cylinder racks exist. A real suppression room needs a release/control panel by the door (with abort/manual release), a manifold/discharge pipe run along the cylinder tops leading to a wall pass-through, and warning signs; none are visible (only a tiny triangle at the door corner).
  2. Huge empty floor: 12x16 or 14x14 rooms have one wall strip of cylinders and 80% bare floor. Either the room should be small/tight or it needs service content: weighing scale/service bay in front of the racks, workbench, cabinet with spare parts and spare cylinders, hose/extinguisher, hoist rail marker. Larger/wealthier rooms should add a second (backup) bank, not just more empty space.
  3. Racks flank the door wall: banks placed on the door wall right beside the door jamb (e.g. seeds with racks both sides of the door) crowd the entrance and put cylinders where the door swings; keep the door side clear and put the banks on the far/side walls, with an aisle of at least 1 m (2 cells) in front.
  4. Layout is always a single thin line in groups of 5 with small gaps; vary: double rows back to back, an L or U along two walls, a central island with walk-around, matching the room proportions. Banks should be centred or tidy against one wall, not bunched in a corner leaving the far end empty.
  5. Cylinder banks stay in the same number (3-6 groups) regardless of room size and wealth; count should scale with the protected volume and be even/mirrored for a backup bank.
  6. Needs a ventilation grille/extractor on an exterior wall and a floor drain; no other furniture (desks, chairs) should appear, and a lockable door with signage (security) is expected.
### meet_me_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the room is almost empty: fill it as a carrier room with rows of racks/frames and cages instead of one wall strip and a vast void; (2) the cross-connect equipment must be arranged in rows with aisles, in the middle of the room, not only on one wall; (3) add the fibre entrance and carrier cages; (4) the door area needs a bench, EPO/fire panel and camera in a sensible place.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x8 to 28x14 cells, dive to luxury): thin long room, carrier racks in rows (back-to-back, hot aisle between) with 3-cell aisles from the door; MMR cross-connect frames (ODF) in a central row; 2-4 carrier cages along walls, each with own lock and card/biometric reader; fibre entrance ducts on an outer wall opposite the door with overhead trays leading to the racks; workbench with OTDR/tester near the door; cameras over the door and at aisle ends; EPO button and gas-suppression/fire panel at the door; UPS/AC rack at an end; luxury: glass cages, biometrics, wall status screen, drone dock; Shadowrun: jackpoint console, shielded cabinet; dive: two racks, a chair and a laptop. Generated 10 rooms (12x10 to 30x10 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Far too empty: in nearly every result (m1-m6, m8) the only equipment is a single row of racks along the north wall (4-14 units, always the whole wall), a short bench by the door and 2 small corner icons; 80-90% of the floor is a blank void. A 24x14 or 28x18 cell room looks like an unfurnished hall. A meet-me room is dense: multiple rows of frames/racks across the room with 3-cell aisles between, filling the area proportionally to its size.
  - [high] Only one arrangement for all sizes and wealth levels: always one rack row at the north wall, regardless of room size. Bigger rooms need more rows (two back-to-back rows with a hot aisle, perpendicular rows), not a longer single row; the rack row also runs flush from corner to corner while the door end remains open.
  - [high] Missing the defining objects of a meet-me room: no fibre entrance/duct points on an outer wall, no cross-connect/ODF patch frames in the middle, no carrier cages with locks. Add the entrance (2-4 ducts, on the wall opposite or beside the door) and cages for each carrier, along a wall, with the gate opening to the aisle.
  - [medium] Racks must face an aisle, not all stand with their backs to the wall at the same spot: rows in the middle should face each other (cold aisle) and be accessible from both sides (0.9 m behind); there is no overhead cable tray or visible fibre route from the entrance to the racks.
  - [medium] The workbench is a small block with stripes next to the door, sometimes in the wall of a different side (m5, m6: on the west wall in the middle, far from the door). The bench belongs near the door or at a short end, with a stool/chair, tester and fibre spool shelves; at the moment it looks like a stray object. Missing a chair.
  - [medium] Small corner icons (camera/alarm, fire/EPO) are tiny and stuck in far corners: the EPO and fire panel belong next to the door, cameras over the door and at aisle ends; none cover the middle.
  - [low] Wealth hardly changes the room: luxury (m4, m10) looks like the low one apart from room size; add per wealth: biometric readers on cages, a wall status screen, drone dock, a second camera; dive: cable heap, a chair and laptop. Shadowrun flavour (jackpoint console, shielded cabinet, rigger/drone dock) absent.
  - [low] Door always at the south wall, and the whole equipment on the opposite side; vary the door and orient the aisle along the long axis in thin rooms (m5, m6 24x8 cells and 30x10 cells have rack rows only at the far wall, 7 m from the door).
### loading_dock
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the room must read as a dock: a row of vehicle bays (big roll-up doors with dock levellers/bumpers) along one exterior wall, regularly spaced; (2) a clear working strip along that wall and a marked walkway to the inner door; (3) staging as ordered pallet rows, not scattered crates; (4) a dock office/guard post, scanning and security (Shadowrun); (5) small details (bollards, bins, signals, cameras).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x10 to 30x14 cells, low to luxury): 1-4 bays of ~7 cells width on 9-cell centres along the long wall, leveller+bumpers+chock+traffic light per bay, a pallet jack parked at each bay, 3 m clear aisle behind the bays, pallets stacked in tidy rows in a staging strip against the opposite/inner wall, guard booth or dock office in the corner at the personnel door with a window onto the bays, parcel scan station + scale, wide inner door/freight lift to the staging room, bollards at jambs and corners, cardboard bin/compactor, extinguisher, cameras; high/luxury adds an airlock vestibule, X-ray or drone scanner, quarantine cage, interlocked doors, two armed guards; low is gritty with a van inside and haphazard (but never walkway-blocking) pallets. Generated 10 docks (12x10 to 36x16 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The room is almost empty and has no dock identity: only 4-9 identical crates, at most one or two yellow forklift/pallet-truck blocks, and one fire extinguisher in a corner. Nothing shows a truck bay: no roll-up doors on the loading wall, no dock levellers, bumpers, wheel chocks, signal lights. The long wall segments are plain windows/wall panels (the cyan strips look like glass) rather than big vehicle doors. A loading dock needs 1-4 clearly marked vehicle bays (more bays on bigger docks, evenly spaced, same wall), each with a leveller and a pallet jack beside it.
  - [high] Crates are scattered randomly across the floor like loot, in the middle of the room (ld1, ld5, ld10 each have crates floating in open space, ld7 has crates only in odd places). Real staging is a staging zone: pallets in neat rows/blocks with aisles, parallel to the loading wall or the inner door, with a 1.5-3 m clear lane between the bays and the pallet rows. Crates should not stand directly in front of a bay, the inner door or the personnel door; keep a free corridor from every bay to the inner door.
  - [high] Pallets and crates are pushed up flush against walls and each other without regard to the doors: crates butt against the east wall (ld5, ld8, ld10), against the north windows (ld3, ld9) or touch each other in an arbitrary pair (ld8). Pallet stacks must be aligned to each other (same orientation, regular gaps) and either free-standing in a row or against a blank wall, not in the bay line.
  - [high] Large dock rooms (24x12 and up: ld4, ld9, ld5) are huge empty halls with 4-6 objects, mostly one forklift. A bigger dock should get more bays, more staging rows, a scanning/sorting area, a dock office and a vehicle lane marking, not just empty floor. Object count has to scale with area (about one pallet position per 4-6 m2 of staging area plus fixed dock equipment).
  - [medium] Missing dock office / guard booth: a real data-centre dock has a booth or desk next to the personnel door with a window onto the bays (guard, log book, visitor badge printer). In Shadowrun add an armed guard post, a metal/drone scanner gate before the inner door, a parcel scan station and a scale with a table, cameras covering each bay and the inner door.
  - [medium] The forklift/pallet truck (yellow block with two circles) is parked in open floor in the middle of the room (ld3, ld6, ld9) or in a corner against crates (ld7); it should be parked at a bay or at a charging spot at the wall, with its forks toward the aisle. Bigger docks need only 1-2 pieces of equipment, but they must have a parking place plus a charging station and a hand-truck/pallet-jack rack.
  - [medium] Door relations: the personnel door sits in the south-west corner and a double door in the north-west corner, both at the same side as pallets, with nothing indicating which one is the inner door to the staging room. The inner door should be wide (2+ m), on the wall opposite the bays or at the end of the dock, with a free lane; the personnel door needs an office/booth beside it. Add bollards at the jambs of the vehicle doors and in corners.
  - [low] Waste: no cardboard/skip bin or compactor, no pallet stack for empties, no spill kit, no signage or floor markings (hazard stripes along the bay line, walkway lines). One red extinguisher is fine but each bay wall should have one, and a first-aid cabinet by the office.
  - [low] Style differences by wealth are not visible: low and luxury look the same except for count. Add gritty extras (graffiti, parked van inside, rubbish) for low and airlock/security hardware for high/luxury.
### staging_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (10x10 to 30x16 cells, low to luxury). Common scheme: wide door near the dock side; unpack/receiving zone right behind the door (pallets, cardboard and waste bins, pallet-jack lane 1.8 m); ESD workbenches in parallel rows or back-to-back islands mid-room, each with monitors, label printer, stool; burn-in racks as one continuous row along the far wall with a 1.2 m aisle in front; spare-parts shelving along a side wall; tool wall, crash cart, fire extinguisher; low wealth = plain tables and cardboard clutter, luxury = tidy ESD floor, glass racks, gowning bench.
- Findings (11 renders, 10x10 to 30x16, all wealth levels):
  1. Workbenches are scattered one per wall at random spots (corners, mid-walls, different orientations) instead of forming a work line. Real build rooms put benches in 1-2 parallel rows or back-to-back islands, same orientation, with a clear aisle between rows, and the stool/chair side facing the aisle.
  2. Pallets/crates float in the room centre or in far corners, unrelated to the door. The unpacking/receiving area must sit directly behind the door (pallets stacked together, waste/cardboard bins beside them), with a clear straight lane from door to the benches for a pallet jack.
  3. Racks/shelves are only tiny 1x4 units tucked into corners and along walls; there is no real burn-in rack bank. Racks must stand in a continuous row (several adjacent units) with an aisle in front, and spares shelving should be a run along one wall, not single pieces in four places.
  4. Large rooms (20x12 and up, esp. 26x18, 30x16) are mostly empty floor with 3-5 isolated objects; equipment count must scale with area (more benches, a rack row, more shelving, more pallets) or the room should read as a different, smaller space.
  5. Missing details of a build room: monitors/label printer/power strips on benches, tool wall, crash cart/trolley, cable reels, parts cage, whiteboard/inventory board, trash/recycling bins, fire extinguisher. Only a single round bin per bench is shown.
  6. Wealth has no visible effect: low and luxury look identical. Low should look improvised (plain tables, piled cardboard, clutter), high/luxury tidy with ESD mats, tool wall, glass-front racks.
  7. Objects are sometimes jammed against walls/corners in odd places (shelf blocking a bench end, pallet next to a shelf front, shelf in front of nothing) so shelf fronts lack walking room; every shelf/rack front needs a free aisle.
  8. Small rooms (10x10, 12x10) hold two pallets and one bench: the pallets dominate and leave no work space; prefer one pallet and a second bench.

## hospitality.yaml
### hotel_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (3x4 m sleeve capsule to 7x10 m luxury suite). Common pattern: short entry hall with bathroom on one side and wardrobe/luggage rack on the other, bed with headboard on a solid wall flanked by two nightstands, 0.6 m+ walkway each side, TV/desk wall opposite the foot of the bed, desk or small table + chair at the window, bath with WC/basin/shower or tub, minibar and safe on mid/high, sofa group and second bathroom on suites.
- Findings (10 renders, labelled guest room / hallway / bathroom / closet, wealth squatter to luxury, 10x12 to 18x22 cells):
  1. The hallway is far too big: it runs the full width of the room as a wide corridor and eats about a third of the unit, while the sleeping room gets one bed and little else. A real hotel entry is a short, narrow passage (about 1.2-1.5 m) with bath and wardrobe beside it, opening straight into the room; no full-width inner corridor with its own wall.
  2. Bathrooms are poorly equipped and arranged: often WC plus tub only, no basin (or basin missing/hidden), no shower in the small ones; fixtures are scattered (tub in one corner, WC against a window wall) instead of a compact wet cell. Every bathroom needs WC + basin + shower or tub, basin visible near the door, WC not facing the door, no windows behind the WC in cheap rooms.
  3. Closets are oversized rooms (almost as big as the bathroom, sometimes two of them) holding only a few shelf cells. A hotel wardrobe is a 0.6 m deep niche/cabinet in the entry hall; luxury may have one walk-in, never two empty closets.
  4. Sleeping room is under-furnished: mostly bed + nightstands + one armchair. Missing: desk with chair (at window or on TV wall), luggage rack/bench at the bed foot, wardrobe in the room, minibar/fridge, safe. The empty floor between bed and hallway wall in large rooms (12x18 and bigger) looks vacant; add a seating/work group or let the bed move to the room centre of a wall.
  5. A lone armchair floats in the middle of the room (sometimes facing nothing, back to the bed) and looks wrong. Armchairs must be paired with a small table in a corner/at the window and face the room or the TV, never float mid-room.
  6. Large dressers/sideboards stand in the hallway or against the hallway wall in the walkway, narrowing the passage and looking like misplaced furniture; round pot/plant items sit in door swing spots. Keep hallways free; furniture belongs in the sleeping room.
  7. Door swing conflicts: the room door swings into the sleeping room onto nightstands/bed corner/dresser (e.g. bed foot directly at the door arc, nightstand under the door). Keep the door arc and 0.9 m in front of the door clear, bed not within reach of the door.
  8. Nightstands are inconsistent: sometimes only one, or one is moved away from the bed, or the second sits beside a TV unit. A double bed needs one on each side flush with the headboard; a single bed one.
  9. TV placement is erratic (on side wall beside the door, next to the bed head, on the hallway wall at the bed side). It must be on the wall facing the foot of the bed (or above the desk/dresser), centred.
  10. All rooms get the same single king bed regardless of size/wealth: no twin beds, no single in cheap rooms, no family variant, no suite split (bedroom + living area). Vary bed type with size and wealth; cheap sleeve rooms should be tiny and bare, luxury should add sofa group, bar, bigger bath.
  11. Window handling: bed headboards sit against window walls with a window beside or behind them while the desk/seating has no window; the window wall should hold desk/table and curtain, headboard on the solid wall. Some windows are in the bath/closet corner while the main room has few.
### guest_room
- Status: done
- Blind design summary: 10 sketches from 5x7 cells budget to 12x14 luxury. Constant pattern: bed headboard on a solid wall, one nightstand each side (symmetric), 1-cell walkway on at least one bed side, TV/dresser on the wall facing the foot of the bed, desk+chair at window or beside TV, wardrobe/luggage rack in the entrance vestibule, armchair+side table in a window corner (mid/high), luxury adds sofa/coffee table, bench at bed foot, minibar/safe; cheap/sleeve: bunk or single bed, locker, small TV, nothing else. Twin variant for mid, family variant with second bed.
- Findings (12 renders, wealth squatter..luxury, 10x10 to 20x14 cells):
  1. Rooms are far too empty. Beyond 12x12 cells the bed, 2 nightstands and 1-2 small items float in a huge void; even luxury has no more than middle. Larger/richer rooms must gain furniture (sofa or two armchairs with a table, desk, dresser, wardrobe, bench, rug) or the room must be split (entry vestibule + bathroom/dressing zone) instead of being one hall.
  2. Missing core items: no wardrobe/closet and no luggage rack by the door, no bathroom/WC or at least an en-suite zone, no minibar/fridge or safe. A hotel room without wardrobe and bathroom reads as a bare box.
  3. Nothing is related to the door: the door sits in a corner, the entry has no vestibule, and a lone small box in the entry corner is the only thing there. The wardrobe/bathroom belong in the entry zone, the bed should sit away from the door line, and there needs to be a clear walkway from door to window.
  4. Bed placement is inconsistent: often pushed into a corner with its side tight against the side wall (one side with no walkway, so one person cannot get out), and the headboard sometimes faces a wall, sometimes is turned sideways. The bed should be headboard-against-wall, centred on the wall (king/double) with access on both sides and nightstands on both sides; corner placement only for cheap/single beds.
  5. Nightstands are unpaired in several rooms (one bed side with two small items, the other with none; g1) or are squeezed to the wall corner next to the bed. Always one per side, symmetric, flush with the headboard.
  6. TV does not face the bed: it appears in far corners, at the window wall or beside the door. It must be on the wall opposite the foot of the bed (on dresser/desk), centred on the bed axis.
  7. Desk and chair are placed arbitrarily (desk end against the side wall with the chair away from it, desk in the middle of a long wall far from anything).
