Desk should be at the window or next to the TV wall, chair tucked in, and not duplicated; two desks in one room (g9) is wrong for a guest room.
  8. Armchairs sit in random corners, facing the wall and without a side table or window; an armchair needs a small table beside it and should be turned into the room, near the window.
  9. Many unidentifiable small grey boxes scattered on walls and in corners (bins/stools?) along the walls with no function; they look like clutter. Each small item should have a clear purpose and neighbour (lamp by chair, bin by desk).
  10. Windows are present on every wall of the test (artifact of the isolated room) but the window glow, i.e. the blue lit rectangles, are inside the room as TV; make sure TV and windows are visually distinct, and curtains/window seat are missing.
### capsule_dorm
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 sketches, scratchpad capsule_dorm_blind.md): Tokyo-style hall of 2-tier pods (1x2 m) in back-to-back rows, open ends to a continuous 1.2-1.5 m aisle, ladder per stack, a locker bank by the entrance (one per pod), shoe rack, vending, washroom access, exit signs and fire extinguisher at the far end, vent/night light; variants: women-only, luxury, 3-tier cheap, corp sleep station, mini 4-pod.
- Findings (12 renders, low wealth, 14x12 to 36x24 cells):
  1. The room contains nothing but pods. A real capsule dorm needs lockers (roughly one per pod, in a bank near the entrance or at the foot of each stack/along the wall), a shoe rack/cubbies at the door, and luggage space; the blank walls and floor look unfinished.
  2. Missing access details: ladders or steps per pod stack, curtains/shutters on the pod openings, and small items (vending machine, bin, bench, extinguisher, exit sign, vent) so the dorm reads as lived in.
  3. Pods must be arranged as ordered stacks in rows with the open ends all facing a shared aisle, and every row needs an aisle on its open side. Some rows (single-row blocks, the 2x6 block in the narrow tall room) leave the open side facing a wall gap or far-away aisle, and single rows beside double blocks look inconsistent.
  4. The layout is a generic grid of identical blocks with a huge empty cross aisle (about 4-5 cells wide); aisles are far wider than real ones while the pods are tightly packed. Blocks should be better balanced: either fill space with more rows or put in the missing furniture, not empty floor.
  5. Pod rows in several rooms are jammed flush against the west wall and cut off at the wall line (pods visibly clipped at the room edge), while the east side keeps a gap. Rows must be symmetric and sit clear of the wall (or intentionally against it with complete pods).
  6. Block sizes vary arbitrarily (6, 4, 3, 2 pods per row, and a lone 2-pod stub in one render); partial blocks look like leftovers. Prefer uniform rows or balanced remainders, and end rows should have an aisle or end-of-row access.
  7. Entrance: a wide double door opens straight onto the pod field with no entry zone (lockers, shoe rack, reception counter or check-in). The door should lead into a small lobby/vestibule area, not directly face the last pod row.
  8. Vertical orientation (narrow tall room): pods in a long column pair face each other across a 0-cell gap or appear back to back with no aisle; the dorm should keep the open ends toward a clear aisle, which here is not visible. The block is also shifted off-centre.
  9. No second exit, washroom door or link to showers/locker room: a 36x24 hall with 30+ pods has a single exit; large dorms need a second escape route and a washroom connection.
  10. The label "capsule dorm" collides with the pods in several renders (label placement only).
### shower_room
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 variants, hostel/capsule/gym/spa/barracks/pool): dry vestibule behind the door (benches, hooks, towel shelf, basins+mirrors), wet zone behind it with heads in ONE row (or two rows facing a 2-3 cell aisle) at even spacing ~1.5 cells, curtained/partitioned cubicles in nicer places, drain per head or gutter, door not looking straight at the heads.
- Findings (10 renders, 8x6 to 18x10, squatter..luxury):
  1. Heads are scattered randomly on all four walls (corners, single ones on opposite walls, uneven gaps). They must be arranged in one tidy row (or two facing rows) along a wall at regular spacing, with a clear aisle in front.
  2. The room contains ONLY shower heads and 1-2 basins; it reads as an empty tiled box. Needs benches, towel hooks/shelf, and a drain/gutter at each head or along the row; a changing/dry zone is missing entirely.
  3. No dry/wet separation: the door opens straight into the wet area, heads sit right next to the door (some in the door-side corner) with no privacy. Need a dry entry zone (basins, benches, hooks, towels) next to the door and the heads in the far part; heads must not be visible/hit from the door line.
  4. Huge empty centre in larger rooms (12x12, 14x10, 18x10): count does not scale with area (3-5 heads in 100-180 cells). Larger rooms need many more heads (6-10), a second row, or benches/changing area in the middle.
  5. Basins are a few tiny items stuck against the door wall, and one is jammed beside the door swing; they should be a proper row with mirror on a wall, away from the door swing.
  6. No cubicle separation: no partitions or curtains between heads, so heads feel like single tubs in space; at least middle/high wealth should have partitions/curtain cubicles, squatter open heads.
  7. Wealth barely changes anything; low = bare heads, high = cubicles with bench and hook, luxury = vanity, towel shelf, wider spacing.
### locker_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: lockers as contiguous banks along walls or back-to-back islands (single-bank for small rooms, 2-3 islands for gyms), a bench parallel in front of every bank with a 2-cell aisle, door opening onto an aisle with a privacy screen, basins plus mirrors in one group on a wall, hooks/boot rack/bins by the door, a drain only in the wet zone. Sketches in scratchpad locker_room_blind.md.
- Findings (10 renders, 10x8 to 30x16, all wealth levels):
  1. The room reads as an empty hall with sparse single-cell boxes scattered round the perimeter. Lockers must form contiguous banks (touching, 1 to 2 cells wide each, 6 to 12 in a row), not isolated units with random gaps of 1 to 3 cells. This is the biggest realism problem.
  2. Benches are missing (only one tiny bench in one render). Every locker bank needs a long bench parallel to it, one cell deep, directly in front with the usual aisle left beyond it. A locker room without benches is the clearest tell.
  3. Large rooms (20x12 and up) leave the whole middle empty. Fill with back-to-back locker islands with benches on both sides, or a central bench row; keep aisles of about 2 cells and keep sightlines to the door interrupted by a screen or locker end.
  4. Number of lockers is far too low for the floor area; a locker room should be dominated by lockers (roughly one per person that the room can hold). The tiny items placed also vary in size and spacing without logic.
  5. Items in corners and at door sides look random: a unit placed right beside the door jamb or in a corner next to another unit with a gap. Keep door swing and the first cells inside the door clear, and put a bin, boot rack or coat hooks there instead.
  6. Missing washing/grooming details: a group of basins with mirrors on one wall, plus a drain/wet-zone floor; at least one mirror (wealthier rooms: vanity, towel shelf, plant, rug). Wealth makes almost no visible difference.
  7. Missing hygiene/utility items: laundry/towel bin, waste bin, hooks along a wall, first-aid or notice board, privacy baffle behind the door. Toilets and showers should be adjacent rather than inside, in the shower_room.
  8. Lockers and fixtures face their wall consistently, but orientation is invisible: doors/fronts should visibly face the room (and benches), never a wall.
### linen_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) shelving must be continuous runs on the walls, sorted by linen type, not scattered 3-4 cell stubs; (2) the room must contain the housekeeping kit (carts, bins, sink, ironing/folding), not just shelves; (3) free-standing items must be parked in purposeful spots, not floating mid-room; (4) rooms must not be mostly empty; (5) wealth must change the content.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x2 m closet to 10x6 m luxury laundry): open steel shelving along 2-3 walls in continuous runs, 0.5 m deep, one linen type per bay; 1-3 housekeeping carts parked near the door with open fronts; separate clean/soiled laundry bins (under a chute if there is one); sink or mop bucket; folding table in the middle of large rooms; ironing board/press and washers+dryers in a row on a plumbing wall for luxury; small desk with roster/terminal and key safe; trash bin; camera/keycard lock for Shadowrun flavour; a flophouse version with just wire racks, a mop bucket and a pile of bags. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 24x16 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Rooms are far too empty: 14x10 to 24x16 cell rooms (7x5 to 12x8 m) hold only 6-10 small shelf stubs at the walls and 1-3 crossed boxes; the middle is a huge void (l5, l7: 12x8 m with 8 stubs and 2-3 boxes). A real linen room is dense: shelving fills most of the wall length, plus carts, bins and a table. Either fill more or (better) make big rooms read as a laundry/housekeeping hall with the stations below.
  - [high] Shelving is fragmented: 4-cell and 3-cell shelf pieces stand with gaps between them, in corners and on random walls, some on the door wall right beside the door, so no wall reads as a continuous shelf run (l1, l2, l3, l9). Shelves must form long contiguous runs along whole walls (all bays touching), especially the walls opposite and beside the door, with the aisle in front kept clear. Stubs on the door wall at both sides of the opening are fine only as a short run, not a lone piece.
  - [high] The only non-shelf object is a crossed-out box (cart/table/bin, unclear) floating mid-room at random places and orientations (l2 near the corner, l5 two boxes in the centre, l7 three boxes in a row near the door but not aligned to anything). Carts must be parked in line along a wall or next to the door, parallel to each other and with the open end towards the room; a folding table must be in the centre with free walking space around it; bins must stand against a wall or under a chute, never in the middle of the floor.
  - [high] Missing station content: no sink/mop bucket, no laundry bins (clean/soiled), no washer/dryer, no ironing board or press, no desk/roster, no trash bin, no chute hatch, no key safe/camera. A housekeeping room should have at least a sink (plumbing wall), 2 separate bins, a cart or two, a small work surface, and a trash bin; larger/luxury rooms a washer+dryer row, ironing and a desk.
  - [medium] Aisle logic: nothing guides a route from the door; boxes sit in the line of the door in some seeds (l9: box 3 cells in front of the door, with shelving beside the door), narrowing the entry. Keep a clear 2-3 cell strip straight from the door to the back wall; place carts beside the door, not in front.
  - [medium] Wealth and size do not change the character: low and luxury rooms look the same, only larger and emptier. Low: few racks, bags on the floor, mop bucket; high/luxury: more shelving, carts, ironing, washers, desk, camera. A big room should be split (storage side vs. laundry/work side), not stretched.
  - [medium] Shelf orientation/stacking: shelves on the long walls sit as separate 1x4 blocks with variable length and facing; keep all shelves facing into the room, same depth and same length, in a regular rhythm so it reads as one shelving system.
  - [low] Sprinkled markers (a small red triangle in a corner, likely a panel/extinguisher) are fine; add a camera and keycard reader at the door for corp hotels.
### restaurant
- Status: done
- Blind design summary: 10 sketches (noodle bar, ramen counter, diner, family 2/4/6 mix, corp high-end with round tables, sushi bar, hotel view restaurant, bistro, banquet hall, seedy bar-restaurant). Common: host stand + coat area at the door, mixed 2/4/6-tops, booths/banquettes along walls, window tables, bar with stools, service station and kitchen pass near the kitchen door with a clear wait-staff lane, restroom corridor at the back, aisles of 3+ cells, wealth changes table shape/spacing/decor.
- Findings (10 renders, 16x12 to 40x26, low to luxury, all look the same apart from table count):
  1. Only identical square 4-top tables with 2 chairs on each of two sides, in a rigid grid, regardless of size or wealth. Needs a mix of 2-, 4- and 6-tops (and round tables for high/luxury), and not everything in perfect rows.
  2. Nothing along the walls: all walls are empty, all tables float mid-room. Needs booths/banquettes along walls and windows, window tables, with tables set only a chair-width from the wall.
  3. No entrance furniture: needs a host/reception stand and coat check next to the door, plus a waiting area; the door opens into empty floor.
  4. No service logic: needs a service/waiter station and a kitchen pass or kitchen door in the dining room, with a clear lane to it from every table; the kitchen side is not visible at all (a lone counter/bar appears only occasionally, in some renders at a wall with no stools).
  5. The occasional counter (bar) has no stools, no back shelf and does not feel like a bar; a restaurant bar needs stools along it and bottles behind.
  6. Missing details: no plants, pillars/partitions, decor, wine cabinet, menu board, trash, stage/music for upscale; luxury looks identical to low. Wealth must show in table shape, spacing, lighting, private booths.
  7. Layout bias: tables clump in the left 2/3 with an unused empty strip on the right/top and a wide empty band at the entrance side; the grid should fill the room evenly with wall-side seating, and aisles should be consistent (a bigger gap splits blocks arbitrarily, ex. one gap in the middle of rows).
  8. Large rooms (36x24, 40x26) are a monotonous field of 30-40 identical tables; they need zones (bar/lounge, booths area, private/banquet area, main hall) and a central main aisle from door to kitchen.
  9. Small rooms (16x12): only 4 tables in the centre with a lone corner counter; should use perimeter seating or a counter with stools (noodle bar) instead.
  10. The extra staff/service door and restrooms access are not hinted anywhere in the room.
### commercial_kitchen
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a cooking line, not scattered stoves: all burners, grill and fryer in one row against a wall (or a back-to-back island) under one hood, with a 1-1.2 m cook aisle and a pass/serving counter opposite; (2) work-flow zones: delivery door -> cold/dry storage -> prep -> cook -> pass, dish pit on the return side; (3) enough prep tables and islands in the centre; (4) hygiene: handwash by the door, sinks grouped; (5) fix the sparse, ring-only layout on large rooms.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x8 to 30x20 cells, squatter diner to luxury banquet/corp): hot line (range, grill, fryer, oven) in a row on one wall under a hood, central prep island(s) 1.2 m from the line, pass window on the dining wall with a heat lamp, dish pit (3-bay sink, racks, tray return) at the dirty-return side away from the clean pass, walk-in cold room and dry-store shelves next to the delivery door, handwash basin at each entrance, bins by prep and dish pit, trolleys; luxury adds blast chiller, pastry corner, double hood, conveyor dishwasher; Shadowrun flavour: salvaged range, soy vats (low), robot arm or dish drone (corp). Generated 12 kitchens via hotel --room (12x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] Everything is pushed against the walls as a perimeter ring (all 12 renders); the middle is empty, no island at all in most (at most one lone prep table in the centre, 1-2 of 12 rooms). A kitchen needs central prep islands/work tables in rows parallel to the cook line with 2-cell aisles; 20x14 cells and larger look like an empty hall with objects stuck on walls. Counts do not scale with area (30x20 cells has the same ~15 objects as 16x12).
  - [high] No cooking line: stoves (the pairs of burner rings) are scattered one by one on random walls and corners (k3 four stoves on four different walls, k6, k9) and not grouped in a row, with no hood, grill, fryer or oven recognisable. All heat appliances must stand together in one row on one wall (or one island) with a work aisle in front and prep/pass behind; at most a separate oven/bakery corner.
  - [high] No pass/service window or serving counter, so the kitchen has no relation to the dining room. Add a pass counter (long table with heat lamps) in a wall facing the restaurant, with the door/opening beside it; plus a separate dirty-return opening next to the dish pit.
  - [high] No dish pit: sinks are single basins spread over walls (k3: three single sinks on three walls, k9, k11: sinks at opposite walls), without drainboards, racks or dishwasher. Put a dish-wash zone (long sink run or three-bay sink with dishwasher and rack shelves) in one corner near the return opening, away from the cook line and clean pass; keep one prep sink and one handwash basin separate.
  - [high] No cold storage/dry store: the room has no walk-in, only a few small shelves; grey cabinet/shelf blocks (the wide boxes) sit at random walls. A walk-in cold room plus shelf rack row should be a block next to the delivery/entry door (stock enters, goes to storage), and reach-in fridges belong at the line and prep.
  - [medium] Entry door area: one double door bottom wall; objects (shelf blocks, bins, sinks) sit directly next to the door on both sides (k1, k5, k9). Needs a clear 3-cell entry walkway; handwash basin beside the door is good, shelves or trash cans in the swing area are not.
  - [medium] Orientation: fridge/shelf blocks and sinks on side walls are placed lengthwise without relation to the line; counters and equipment should all face the room interior and form parallel rows (line, prep, pass) rather than random axes. Sinks (white basins) rotated 90 degrees on side walls look random.
  - [medium] Details missing: bins/trolleys at prep and dish pit (only one red bin or an odd round pot per room), wall rack for pans, floor drain, fire extinguisher at the exit, extraction hood, cameras for corp security; wealth makes no visible difference (luxury 26x18 looks like squatter 14x10, just larger and emptier). Add wealth-gated pastry corner, second line, blast chiller, robot arm station.
  - [low] Tiny objects (a few single cells) in corners and corner stoves jammed against walls block the corner for walking; avoid corner placement for stoves and keep shelves reachable.
### bar_room
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 sketches, 8x8 wine bar to 20x12 club bar, dive to corp lounge): 1 m deep counter 8-14 cells long against/near a wall with back-bar shelves behind a 1-1.2 m staff aisle containing fridge, sink, till and a staff door to storage; one stool per 1-2 cells, 1 m free behind the stools; tables of 2-4 chairs, chairs facing the table on all sides, aisles 1.2 m; booths along walls (one facing the door in dives); jukebox/dartboard/TV on walls with free space; coat area and restroom door near the entrance; no center clutter.
- Findings (10 renders, nightclub/hotel, 14x12 to 28x18):
  1. The green pool table placed in the room centre is the worst offender: it sits 1-2 cells from the stools or from the table pairs, blocks the walk from door to bar, and in small rooms (14x12, 16x12) it is the dominant object. Pool/game tables need about 1.5 m free all around and belong to a corner or back zone, never between door and bar; in small rooms omit it.
  2. Bar counter is always short (4 stools) regardless of room size; in 28x18 rooms it is a stubby 8-cell block in a corner with a huge empty floor. Counter and stool count must scale with the room (bar should span a large part of a wall; a stool about every 1-2 cells, 8-12 in big rooms).
  3. The bar looks like a free-floating box: open at both ends, no visible staff side. Behind the counter there should be a clear staff aisle with a fridge, sink/glass washer, ice and till, a flip-up or open end for the bartender to enter, and a staff-only door to storage. Back-bar shelves alone are not enough; the staff aisle is one clean width and closed against guests.
  4. Tables: only 2 chairs per table, placed diagonally at opposite corners instead of centred on sides, which reads as misplaced. Chairs must sit centred at a table side and face it; vary 2-/4-seaters, and use small standing (poseur) tables near the bar.
  5. Tables sit in a rigid grid of identical pairs floating in the middle (e.g. 8 identical tables in two rows). Real bars have booths/banquettes along the walls, mixed table sizes, some tables pushed against walls; keep the room centre partly open as walkway/standing area.
  6. Door opens straight into the middle of the room with nothing near it: no coat hooks/ coat check, no bouncer/entry zone, no restroom door signal. Add an entrance zone, and the restroom/staff doors on the rear wall away from the bar; the walk from door to bar must be unobstructed.
  7. Wall objects (yellow, blue, pink-brown blocks) are unreadable and randomly placed in corners (a jukebox or sofa in a lone corner, a yellow item on a side wall). Jukebox, dartboard, TV, arcade etc. should clearly be on walls with free space in front (dart lane ~3 m), and grouped by activity; a stage/lounge zone needs seating facing it.
  8. Stools are spaced consistently but sit directly against the counter with the pool table or tables 1 cell behind them, leaving no pass-behind lane (needs ~1 m).
  9. Wealth variants look the same: a dive bar and a cocktail lounge need different furniture (sofas, armchairs, low tables, plants for high; mismatched tables, more clutter for low).

### dance_floor
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) the hall needs one clear focal axis: stage/DJ at one end, speakers beside it, an open floor in front, everything else on the edges; (2) the entrance zone near the door is a jumble and must become a tidy cloak/security area; (3) bars and seating belong along walls, not floating in the room; (4) add the missing club details (lighting, second exit, restrooms access, booths).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m basement dive to 30x20 m luxury; punk dive, BTL club, corp club, warehouse rave, lounge-style, biker bar, trideo club, hotel club): DJ booth/stage on a short wall facing the entrance with a clear sight line; 2-4 speaker stacks flanking the stage plus subs, never in the dance area; 40-60 % open floor in the centre; bar of 3-6 m along a side wall with stools on the floor side; booths/banquettes along the edges facing the floor, high tables only at the rim; light truss/LED wall over the floor; entrance far from the stage with cloakroom and security; 1.2 m emergency exits (2-3 above ~100 m2) and a restroom corridor near the entrance; Shadowrun flavour: trode/jack-in booths, drones, BTL dealers, graffiti on dives. Generated 12 floors (16x12 to 50x30 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] The DJ/stage axis is missing or wrong: the DJ booth (cyan-edged desk with mixer and a stool) stands directly beside the entrance in the south, with its back to the door, and the "stage" is a separate empty purple-outlined rectangle in a random north corner or on a side, often in the far corner (the stage is just empty). Stage/DJ must be one unit at the end of the room opposite the entrance, centred on the room axis, facing the floor, with speakers left and right and nothing in front of it.
  - [high] Dance floor is not defined as a space: the middle is just empty, no floor marking/podium, no lighting rig, no LED wall, no fog/laser props. Needs a dance area clearly in front of the stage (about 40-60 % of the area, free of furniture), plus overhead lights, subs on the floor and a few props to read as a club.
  - [high] Free-standing high tables with 2 stools are scattered in the hall (rows of 3-6 on the north side in big halls, single ones next to the bar or the DJ), including in the middle of the room (e.g. one at the room centre overlapping the label, others pairs mid-floor, one stuck between the two bars). Tables must stand in groups near walls or at the rim of the floor, aligned in a row at constant distance, with stools on both sides, never inside the dancing area or in front of the stage/door.
  - [high] Entrance clutter: a heap of unrelated objects (guard desk, bench, DJ desk, coat/cloak racks, a bench with stool, two red-ended bars = security gates/barriers?) sits in the first 3 m inside the door and partly blocks it (small rooms: the guard post, a desk and a bar all against the door wall, leaving a narrow lane). Put a coat-check counter and one security stand to the side of the entrance, keep a straight 2 m aisle from the door into the floor, and move the DJ booth away.
  - [high] A row of items (two round drums/stools and two green 4-cell benches) is laid out in a line in the middle of the room right in front of the entrance, 6 m from the door, in the dancing area. Looks like loose cargo; benches belong against walls (banquettes facing the floor), and sets must not float in the room centre.
  - [medium] Bars: 1-2 bars with stools on one side only; in the big halls two bars face the wrong way (back to the wall is fine) but they float away from the wall with a gap, are separated from the wall by shelf/fridge row, and in the narrow 18x14 example the bar is cut off by the north wall with the first stool half outside. A bar should be tight against the wall (back bar/shelves behind it), 5-8 m long, stools evenly spaced along the whole counter (currently 4 stools on a 12-14 cell bar), and a service gap/door at one end; the second bar belongs at the opposite side, not in the same wall.
  - [medium] Bar and wall items on long walls: the grey wall strips with light squares (shelving, beverage coolers?) are placed in short groups along walls, sometimes behind the bar and sometimes alone in the corners; fine, but they should sit consistently behind the bar only.
  - [medium] Seating is nearly absent: no booths, banquettes or lounge sofas around the edge in most halls, so the room looks like a warehouse; only tiny dark pods next to tables. Add banquette booths along the walls facing the floor (every 3-4 m), plus a small chill-out area by the bar in middle+ clubs.
  - [medium] The purple glowing object on a side wall (about 3x5 cells, in the middle of the long wall) is not explained, appears in every hall, and is not related to any other furniture. If it is an LED wall/screen it should be behind the stage, if a door/exit sign it needs a door; make its meaning clear and put it on the stage wall.
  - [medium] Wall-hugging objects in corners and on walls (brown boxes with dark halo, speakers?) are tiny and scattered (single boxes on west/east wall, corner top-left/top-right) and not symmetric to the stage; speakers must be paired and symmetric at both sides of the stage, 3-4 pairs in big halls and a delay pair half way along the room.
  - [medium] Missing: second emergency exit (a 36x24 or 50x30 hall with a single door and no panic exits), restroom corridor / WC doors near the entrance, cameras, plants, trash bins, fire extinguishers; assembly halls above 100 m2 need at least two widely spaced exits.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour absent: no jack-in/trode booths, drone or dealer corner, graffiti, wealth differences (low and luxury floors look identical apart from size). Add dive vs corp look: dive = few mismatched tables and a small bar; luxury = podium, VIP rope and booths, 2 bars, pillars.
  - [low] Sparse in big halls: 50x30 cells (25x15 m) has the same few objects as 24x16, large empty plains remain; scale bars, speaker pairs, booths and tables with area.
### lounge
- Status: done
- Blind design summary: 10 lounges (10x10 wine bar to 28x20 club) built around a focal point (bar, stage/DJ, window or trid screen): bar along one wall with back-bar and a staff aisle, stools only on the customer side; booths backed to walls with a table in front; sofa + armchair groups around a low table on a rug; round two-tops with chairs facing each other; DJ/stage with flanking speakers and a dance floor in front for bigger/nightclub variants; coat/host stand by the entrance; restroom/back door at the rear; 1 m aisles; plants, lamps, art. Luxury adds club chairs/VIP U-sofa/bottle table, low-wealth adds jukebox/cheap tables.
- Findings (reviewed 10 renders, nightclub --room lounge, 16x12 to 36x24, all wealth levels):
  1. Bar tables have their chairs scattered: the two stools of a round table sit on opposite diagonal corners, not on opposite sides, so a table reads as "table plus two random stools". Chairs must sit directly against the table, on opposite (or all) sides, facing it.
  2. The room has no focal point or zoning: no stage/DJ booth, no dance floor, no screen. Large lounges (24+ wide) are mostly empty floor with 3-5 items floating in it. Big rooms need a defined floor area (dance/stage with speakers) or more groups; small ones are fine.
  3. Booths along walls: the brown table in front of each booth is well placed, but the booths are spaced randomly and sometimes only one wall is used. Booths should form a regular row along a wall (equal spacing, same orientation, tables aligned) and leave an aisle in front.
  4. Green felt tables (pool/card) are dropped in the middle of the floor, sometimes two touching/abutting, sometimes with the label over them. They need cue/walking clearance on all sides (~1.5 m), should be aligned with room axes and each other, and a lounge should have at most one or two, near a wall with seating near, not as the room centrepiece.
  5. Bar: the bar with back-bar shelves and stools is good, but stools should stop short of the bar ends and be evenly spaced; the stool row sometimes hits the end or collides with the room label area. Add a service gap at the bar end and keep the bar away from the entrance sightline (current placement next to entrance in some seeds causes queueing at the door).
  6. Sofa/armchair groups are missing: nothing forms a conversation cluster (sofa + 1-2 armchairs around a low table, on a rug). Seating pieces are isolated singles; group them with a table and face them inward.
  7. Entrance zone: nothing at the door (coat rack/host stand/cloak or a plant), the door opens straight into an empty field. Add a small arrival zone and keep the 2 m in front of the door clear.
  8. Missing details a lounge has: restroom/staff door at the rear, plants, standing lamps and decor in the empty middle zones, rugs under seating groups; wealth variants look nearly identical (low vs luxury differ only in counts). Luxury should show club chairs/VIP area, low should show cheap tables/jukebox.
  9. Wall decoration dots (pillars/lamps) bunch in pairs next to each other along walls rather than being evenly spaced.

### vip_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x4 m to 12x6 m, budget to megaluxury): U/L banquette wrapped round one low bottle-service table (seats face the table, 0.4 m gap), a second seating group in big rooms, a bar with 2-5 stools on a wall away from the door, wall screen/glass window, rug under the group, plants, bodyguard stand by the door, clear 1 m entry aisle; luxury adds poker/stage/private restroom, Shadowrun adds trode booth, camera. Generated 10 (12x10 to 24x14 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10, renders in scratchpad vip_room_r).
  - [high] Seating is not grouped: sofas are scattered one per wall (often 3-4 sofas around the room edge) facing nothing, and the room looks like a waiting room. A VIP room needs a lounge group: sofas arranged in a U/L round a shared low table, close to each other, seats facing the table.
  - [high] Low tables are missing or detached: most results have one single table next to one sofa while the other sofas (up to 3) have no table at all (v3, v4, v5, v9, v10); 2 rugs/tables sit alone in the middle of the room with no seats around them (v6, v9). Every sofa group must have its table within reach (0.4-0.5 m), and a rug belongs under the table plus seats, not as a lone item.
  - [high] Huge empty centre in larger rooms (v4 24x14, v8, v10): 60-70 percent of the floor is empty with everything pressed against the walls. Fill with a second seating island or a dance/stage/pole area instead of leaving a void; scale the number of groups with area.
  - [high] No real bar/bottle service: the room has no bar counter with stools in most seeds, only a thin grey strip on a wall (v2, v4, v7, v10) and a glowing screen/aquarium-like item; no ice bucket/bottle table, no fridge. A VIP room is defined by drinks: a short bar (wall or corner) with 2-4 stools, plus a bottle-service table.
  - [medium] Poufs/stools are sprinkled randomly along walls and in corners (one at the door in v7, singles on side walls in v3/v9/v10) not tied to any table. Put them around a table (2-4) or drop them.
  - [medium] Sofa near door is in the entry line: v5 puts a sofa in the south-west corner directly beside the door, v7 puts a sofa and two poufs at the door; keep 1 m entry aisle and put the most private seating at the far end, with a bodyguard/host spot by the door.
  - [medium] Missing details: no wall screen above the main seating or glass window to the club floor, no speakers, no plants/art for high/luxury, no coat hook, no camera; the green item in the corner is the only decoration. Luxury should visibly differ (second group, bar, stage/pole, plants), but low and luxury look almost identical.
  - [low] Sofas have identical size and orientation, so wide rooms get a row of 2 sofas back-to-back with a gap (v6 north wall); join them into one long banquette or make them face each other across a table.
### backstage
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a dressing/makeup station: a long mirror counter on one wall with a row of chairs (one per artist) all facing the mirror, aisle behind them; (2) a real lounge group (sofa(s) + low table facing a screen); (3) catering/rider (table, fridge) near the door; (4) costume racks, lockers and flight cases against walls, not floating; (5) fix scale: the room reads empty.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 closet to 30x16 arena green room): dive = 1 mirror counter with 2 chairs, a sofa, a rack, a flight case and a bin; mid club = counter with 4-6 chairs, sofa + table + TV lounge, fridge/snack table, 2 racks, lockers, flight cases near the stage door; luxury = lit makeup stations, 3 sofas round a table, mini bar, fruit table, rug, plants, shower/WC door; theatre/band/Shadowrun variants add ironing/sewing, instrument stands, trode couch, weapon lockers. Chairs face mirrors, lounge faces TV, 1 m aisles, no furniture in the door swing. Generated 12 (16x12 to 44x26 cells, squatter to luxury, nightclub --room backstage) and compared.
  - [high] Far too empty and the same at every wealth: a 40x24 or 44x26 cell room (roughly 20x12 m) holds 2-6 objects and large areas are bare floor (b5, b10, b12); squatter and luxury look alike. A green room needs 15-30 objects scaled with size and wealth, grouped in zones (dressing, lounge, catering, storage).
  - [high] Dressing station is missing or broken: only some results have one mirror counter, always with a single chair, and many results (b3, b9, b12, b1) have none at all. There are loose single stools/small blocks scattered along walls and in corners that belong to nothing (b4, b6, b7, b9). Chairs must stand in a row in front of a long mirror counter, one per place, facing it; a backstage without a mirror station is not a backstage. Counter should be 3-6 chairs long on bigger rooms, not one lonely desk.
  - [high] No lounge: a sofa shows up only in about a third of results, alone against a wall with no coffee table, no TV/screen and nothing to face (b2, b4, b8, b10); at least one sofa group with table and screen should always exist.
  - [high] Flight cases float in the middle of the floor (b1, b4, b5, b7, b8: 2-4 cases at random spots in open space, even one inside the walkway; b11 two cases beside the door overlapping). Cases should be stacked/lined along a wall or in a corner, preferably near the stage door, never blocking the main route.
  - [medium] Missing typical items: costume racks (clothes rails), lockers, fridge/drinks rider table, snack table, full-length mirror, wall screen with stage feed, running order board, power strips, waste bins (only a tiny bin in some corners). Add by wealth: low = rack, bin, sofa; high/luxury = minibar, extra sofas, plants, rug, wardrobe, make-up lights.
  - [medium] Only one door (south, generic double door) and it opens straight into an empty zone; a backstage normally has two connections (stage wing and back/stage-door exit to alley). Place the door-side clutter (cases, catering) near the exit and keep a clear walkway from door to stage side.
  - [medium] Placement looks random rather than composed: objects cluster in corners (b4 two stools stacked on the west wall, b6 stools in a line along the north wall without a counter), with no relation to the door or to each other. Group related things (rack + mirror, sofa + table + TV) and keep >= 1 m clear in front of each.
  - [low] Rooms with extreme aspect (b5, b10, b12 wider than 2:1) read as warehouses; a backstage is compact, so split into zones or keep it near 3:2 and put a partition/lounge zone at the far end.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour absent: no trode/simsense couch, no drone dock, no weapons/cyberware lockers, no security guard post at the door of a VIP green room.
### cloakroom
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) form a real coat check: a counter with the attendant side behind it and the rail rows behind the counter, instead of loose items scattered along the walls; (2) rails in parallel rows with aisles, perpendicular to the counter; (3) guest queue zone in front of the counter near the door; (4) round items (stools/stands) go beside the counter or door, not floating in the middle; (5) details by wealth (lockers, mirror, bench, bag shelf, ticket board).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x4 m tiny club to 14x8 m gala): service counter (half-door, shelf for guests) next to the entrance, 1 m queue zone in front, 1.2 m work aisle behind, 2-6 parallel coat rails behind it with ~1 m aisles, bag/hat shelves on the back wall, attendant stool, till/ticket board, umbrella stand at the door; luxury adds lockers, mirror, bench, fur room; dive is a table plus one rail; Shadowrun flavour: weapon check lockers and a bouncer. Generated 10 rooms (12x10 to 30x20 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] No coat-check composition: the counter is a lone slab on a random wall (often the far wall, c1, c5, c7, c9) and the hanging racks (4x1 strips) are spread over all walls and corners with no relation to it. The racks must sit behind the counter (staff side), in rows, and the counter must separate guests from the racks; today a guest can walk straight to any rack.
  - [high] Racks are single 4-cell strips glued to the walls (north, east, west, corners, even beside the door, c3, c6, c8); nothing is a real rail row. Real coat rooms have free-standing double-sided rails in parallel rows (perpendicular to the counter) with ~1 m aisles, filling a good part of the room. Large rooms (c4, c5, c7: 20x14 to 30x20) are almost empty with 3-4 strips: scale the number of rails with the area and build rows.
  - [high] Counter placement/orientation: it is not near the door and does not face the entry; in c1/c4 it sits on a side wall with the room empty in front of it. It must be next to the entrance side, parallel to the door wall or at a short distance, with a clear queue zone in front and the door not leading into the back side.
  - [medium] Round objects (stools or stands, 1-4 per room) are in random spots: mid-room (c10: four round items in a diagonal line in the middle; c7: two in the middle), in a corner, or directly at the door (c9). An attendant stool belongs behind the counter, an umbrella stand beside the door; nothing round should stand free in the walkway.
  - [medium] Rack placed in the corner by the door (c3 SW, c6 SE, c9 SW) or just beside the door blocks the entrance and the queue zone; keep the door zone free except for a stand.
  - [medium] Missing details a cloakroom has: ticket/number board or hook numbers, bag and hat shelf above/behind the racks, lost-property shelf, till/terminal on the counter, mirror, bench for boots, lockers for valuables (high/luxury), umbrella stand, weapon-check lockers (Shadowrun). Nothing here makes the room read as a cloakroom (labels aside), and wealth makes no visible difference.
  - [low] Rooms are too big and empty: a cloakroom is compact (about 10 m2 to 30 m2); big ones should be a deep rail field, not a hall with ornaments around the edge.
### coffin_hall
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 12x10 to 40x14 cells, squatter to luxury): stacked 2-tier coffin units in wall rows and back-to-back island rows, openings (curtain or hatch) toward a 2-3 cell aisle, a ladder or step per stack, a locker per unit or a locker bank at the entry/row ends, shoe rack and bench by the door, a straight aisle from door to the far end, exit sign and extinguisher, luxury adds a lounge nook and larger aisle, seedy adds vending machine and bins. Generated 11 halls (12x10 to 40x14, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-11) and compared.
  - [high] The hall is almost empty: the only furniture seen is one unit-type row; no lockers, no ladders, no shoe rack, no bench, no luggage rack, no vending/bin, no extinguisher or exit sign. A real coffin hall has a locker per sleeper (at the unit or in banks near the entry) and a place to leave shoes/bags by the door. Add these.
  - [high] On wide halls (h4, h9, h10, h11: 24-36 cells wide) a huge empty floor lies between two thin side rows (up to 28 cells of nothing). Real halls fill the room with more rows: add back-to-back island rows with aisles of 2-3 cells between, so units fill the room; leave only a modest open area at the door (a small lounge/seating nook, lockers).
  - [high] Units are only placed along two opposite walls in short rows, the aisle always runs the width of the room; in long halls (h7) the units are one long single row with every second partition and an enormous open strip in front. No island rows, no double-height feel; reads as cubicles/cells, not coffins. Units should be stacked tiers with a visible ladder or step and a smaller footprint so more fit.
  - [high] Each unit is a closed cubicle ~5x4 cells (2.5x2 m) with its own swinging door whose arc fills the aisle and collides with the opposite row's arcs on narrow halls (h2, h5: doors from both rows overlap in the same 3-cell aisle). Coffin units are about 1x2 m with a curtain/sliding hatch, so units must be smaller (about 2x4 cells), with no swing-door arcs into the aisle.
  - [medium] Unit doors face the aisle correctly and the bed is at the wall end; a good feature, keep. But in h1 and h7 (row along the north wall, aisle in front), the open floor in front is 8+ cells deep with nothing in it; add a second row or a bench/locker row on the opposite wall.
  - [medium] Windows run along nearly the whole long wall of the hall (cyan rows in h3 to h11), including the wall behind units; a coffin hotel is typically windowless inside or with a few small windows. Reduce windows, none behind units.
  - [medium] Door and aisle relation: the entrance is at the middle of a short or long wall opposite nothing in particular; the aisle is straight from the door, good. But no reception counter / key desk / lock-up at the door, and no side door to a washroom (a shared washroom/shower is the defining facility of a coffin hotel).
  - [low] Single purple object (vending machine or terminal, seen in h2, h3, h5, h7, h10) is the only decoration and appears at random wall spots; place it by the door/lounge. Unit counts are even and symmetric (good); partial last rows (h2 middle open slot, h9) look fine.

### coffin_aisle
- Status: done
- Blind design: 3-4 cell clear aisle between two facing rows of stacked coffins, exits at both ends (or one end plus a fire door on long aisles), shoe racks/lockers at the entrance end, ladders shared per pair of units, floor evacuation strip and exit sign over the ends, extinguisher and smoke detector, dim light every ~4 cells, luxury = single-stack with carpet runner and bench, cheap = 3-high with trash/vending at a dead end.
- Findings:
  1. Generated alone the aisle is an empty rectangle (one door, one alarm triangle, sometimes one magenta box, a corner sensor): it reads as a bare room, not an aisle; no sleeping units, so nothing says what it serves.
  2. Only a single door at one end, even at 30+ cells: a long aisle needs a second exit or fire door at the far end; the far end is currently a dead end.
  3. Nothing is placed along the long walls: shoe racks, lockers/luggage lockers, a ladder/step or unit numbering are missing; the entrance end should hold shoe rack and lockers.
  4. Fire safety is too thin for the length: one alarm only; add extinguisher, exit sign over the door and evacuation light strip, scaling with length.
  5. Wall fixtures (the magenta box, wall lamp) appear at random positions on any wall, including mid-aisle on the end wall; they should sit at ends or at regular intervals along the walls, never block the centre.
  6. No wealth variation: squatter, low, middle and luxury look identical; cheap should have trash/graffiti/vending, luxury carpet runner, bench, plants.
  7. In coffin_hall context the aisle is a very wide (~8 cell) open floor with cubicle doors swinging into it; it is much wider than a real aisle, empty, and the door swings face each other directly across with no stagger. A bench, lockers or a lounge strip should use that width, or the aisle should be narrower.
### coffin_unit
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 2x4 to 4x6 cells, squatter to luxury): pod about 1x2 m (2x4 cells) mostly filled by the mattress, pillow at the wall end, 1-2 tiers with one ladder per stack, shelf, lamp and socket at the head, sliding hatch or curtain (no swing arc), vent grille, personal storage as under-bed drawer or locker at the foot, luxury adds trode/deck, safe, mirror, stool; squatter has a lockbox and no curtain. Generated 12 units alone (min size 10x10 to 12x12 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] A unit is an ordinary 10x10-cell (5x5 m) room with a swing door and a single bed in it, no pod: the bed takes about 4x2 cells of a 100-cell floor and 90 percent of the room is empty. A coffin unit must be a tiny enclosure just big enough for the sleeper (about 2x4 cells, 3x5 for luxury), with the mattress filling it; the generator should be able to produce it at that size, not only as a big room.
  - [high] Missing everything that makes it a pod: no shelf, lamp, socket, clock, vent, hatch or curtain, no ladder or second tier (all 12 results are one flat bed, no tier), no personal storage next to the bed (in 6 of 12 results there is nothing but the bed; otherwise one grey locker-like block floating on a wall far from the bed, e.g. u3, u4, u8, u9, u11, u12). Add a head-end shelf/lamp/socket and a storage box or drawer at the foot or under the bed, always touching the bed zone.
  - [high] The door is a normal swing door with its arc opening into/outside a full room; a real unit has a sliding hatch or curtain on the long side or foot end facing the aisle, and the opening must be right at the foot of the bed (sleeper climbs in from the aisle), not a separate door in a wall far away from it.
  - [high] Bed placement ignores the opening: bed is pushed into random corners or against the far/north wall (u2, u5, u6, u7, u1, u3), and in u9 the foot sits in the south-west corner right beside the door. The head must be at a closed wall end, the foot toward the opening with 1 cell of step/floor in front of it; only one consistent orientation.
  - [medium] Mattress and pillow are rendered fine (pillow at one end, blanket zone), but there is no distinction by wealth: squatter, middle and luxury units look identical (same bed, same emptiness). Luxury needs a wider mattress, a safe, deck/screen and stool; squatter a plain cot and a lockbox; higher wealth means more details, not more floor.
  - [medium] The unit has a window-less look (good) but the red triangle marker in the corner and large unused floor suggest a utility room; if tiers are not possible alone, show at least a headboard shelf and the step so it reads as a sleeping pod.
### taproom
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches 10x8 to 24x12 m, squatter to luxury): straight or L bar along a wall with a back-bar (shelves, fridge, taps), one stool per ~0.8 m (5 stools in a tiny dive, 9-12 in a big one, bar length scales with room), 1 m aisle behind the stools and a bartender lane; wall booths (bench plus table, benches facing each other) and round/square tables with 2-4 chairs spread evenly; pool table in open space with 1.2 m cue clearance on all sides (a second one in big rooms), darts board, jukebox, ATM/machine on the wall; TV/screens over the bar; toilet corridor in a corner away from the bar; clear 2-cell landing and a straight aisle from the door to the bar; fixer/runner corner booth facing the door; luxury adds stage, horseshoe island bar, high tables. Generated 12 rooms (10x8 to 30x16, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] The bar does not scale: every room gets the same bar (8 cells long, 4 stools) from 12x8 to 30x16, so large rooms (24x12, 30x16) have a tiny bar and a vast empty floor with a few tables, while it is ok only for the smallest rooms. A bigger room needs a longer bar with more stools (roughly 1 stool per cell-pair along the whole counter) or a second bar/L-corner; the stools should run the full length of the counter, not stop 4 cells short of the bar end.
  - [high] Large rooms look empty and ordered like a conference hall: 30x16 has 7 widely spaced identical tables in two rows and 60 percent empty floor; no pool, no booth rows, no darts, no stage. A taproom should be filled by zones (bar zone, table zone, game zone, booth wall) and tables should be grouped/pushed together, not evenly dotted over the centre.
  - [high] Pool table is unreliable and badly placed: absent in 24x12, 30x16 and the 20x12 render although it is the signature object; where present it is pushed against a wall (12x8, 12x12: long side against the west wall, no room to cue) or 1 m from the door and in the walkway (16x10). It must stand free in an open spot with about 3 cells clear on every side, away from the bar stools and the entrance route, with a cue rack/darts nearby; large rooms should get two.
  - [high] A guard desk (counter with front facing the entrance, cyan edge) is placed in the entry line right in front of the door in most results and in 24x12 two of them stand side by side; a dive taproom has no reception counter. A bouncer should be a single stool/stand beside the door, not a desk blocking the route to the bar; drop the second one entirely.
  - [high] There are no real booths: the wall pieces are sofas with a coffee table 2-3 cells out, so they read as a lounge, not as bar booths. A dive bar wants wall booths (high-back bench seats facing each other across a fixed table) in a row along one wall, plus a corner booth with a view of the door for the fixer/runner meeting.
  - [medium] Table seating is sloppy: tables carry only 2 chairs, placed at diagonal offsets so one chair sits north and one south of a table and the rest of the table is bare; stools are drawn partly overlapped by the room label. Tables should have 2-4 chairs symmetrically around them, and chairs not stray diagonally into the aisle.
  - [medium] Missing things a dive bar always has: dart board with throw line, jukebox, cigarette/arcade/gambling machine, toilet door hint, coat hooks, bottle storage behind the bar, a trash bin near the bar and a bar mat/drain; Shadowrun flavour (matrix terminal booth, trid/screen over the bar, bullet holes/brawl damage) is only represented by a few neon strips and wall screens.
  - [medium] Back-bar is a row of shelves/fridges against the wall with a 2-cell empty strip and then the counter; there is no cash register, no taps, no sink and no gap/flap where the bartender enters, and the counter never reaches a wall so the end is open at both sides. A bar should connect to a wall (or form an L) and have a service opening on one end.
  - [low] Bar stools and tables sit very evenly on a coordinate grid (identical spacing, same orientation everywhere); a bit of irregular spacing and some tables pushed to the wall would read less artificial. Plants/side tables dotted along the north and south wall in corners look like sofa side tables, not bar clutter.
### fixer_room
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (street fixer 10x8 to luxury 20x14). Common rules: desk faces the door across the room, fixer chair behind it with back to a wall, 2-4 guest chairs on the door side of the desk in an arc, safe behind the desk, drinks cabinet and job/map board on side walls, guard stool just inside the door, a second escape door, luxury adds sofa group with table and a meeting nook.
- Findings:
  - [high] The room is nearly empty: only a desk, one chair and two stools plus an occasional cabinet row or corner box on a 10x10 to 20x14 floor. A fixer office needs at least a safe, a drinks/bar cabinet, a job/map board, guest seating and a terminal; more than 70% of the floor is bare in every render.
  - [high] The seating relation is wrong: the desk sits against a wall and the single chair stands on the room side of it, so the fixer faces away from the wall and nobody sits opposite. Real layout: fixer chair behind the desk with its back to a wall, 2-3 guest chairs on the open side facing the desk, and the desk oriented so the fixer faces the door.
  - [high] The two round stools are placed at the wall ends flanking the desk (corners of the desk wall), not as guest seats. Guest chairs must stand in front of the desk, facing it, with a walkway behind them to the door.
  - [high] The desk placement varies randomly (right wall, left wall, north wall, right next to the door wall, back toward the door in seeds 3/4/8/10). A visitor entering should see the desk across the room, never have it beside or behind the door; the desk should be on the far side from the door.
  - [medium] The cabinet row (4 lockers) appears only sometimes and is jammed in a corner or beside the door. Cabinets/safe should sit in the wall behind or beside the fixer, not on the visitor's entry path.
  - [medium] No bodyguard post, no second escape door/hatch, no wealth differentiation: low and luxury rooms look the same except for size. Luxury needs sofa group with low table and rug, bar cabinet, screen; street fixer needs cheap crates/fridge/stools; a bigger room should add more furniture, not more floor.
  - [low] Corner objects (cyan and grey squares) float in far corners with no relation to the desk; a terminal/comm should be on the desk, plants/trash in corners with a purpose.

## industry.yaml
### warehouse_floor
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 12x8 m smuggler shed to 30x20 m corp logistics, cold-chain, cross-dock, block-stack, automated): pallet racking in parallel rows with 3 m forklift aisles and a cross aisle, dock doors on one wall with a staging zone, glazed shipping office by the docks, columns on a grid, forklift charging bay with 2-4 forklifts, caged high-value area, bollards at rack ends, extinguishers on columns, clear fire lanes to exits. Generated 10 halls (16x12 to 60x40 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] Missing the dock logic: the hall has doors at north and south ends but no staging/receiving zone at the dock door; crates and barrels are scattered at random across the whole floor (incl. in front of the doors and in corners) instead of pallets lined up in a staging area at the loading door. Goods must cluster next to the dock door, not be sprinkled between racks.
  - [high] Missing vehicles and equipment: no forklift, pallet jacks, charging station, conveyor or scanner anywhere. A warehouse hall without a forklift/charging bay looks empty; add forklifts and a charger near the dock, pallet jacks near staging.
  - [high] Rack count and arrangement are bland: racks are all identical long blocks in evenly spaced rows with the same short gap in the middle of each row; no cross aisle logic (a gap exists but is not a deliberate perpendicular aisle), and on mid sizes the area at the dock end is simply empty. Racks must be oriented consistently, with rows reaching close to the walls (aisle only at ends), and the rack field should be centred/organised relative to the dock door aisle (main aisle straight from door to door). Small halls (16x12) get a single lonely rack row in the middle, which looks wrong: use two rows along a wall or shelving along the walls.
  - [high] Loose crates on the floor sit in aisles and touch rack ends (w4, w5, w7, w10: crates directly against a rack end, or in the aisle between two rack rows). Pallet goods must never block forklift aisles; aisles stay clear and goods are placed in marked staging blocks.
  - [medium] Pallet-like crates are single 2x2 cells at random positions; real floor stock is stacked pallets in blocks of several, in neat rows with an aisle, and bulk goods along the wall. Group into rows/blocks.
  - [medium] Shipping office / caged area is shown as large orange plain blocks (1-3 per hall, up to 12x20 cells) with no visible internal detail, doors or glazing, plus no relation to the docks. Needs to be one office at the dock door with desk, and one smaller cage for high value; do not put orange blocks into the far corners and across the wall with windows.
  - [medium] Missing safety details: fire extinguishers only a few at random wall spots, no hose/hydrant reel, eyewash or first aid, no bollards at rack ends or at the door, no floor markings for fire lanes. Extinguishers should sit at columns/exits regularly (every ~25 m of walkway).
  - [medium] No columns on a grid in large halls (w6-w9): 60x40 cells with zero columns/supports looks like a tent; add a column grid (every ~8 m) that racks align to.
  - [low] Stray objects: a few barrels/drums standing in the middle of aisles (w3-w6) and a tiny wall object (toilet-like white shape at the east wall in w4/w7) look misplaced; barrels belong in a group along a wall; a sink/toilet shape in the hall wall looks wrong.
  - [low] Wealth barely shows: squatter and luxury differ only in crate count; luxury should show automation (conveyor, scanner gate, tidy markings), squatter loose clutter and fewer racks.
### shipping_office
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x8 to 24x14 cells, low to luxury): 1-6 desks with PC in a row facing the dock window, a drivers' counter with label printer and scale next to the door, filing cabinets and a paperwork shelf in a row, a coffee corner (table, kettle, fridge), bulletin board, PPE lockers, first-aid and extinguisher, at high wealth a meeting table, copier, waiting bench/vending for drivers; Shadowrun flavour (RFID terminal, drone dock, guard post, camera, safe). Generated 12 rooms (10x8 to 24x14, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] The room is far too empty: every size from 10x8 to 24x14 gets the same 2-3 desks, one filing-cabinet block, a cot-like piece and 1-2 tiny corner items, leaving 70-80% bare carpet (the 24x14 room has about 5 objects). Furniture count must scale with area and wealth: more workstations (up to 4-6 in a big office), several cabinets, shelves, and a real coffee corner.
  - [high] The defining furniture is missing: no drivers' counter/service hatch near the door, no scale, label printer, packing table, pigeonhole/paperwork rack, bulletin board, or lockers. Without these it reads as a generic office; add a counter near the door facing into the room with free standing space on the driver side.
  - [high] Desks are scattered at random places and orientations (one on the west wall, one on the north, one by the east wall, facing the corner or away from the windows). A shipping office needs the workstations in a row or two rows, side by side along the warehouse-facing wall, all facing the same direction (toward the window onto the dock), each with a chair on the room side.
  - [high] A bed/cot-like object (dark with cyan end) stands in 8 of 12 rooms in the middle of a wall or in a corner next to the door; it does not belong in a shipping office (at most a couch in a waiting/rest corner for high wealth, not a bed, not by the entrance).
  - [medium] Windows run on every wall including the door wall, so there is no clear dock-facing window and the room reads as a glass box; windows should concentrate on the warehouse-floor side (a wide glazing strip), with solid walls elsewhere where cabinets and shelves can stand.
  - [medium] Filing cabinets are lone blocks of 3-4 in random spots (one right beside the door, one in the middle of the east wall); they should stand together in a row against a solid wall, with front clearance, not next to the door swing or under a window.
  - [medium] Desk-to-chair relation is loose: chairs float beside the desk (to its side, not behind it on the user side) and desks on the west/east wall have the chair pointing sideways; chair must be directly in front of the desk's working edge, desk back towards the wall or facing the window.
  - [medium] Door area is empty and the door opens straight onto empty floor; a real office has a sign-in/counter, a hi-vis/coat hook, a notice board and a fire extinguisher/first-aid kit by the door; for small rooms the single desk should be close enough to the door to serve drivers.
  - [low] Corner items (teal square, small circles, triangle) are tiny and hard to identify; a kettle/coffee machine on a table and a bin should look like a defined corner with a counter, not isolated pieces. Shadowrun flavour (RFID scanner terminal, camera, safe, drone dock) absent at all wealth levels.
### clean_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m university to 20x12 m corp/pharma): gowning airlock at the entry (bench, lockers, sticky mat, shoe-cover dispenser, hand-wash sink, optionally air shower, inner door offset from outer), process bays with tool rows at 1.2-1.5 m aisles, laminar-flow hoods/biosafety cabinets over benches, material pass-through hatch to the grey chase, viewing window, particle counter by the door, eyewash, chemical cabinet, carts. Luxury/Shadowrun adds a central nanofab/assembler with ring aisle, cameras, ID scanner. Generated 12 (14x12 to 40x24 cells, low to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] The room is a plain hall with the door straight into the work area: no gowning airlock/vestibule, no sticky mat, no gown bench or lockers, no second (inner) door. This is the defining feature of a cleanroom; the entry zone must be a separated lock-like area (cabin or wall strip) and the work area must be reached through it.
  - [high] Almost the whole room is identical white benches in pairs/rows, so it reads like a classroom or generic lab, not a cleanroom. Missing: laminar-flow hoods / biosafety cabinets on benches, process tools of different kinds, incubators/freezers/centrifuges, chemical cabinet, carts, pass-through hatch, particle counter, viewing window, eyewash/emergency shower. Add variety by grouping into bays (e.g. benches with hoods, a tool row, a wet station) rather than one object type repeated.
  - [high] Stools are inconsistent: many benches have none, some have one stool under a pair of benches, others sit offset from any bench centre (c1 stool in the aisle between two benches, c3 single stool for four benches). Each working bench should have exactly one stool on its working (south/aisle) side, centred; wall-facing sides need none.
  - [medium] Benches are placed as free-floating islands with uneven gaps: some pairs touch, then a big empty gap, then a lone bench (c5, c6, c8, c10, c12 columns with a left pair and a single right bench). Rows should be complete and evenly spaced, with the same aisle (about 1.2-1.5 m) between rows and a clear main aisle from the door; leftover single benches at the ends look random. Large rooms (c6, c12) have far too much empty floor around the edge; put tools along the walls or use a back-to-back layout with a service strip.
  - [medium] The wall sinks/sink-like units sit in arbitrary spots (north wall middle, east wall, far corner) away from the entry. Hand-wash and eyewash belong at the entry/airlock; chemical/wet stations along a wall near drainage.
  - [medium] Small cyan wall items and the small corner marker (triangle) are unreadable and positioned at random (some beside the door, some in far corners); a particle counter/monitor should be near the entry or on the inner wall at eye height, an alarm/panel next to the door.
  - [low] Wealth barely changes the result (c7 luxury 14x14 only has two benches and 3 wall units); luxury should add a central high-end tool (nanofab/assembler) with a ring aisle, cameras and an ID scanner at the inner door; low should be a small bench-and-hood lab. The tiny 12x10 room with a single bench looks empty; small rooms should still get the lock area plus two benches.
### wet_lab
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (10x14 to 30x18). Real wet lab = continuous wall benches (depth ~1.5 cells) along 2-3 walls with sinks built in, 1-3 parallel island benches with sinks at the ends and shelving, fume hoods in a row on a dead/outer wall, a cold row (fridges, freezers, -80) in one cluster, instrument zone (centrifuge, incubator, PCR, autoclave), eyewash+shower beside the door, gas cylinders chained in a corner near the hoods, write-up desks with chairs outside the wet zone near windows, bins at bench ends, stools at benches, aisles of at least 1.5 cells.
- Findings (from 10 renders, corp_lab --room wet_lab, 12x10 to 30x18, low to luxury):
  1. There are no wall benches. Walls hold only a few tiny isolated units; a wet lab must be lined with continuous benches on at least two walls, with the other equipment standing on or beside them. Rooms read as empty halls with 2-3 slabs.
  2. Island benches are blank white slabs: no sinks, taps, equipment, shelf or reagent rack on them. Islands need visible content (sink at an end, microscopes, racks, shelf spine down the middle).
  3. Equipment is far too sparse for the room size (a 30x18 lab has 9 islands but about 6 wall objects). Add more instruments (centrifuges, incubators, PCR, balances) grouped in a zone, and fridges/freezers as one cold row, not single items scattered at corners.
  4. Fume hoods appear as 1-2 stray units, sometimes at the door wall or in different corners. They must be a row of 2-4 on one dead wall, facing the room with free standing space in front, away from the door.
  5. Stools are inconsistent: only some benches have one, always on the same side, many benches none. Each work position needs a stool tucked at the bench, and benches be worked from both sides if aisle allows.
  6. No write-up desks or chairs at all; a lab needs a desk or two with chairs near the door or a window, separate from the wet benches.
  7. Gas cylinders (red) float in random spots (e.g. mid north wall, next to the door) with nothing around. Place them in a pair or rack in a corner near the hoods, against the wall.
  8. Eyewash/shower, bins and sharps bins are missing or unclear: need an eyewash/shower right beside the entry, and waste bins at bench ends/by sinks.
  9. Layout: islands are centered in the room leaving wide dead bands to the walls (up to 3 cells) while walls are empty; islands should keep about 1.5-2 cell aisles to wall benches and be aligned with the room's long axis. Wide labs (22+) leave a big empty gap between island groups; one island line of 3+ in a row with a single central aisle is more natural.
  10. Large labs have a single door at the bottom; add a second exit and lay out the zones (wet benches / instruments / cold storage / write-up) relative to it.
### specimen_storage
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x10 to 30x20 cells, low to high security): door on a short wall with a gowning/PPE vestibule (lockers, sink, sticky mat) just inside; ultra-low freezers in straight rows along the long walls (back to the wall, 1 m aisle between facing rows, optional back-to-back island in big rooms); a cluster of LN2 dewars in a far corner with O2 monitor; cryo racks and box shelving on one wall; inventory desk with barcode scanner and PC near the door; thaw/prep bench; alarm/UPS panel by the door; Shadowrun flavour (paracritter sample cases, biohazard marks, locked terminal). Generated 10 rooms (12x10 to 30x20, squatter to luxury, corp_lab --room specimen_storage) and compared.
  - [high] The room contains only one kind of object: identical small cyan freezer boxes, 6-9 per room regardless of size, and nothing else (no dewars, racks, shelves, desk, scanner, bench, panel, PPE lockers). It reads as an empty hall with a few fridges. Needs the defining furniture set: LN2 dewar cluster, cryo racks/shelving, inventory desk with scanner, prep bench, alarm/UPS panel, gowning lockers at the door.
  - [high] Freezers are scattered individually at random along random walls, in pairs and singles with large gaps, one at a corner, one lone box on the far wall (s7, s10: one freezer alone mid-wall). They must be arranged in a tight, evenly spaced row (touching or one-cell gaps) along a wall, all facing the same way into the room, with an aisle in front; a freezer bank is the main visual of the room.
  - [high] Big rooms are mostly bare floor: 20x14, 24x16 and 30x20 cells have 7-9 objects and 85-90% empty floor in the middle. Object count must scale with area; large rooms need multiple parallel freezer rows or a back-to-back island with aisles (about 2 cells between rows) instead of everything hugging the perimeter.
  - [medium] Freezers sit right next to the door on both sides (s4, s5, s6, s9, s10, s2: units against the door wall adjacent to the door opening), crowding the entrance and door swing. Keep the door zone free for a gowning/decon area and sign-in desk; freezer rows belong on the walls away from the door.
  - [medium] Wealth is not visible: squatter, low and luxury look the same apart from count. Luxury/high should show an automated or dense freezer wall, dewar bay, clean tidy rows and extra monitoring; low/squatter mismatched, fewer units and clutter (boxes on the floor, a cheap shelf).
  - [medium] Freezers are placed at differing orientations (some long side to the wall, some short side) in the same room (s1, s7), giving a random look; all units of one bank should share orientation and size.
  - [low] Missing Shadowrun/safety flavour: no biohazard/warning markings, no emergency eyewash, no extinguisher, no camera or locked terminal, no containment cases for paracritter samples.
### test_chamber
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x8 interrogation cell to 30x20 combat arena): bare central test floor with floor markings and a drain, one subject object (restraint chair/rig) in the middle with >= 3 cells clear around it, targets in a row or cover blocks for arenas, a long observation window with the control console and intercom OUTSIDE (or on the window wall), two doors on different walls (subject entry and staff entry, decon/airlock at the subject door), cameras in the corners, safety shutter, eyewash/extinguisher. Generated 12 chambers (12x12 to 30x20 cells, seeds 1-12) and compared.
  - [high] No observation window or control side: all 12 chambers are closed boxes with a single door and no window, so the room reads as an empty storeroom, not a test chamber. Needs a long glass wall (4-8 cells) with the console/intercom behind it, and the console block that does appear (grey slab on the wall, 3 of 12) is not tied to a window or facing the floor.
  - [high] Only one door in every result (south wall, centred); a test chamber has a separate subject entry and a staff/exit door on a different wall. Also add a safety shutter or decon airlock at the subject door.
  - [high] Far too empty on large floors: 24x16, 30x20 results hold one 2-circle pad plus 1-3 cameras and nothing else (t5, t6, t9, t10: 1 object in 28x18 cells). Furniture count must scale with area (more rigs/targets, cover blocks, floor markings, a second test station) or the room should stay small.
  - [high] The central subject object does not read as a restraint chair or test rig: the same small 2-circle pad is repeated, sometimes up against walls or in corners (t2 west wall, t8 east/south-east corners half cut off by the wall), where it is not a test station. The main subject station must be central, free on all sides, facing the window, with a floor marking/drain around it; secondary stations belong in a regular row.
  - [medium] Crates (3x3-cell) are scattered at random on the floor (t4 two diagonal crates, t6 one beside the entry, t11) and block sight lines without reason; in a test chamber they should be few, deliberate cover blocks or none, and certainly not right in front of the door.
  - [medium] Cameras are inconsistent: 1 to 4 per room, some mid-wall (t9/t10 east wall, south wall next to the door), some only in a single corner of a 28x18 room. Expect one in each corner (or at least two diagonal) plus one over the door, always.
  - [medium] Missing details a real chamber has: floor drain, hazard/line markings, eyewash and extinguisher by the door, decon sprayer, intercom, warning lamp over the door, a storage cabinet for test material, wall-mounted monitors.
  - [low] Entry door opens straight onto the test pad line (pad directly in the door axis in t5/t6/t9): keep the door aisle clear and put the subject object off-axis or centred far from the door.
### corp_lobby
- Status: done
- Blind design (10 sketches, 12x18 up to 30x20, low to luxury): flow door -> (visitor kiosk) -> metal-detector arch / bag scanner -> a row of equally spaced badge gates (one wide, accessible) -> elevator bank on the far wall; guard desk beside the gate line facing the entrance; logo wall visible from the door; reception/visitor desk facing the door with 1-2 staff behind; waiting lounge (sofas around low tables, facing reception or the windows) off the main path; plants flanking, camera at the door, fire extinguisher by exits; larger/luxury lobbies add a two-stage security zone, several guards, a security booth, art/fountain and a separate visitor-processing area; low-wealth lobbies get a barred guard window and bare benches.
- Findings (10 renders, 14x10 to 28x24, low to luxury, corporate/aaa security):
  1. Does not read as a corp security lobby. The room is a huge empty floor with one oversized reception block (about 9x3, with the room label on it, one chair behind) floating in the middle and a small gate cluster near the door. There is no logo wall, no elevator bank, no detector arch/scanner, no visitor kiosk, no waiting lounge. Needed: a clear entry -> screening -> gates -> elevators sequence along the room's depth.
  2. Badge gates are the wrong shape and place. They are 2-3 parallel strips stacked one behind another (a column), starting beside the door, so they do not form a barrier. They must stand side by side in one row across the room (perpendicular to the walking direction), equally spaced with a clear lane per gate, one wider lane, and a solid barrier or glass wall continuing to both side walls so nobody can walk around them. They also need free space (3+ cells) in front and behind.
  3. The guard desk (a vertical block at the door in the larger renders) sits in the door's swing zone and faces nothing. A guard post belongs at the end of the gate row, facing the gates and entrance, not at the doorway; there should be 2+ guards in bigger/AAA lobbies.
  4. Reception desk is far too big and always dead centre, regardless of room size, with empty space on every side. It should be a long counter parallel to the entrance wall or against the back, sized to the room (3-6 cells), with a visitor/badge-print position at one end, and the staff chair behind it; a visitor stands in front, so the counter must face the door.
  5. Furniture is scattered on the walls with no grouping: single benches, single display cases, a plant per corner, wall screens. Seating must be grouped as a lounge (2-3 sofas/chairs around a coffee table, facing the desk or windows), set off to a side away from the gate path; a bench line is acceptable only in low wealth. Plants should flank doors/desks and corners in pairs, not lone.
  6. Room scales badly: a 28x24 lobby has the same content as a 14x10 one (one desk, 2-3 gates). Larger rooms need more of everything (extra gate lanes, second desk, more seating groups, art/statue, planters dividing zones) or a layout where large halls get split into an outer public zone and an inner screened zone.
  7. Wealth/security is not visible: low wealth and luxury look the same. Luxury needs marble/art/water feature/hologram and more guards; low needs a barred guard window and bare benches; aaa security needs detector arches, bag scanner, armed guards, drones/cameras.
  8. Oddities: a roof hatch appeared in a lobby corner (misplaced for a public lobby); a stray tiny wall item near the door looks like a camera but is unlabeled; wall screens clump in one wall without relation to seating.
  9. Entrance door has a wide recess/vestibule but nothing inside it (no revolving door, no mat, no vestibule screening).
### cubicle_farm
- Status: done
- Blind design: 10 sketches (10x10 to 32x20, low to corp wealth): back-to-back double rows of ~3x3 to 4x4 cubes with a shared spine partition, L-desk in a corner, monitor, chair swung toward the aisle opening, a pedestal/file cabinet per cube or per two cubes, 2-3 cell aisles, a wider cross aisle from the door, a copy/print island and water cooler at row ends, a team/huddle table, plants or glass offices on the window side, supervisor desk overlooking a wage-slave pen, trash and recycling at aisle ends. Renders reviewed: 10 (12x12 up to 40x24, low to luxury; renders in scratchpad cubicle_farm_r/).
- Findings:
  1. Cubicles read as bare slabs: each cube is one wide desk bar plus thin partition lines. No chair is visible in any cube, no L-return, no file cabinet or pedestal, no monitor or terminal detail, no overhead bin. A cubicle needs a desk, a chair at it, and some storage to be recognisable.
  2. Cubes are only separated by thin parallel partition lines and have no back (end) partition and no side return, so they read as open desk rows with dividers rather than enclosed cubes with one opening. Each cube should look like three walls with one opening onto the aisle.
  3. The desk bars sit on the spine and are tiny compared to the cube depth: the large dark floor area between desk and aisle is empty, with no chair, guest chair or pedestal in it. Cube depth and desk should be proportioned (desk fills the back, chair space in front).
  4. The block is rigidly centred with equal wide empty margins (several cells) to all walls, especially in the small and medium rooms (12x12, 16x12, 20x14, 28x18 render 5): big empty bands of carpet with nothing in them.
