Perimeter space should be used (cubes against the walls, row ends against the walls, storage along walls) and the aisles should be tighter and hierarchical (main aisle from door, narrower side aisles) instead of uniform empty frame.
  5. Aisle hierarchy is missing: no main aisle that leads from the entry door through the room, no cross aisle; the one wide gap in the 40x24 room is a vertical gap with stub cubes beyond it, which looks like a mistake (an odd single cube column after the gap) rather than a deliberate cross aisle.
  6. Support items are scattered randomly on the walls at far corners (printer, filing, plant, water cooler) and not grouped. The copy/print station should be one group (printer, copier, paper/supply cabinet, recycling bin) at a row end or near the door, with the water cooler and trash near it, reachable without crossing cubes. Printer count seems fine (1-3), but they are often placed in dead corners far from any cube and some are half hidden in the wall line.
  3a. Missing team space: no huddle/meeting table with chairs, whiteboard or standing area at the end of the rows, no manager/glass office or supervisor desk with a view of the floor. Larger rooms (32x20 and up) especially feel like a pure repetition of identical cubes; supervisor and meeting nooks along the window wall would add realism.
  7. No variety per cube: all cubes are identical. Real farms have some taller/bigger cubes at the ends or window, coat stand, plants, pinboards, trash bins, personal items; a few empty or cluttered cubes. Cyberpunk flavour (cyberdecks, cable trunking, drone chargers) absent.
  8. Orientation: in the tall room (14x20 render) the rows run perpendicular to the others with partitions running E-W; consistent, but the cubes face each other across the spine with nothing at the ends and the block is far from every door; cube rows should align toward the entrance side and keep an aisle that connects the door to the stairs/exit at the opposite corner.
  9. Entry area: the door opens straight into carpet with nothing near it (no reception counter, coat rack, mailbox, or fire-exit route marking). The route door to stair/fire exit is an unmarked open field; keep it explicit as an aisle.
### airlock
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x2 m to 6x4 m, clean-room gowning/air-shower lock): narrow through-room with two interlocked doors on opposite short walls (dirty side / clean side); changing bench across the dirty half with shoe rack and hooks/lockers behind it; a sticky mat or shoe-cover station; a line on the floor separating dirty and clean zones; an air shower cabinet (or two facing nozzle walls) between the two zones; hand wash and sanitizer; gown dispenser and clean-garment shelf plus waste bin on the clean side; status light and card reader at each door; ceiling camera; high/luxury adds a second shower stage, a body scanner, a mirror; low is a bare box with a bench and a locker. Generated rooms (10x8 to 28x20 cells, seeds 1-6, `--room airlock`) and compared.
  - [high] The room is far too big and empty: generated sizes are 130 to 560 cells (6x5 m up to 14x10 m) with only 1-3 lockers and sometimes a bench, so 95 % of the floor is bare. A real gowning lock is 12-40 m2 at most in a pharma plant, usually 6-12 m2 (24-48 cells), and narrow. Cap the size (area ~ 24-60 cells) and fill it; large footprints must grow the contents (more benches, a second stage), not stay empty.
  - [high] No air shower, no mat, no wash/sanitizer, no gown dispenser: the objects that define an airlock are absent; only lockers and a bench exist. Always include an air-shower cabinet (or nozzle wall) on the path between the two doors, a sticky/clean mat at the dirty door, a sink/sanitizer dispenser, a gown shelf/dispenser and a waste bin.
  - [high] Only one door (south wall, centered, 3 wide) and a dead end. An airlock needs two interlocked doors on opposite walls (dirty and clean side) with a straight 2-cell lane between them; one door makes it a changing closet. The second door must be to the clean room, not on the same wall.
  - [medium] Bench is a 1-cell-wide strip of 7 cells against the east wall in a random place (seeds 2, 5: bench runs down the side wall from the middle of the room, nowhere near a locker). The bench must sit with the lockers: a bench across the dirty zone in front of a locker bank (lockers in one continuous run on the wall, bench 1 cell in front, 1.2 m aisle), not apart.
  - [medium] Lockers are single, scattered individually on random walls and corners (seeds 1, 3, 4, 6: one locker top corner, another bottom, a third mid wall). They should be a continuous bank of 4-8 on one wall, in the dirty half, with the clean half having only garment shelves.
  - [medium] No zoning: dirty and clean halves are indistinguishable. A floor line / different floor material or a bench barrier across the room splitting dirty from clean, with the air shower at the boundary.
  - [low] No cameras, door readers, status lights, mirror or signage; no wealth variation (low: bare bench; high/luxury: two-stage shower, scanner, mirror).
### icu
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m 4-bed unit to 20x14 m 16-bed ICU, incl. glass-box single rooms, open bay with central nurse station, U/ring layout, neuro/cardiac and Shadowrun cyber-ICU): beds with the head to a wall, each bed with a ventilator, monitor and IV pole at the head and 1 m free on both sides and 1.5 m at the foot; a central nurse station with a view of every bed; sink/hand-wash and sanitizer at the entrance; medication/supply cabinets and a crash cart near the door; isolation rooms with an anteroom; wide double door for bed transport; a window per bed or day-light bay; Shadowrun: cyberware diagnostic rig, deck/trauma-patch dock. Generated 6 rooms (17x12 to 32x22 cells, middle wealth, seeds 1-6) as a single room and compared.
  - [high] Beds are not in bays: beds stand scattered with head to the north or south wall, some freestanding mid-room (south row on 17x12), some on the west/east wall sideways, with vast gaps and no regular spacing (seed 6: 3 beds on the south wall 9 cells apart, others alone on the sides). An ICU needs beds in a regular row along the window/outer walls with equal spacing (~3.5 m centres), head to the wall, each at the same distance, and the foot side free. Count must grow with area: 704 cells (176 m2) holds only about 9 beds; a real ICU has a bed per 20-25 m2 plus nurse station.
  - [high] No nurse station: the most important ICU element is missing; the centre of every room is empty floor (60-70 % bare in seeds 3-6). Add a central or wall-side nurse desk with chairs and monitors/terminals that sees every bed, and a crash cart and med cabinet beside it; use the empty middle for it.
  - [high] Ventilators/machinery are in 5x3 blocks 1-2 per room placed in corners or along the wall far from any bed (seed 5: two 3x6 machinery columns on the east wall, seed 6 three blocks on the north wall); equipment must stand directly at the head of each bed (ventilator, infusion pumps, monitor on a boom) so every bed is a unit. Vital monitor and IV stand are mostly correct at the bed head/foot; a few monitors are only near beds, fine, but beds without them occur (the west beds of seed 6 have a bare bed). Equipment per bed 1:1.
  - [medium] Missing hygiene and access: no hand-wash sink or disinfectant dispenser at the door (the first thing in every ICU), no isolation anteroom/glass-box rooms, no trash/biohazard bin, no linen cart; the door is a single door in the south wall corner, while bed transport needs a wide double door. Add a sink at the door and sanitizer dispensers per bed pair, and give the entry door double width.
  - [medium] Windows: the room is marked windows required, and the outer walls here have windows, but beds are not arranged relative to them (the north row sits under windows, the south row too, the west and east rows against walls); one escape route warning (building code: icu has one escape route and no rescue window) appears in every seed; an ICU is a protected-in-place area and needs a second route through a horizontal-evacuation door or a balcony; add a second door in the opposite wall.
  - [medium] Medical cabinets appear only in one stack of 3 cells at a side wall, far from the door (seeds 3, 5, 6); meds and supplies belong next to the nurse station or by the door, and a crash cart/defibrillator and a clean-supply shelf should be visible in all sizes.
  - [low] Few extras for flavour: no privacy curtains/dividers between beds, no wall clock, no visitor chair beside the bed, no computer on wheels, no cyberware rig or deck dock for the Shadowrun setting; dive/low wealth should have fewer pumps and no monitors on some beds. Wide-open hall look on the large sizes: split into bay groups of 4 beds with a nurse station between.
### imaging
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a control zone: a lead-glass booth/console with operator chair beside the door, looking onto the scanner; (2) a recognisable CT/MRI setup (gantry with patient table, equipment cabinet, not one lone block in the middle); (3) patient support (changing cubicle, bench, aprons, sink); (4) room size and wealth scaling.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 7x6 m clinic X-ray to 14x10 m corp radiology suite): X-ray room with tube-arm table, wall bucky, apron rack, lead-glass control booth with console and chair by the door; CT room with gantry on a patient table, a control room behind a window (shared wall), power/cooling cabinet, contrast cart, sink; MRI with a shielded door, a wall-mounted cabinet and a "quench" panel; large suites with 2 modalities, changing cubicles, a waiting bench and a viewing/reading desk with 2-3 monitors; Shadowrun flavour: a cheap street-clinic X-ray with a crate and patched cable, corp scanner plus matrix console and drone arm. Generated 18 rooms (hospital, 12x10 / 20x14 / 30x18 cells, seeds 1-6) and compared.
  - [high] The control zone is missing: the single terminal ends up on the wall opposite the door (or 3-4 cells from the scanner) with the chair floating 2 cells below it, so the operator sits in the open at the far end of the room. The operator console must stand next to the door side, facing the scanner, preferably behind a lead-glass partition/booth, with the chair tucked directly at the desk.
  - [high] The scanner is one block in the room centre (alone in 280-cell rooms, the whole ring around it is empty). A real CT/MRI has a gantry plus patient table that is aligned to the room axis, its head end towards a wall, with an equipment cabinet beside it and clear 1 m access on both sides; here it is a single rectangle with no table, no cabinet and no relation to the door. Add a patient table at the scanner, and align it along the long room axis.
  - [high] Items scale poorly with size: 120-cell and 280-cell rooms hold the same 1 scanner and 1-2 terminals. Large rooms should get a second imaging station (X-ray with wall bucky), a reading desk with monitors, a waiting/changing area, or the room should be capped smaller (area 60-140 allows 14x10 m with 80 % empty floor).
  - [medium] Patient support is missing: changing cubicle, bench or chair for the patient, lead aprons rack, sink, trash bin; the `chair` is only the operator seat, and appears in about half of the seeds (0-1), so in many rooms there is no seat at all. Make the operator chair mandatory when a terminal exists.
  - [medium] The medical cabinet appears in only about half of the rooms (count 0-1) and then in a random wall position (next to the door or in a corner). It should be in a fixed spot (beside the scanner or the door) and always present as the equipment/contrast cabinet.
  - [medium] Wealth has no effect: middle and luxury look the same; high should add a second terminal bank/wall screen and a console, low should look improvised (old scanner, crate, single cheap stool).
  - [low] No safety details: radiation warning sign at the door, door lead-lined (heavier door) - Shadowrun imaging often has a camera and an alarm panel; no light/trideo wall screen showing the scan.
### cafeteria
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m break room to 20x14 m works canteen): serving line (counter with tray rail, hot/cold display, till) along one wall or short side, near the door but not in the entry line; queue lane 1.2 m along the counter; dining zone with 4-seat tables (or long bench tables in rows) with 1.5 m aisles and chairs on both long sides; a tray/cutlery/dish-return station at the exit; vending machines and a coffee machine in a corner or alcove near the entrance, bins beside them and at the return; sink/handwash by the door; microwaves on a side counter; windows on the long wall, a pass-through to a kitchen behind the counter; large halls get a second door, a small stage/notice board, a couple of plants. Generated 6 rooms (factory, middle, 16x12 to 40x24 cells, seeds 1-6) and compared.
  - [high] The serving line is missing: 1-2 `counter` pieces (7 cells wide, one cell deep) stand on arbitrary walls (seed 3: one on the north wall mid-span, one on the south wall; seed 4/5: lone block on the south wall beside the door; seed 2: on the west wall) with no tray rail, food display, till, queue lane or kitchen behind. A canteen needs one coherent serving line (counter plus display/till plus staff chair) on one wall or short side, away from the door line, with a free queue lane in front, and a kitchen/pass-through door behind it if a kitchen room is adjacent.
  - [high] Seating is a rigid lattice with the wrong proportions: every table (3x3 cells) has just 2 chairs on its north row and 2 on its south row, nothing on the long sides; tables stand with 3-cell gaps in both directions, evenly over the whole room including right up to the walls, with an empty strip 5-8 cells deep on the entrance side. Real canteens have 4-6 chairs per table (both long sides), long tables or table groups pushed together in rows, wider cross aisles every few rows, and tables kept clear of the serving line and of the door zone. Chairs should be 1:1 with a table side and not form a "c c / ttt / c c" pattern repeated verbatim.
  - [high] No main aisle: the lattice has the door at the south wall on the left, and the first table rows start at x=8 with an unbroken aisle only between the table columns by chance (seed 2 and 3 show a central 4-cell gap only because of the width; seed 1/4 have none). The route door to counter to tables to exit should be a defined 2 cell wide aisle, with the serving line queue running beside it.
  - [medium] Fixed fixtures are thin and misplaced: vending machines are placed anywhere on a wall (seed 3: mid east wall, seed 5: west wall block 3x1 in the middle of the seating zone), trash bins sit in corners on the far side from the exit, coffee machine appears in one of six seeds. These belong together as a "drinks corner" next to the entrance (vending, coffee, bin side by side, bin also at the tray return) and not in the dining space. Add a tray/dish return station near the exit, a sink/handwash and microwave counter, water dispenser.
  - [medium] Scaling: a 40x24 room (960 cells, 480 m2 at 0.25 m2 per cell) gets ~70 seats and 2 counter blocks, a 16x12 room gets 8 tables-halves; the number of counters and vending machines does not grow with the number of seats (1-2 flat). A 200-seat canteen needs 2 serving lines or a longer one, 3-4 vending machines and 2-3 bins; per-seat scaling (`per: 40` seats) is needed.
  - [medium] Large rooms are one flat field: no zoning (a quiet corner with sofas, standing tables by the windows, a stage/notice board, plants for high wealth). Wealth does not change the look: middle/high/luxury hospital or office canteens all get the same plain tables; add booth seating or round tables and plants for higher wealth, and plain long benches for low/factory.
  - [medium] Only one entry door with `door_width: 3` and the building-code warning "one escape route and no rescue window" in all 6 results: a 480 m2 assembly room with 70+ people needs a second exit (ASR A2.3 / VStättVO above 200 persons; 2 exits above 100 m2) on a different wall. Make the second door a required property for areas above ~120 cells and put it far from the first.
  - [low] Windows `required` are drawn but nothing responds to them: tables and counter ignore the window rhythm; vending machines on window walls would block them. Keep fixtures off window wall segments; put window tables (standing counter) along them.
  - [low] Missing details: no wall screen/menu board above the counter, no clock, no cleaning cart, no cameras for high wealth, no trash/recycling pairs, medkit appears in only some seeds (one in the middle of the south wall in seed 2, far from the counter and the exit); the medkit/fire extinguisher should be near the serving line (kitchen risk).
### storefront
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 7x5 m to 12x8 m: pawn shop, noodle bar, news kiosk, pharmacy-front, electronics repair, tattoo/cyberware front, vape/convenience, sim-card shop, gun-less pawn, ramen counter with back door to the clinic): glass front with a door and shop window display on the street wall; sales counter on the side wall opposite or beside the door, 1 m behind it a till, stool/chair, shelf wall and key cabinet; display cases (glass) in front of the counter or as a row on one side; shelves along the other side wall; one or two free-standing gondolas/tables in the middle; a customer aisle of 1.5 m from the door to the counter; a door to the back room (clinic) behind the counter; cameras, a sign, bell/security grille, trash bin; noodle variant: counter with 4-6 stools, tables, kitchen wall behind. Generated 6 rooms (14x11 to 24x16 cells, seeds 1-6) and compared.
  - [high] The hall is mostly empty: 154-384 cells hold 5-15 objects, the middle 70 % is bare floor, only the perimeter is used. A shop fills with size: free-standing gondola/table rows or display tables in the middle, more shelf runs and display cases (count grows with area, e.g. `per`-based, not capped at 2), so that it reads as a shop and not as a hall.
  - [high] The counter is placed randomly: a 7-cell counter in the top-left or hugging the side wall (s4, s5, s3, s6: a 7-cell counter along the side wall, far from the door or not at all, s1/s2 none). The counter is the heart of the room: it must always exist, stand on the wall opposite the door or beside the back-room door, with the staff side towards the wall (stool/shelf behind, key cabinet, till) and customers in front, 3+ cells from the door; it should not sit in the corner next to shelves with a 1-cell gap.
  - [high] No stool at the counter in most results (the stool is optional `count: [0, 1]` and was in 1 of 6), and no till/terminal; the counter should have a staff stool or chair behind it, plus a terminal/till and, for pawn shops, a display case in front of or behind it. Make the stool mandatory.
  - [medium] Display cases (0-2) are tiny rows `ddddd` jammed in corners or against the street window wall; they should stand at the counter (glass counter), or one row near the door; a pawn shop is defined by display cases. Raise the count for a storefront and place them in front of the counter.
  - [medium] Shelves stand in triples `sss sss sss` in a row on the street/entrance wall, in front of the windows (s2, s5, s6), blocking the shop window display and looking like a warehouse; shelves belong on the side and back walls, and the window wall should stay free for display tables/mannequins.
  - [medium] Vending machines and ATM end up in far corners next to shelves (s4: ATM+vending in the SW corner, s6 vending in the SE corner); a vending machine/ATM should be next to the door inside (visible, near the entrance), and only in convenience-type fronts.
  - [medium] No route/zone structure: nothing separates the customer area from the staff area (no counter line across the room, no door to the back room behind it), so the shop front reads as one open box; a counter line with a gap and the door to the clinic behind it would give the room its logic (the street doc's back room must connect behind the counter, not in the customer area).
  - [low] Missing small details: cameras, trash bin, a sign/poster, door mat, cart/basket stack at the door, a bench or tables for a noodle bar variant (stools only 1 here); noodle-bar variant should have a counter with 4-6 stools and 2-3 small tables.
### recovery_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m two-bay PACU to 9x7 m eight-bay ward, incl. a street-doc aftercare room): beds in a row (head to the wall) or two facing rows with a 1.2 m central aisle and 1 m between beds; each bay with a vital monitor, IV pole and O2/suction outlet at the head; nurses' station/desk with a view of all beds near the door; wall sink and hand-rub, med cabinet/crash cart by the door; curtains or glass between bays; door wide enough for a bed (2 m) next to the OR; Shadowrun flavour: cyberware/trauma kit, drone medic. Generated 12 rooms (10x6 to 16x12 cells, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] Beds are scattered, not arranged: they stand as 3x7 and 7x3 cells in mixed orientations (g seed 1 16x12: vertical bed top, horizontal beds east and west), some bed groups butt side by side (3-wide blocks of 2-3 beds at 10x6), others are alone in the room centre area. A recovery room needs all beds parallel, heads on one wall (or two facing rows), equal spacing (>= 1 m between beds), and the foot end facing the door/aisle so a trolley can reach each bay.
  - [high] No bedside kit: IV stands drift on their own (seed 2 14x8: an iv_stand in the open floor, far from any bed; seeds 3/4: stands in room corners, 2 for a single bed, none for others). Each bed needs a monitor and an IV stand at the head/flank, 1:1, directly beside it; the `at: bed` rule only applies to one bed. Add vital_monitor per bed and a sink.
  - [high] Too few beds and too much floor: 112 cells (28 m2) holds 3-4 beds, 16x12 (48 m2) holds 4-5 and the middle of the room is bare (seed 1/3 16x12: one bed top, one bottom, the 6 m centre empty). Beds per area should be ~1 per 7-8 m2 including aisles (so 6 in 48 m2), arranged in rows, rather than 1 per 15 cells.
  - [medium] Missing fittings: no nurses' desk/station near the door, no wall sink/hand-rub, no crash cart, no oxygen/suction panel, no cameras, no curtains/dividers; the medical cabinet appears in only ~half of the rooms and as a 3x1 block in a random wall (seed 2 14x8: east wall mid, seed 4: top-left corner). Always place sink, cabinet and a desk by the door; wealth-gated cyberware rack or drone dock for Shadowrun flavour.
  - [medium] The room is generated with a window wall (windows `"` on the long sides) next to the OR, and the door sits in the south wall mid-way, so the bed in front of the door (seed 4 10x6: bed block at the east centre directly opposite the door) can block the entry. Keep a clear door-to-bed route of >= 1.2 m, door aisle clear, bed heads away from windows/draughts, and keep the door wide (it is 7 cells, fine) with the OR door near it.
  - [low] Small 10x6 rooms (60 cells) pack 5-6 beds in two blocks with no walkway in between (seed 1: three beds wall to wall + one block of two); drop beds rather than touch them. Beds should keep a 1-cell gap each.
### back_room_or
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m squatter back room to 10x8 m clinic OR in a street doc): one operating table (3x1 m up to 2x0.9 m real) centred, with a surgical lamp above; anaesthesia/monitor/IV stand at the head, instrument tray (Mayo stand) and a scrub sink by the door; 2 medical cabinets and an instrument table on the walls; a bin and a sharps/waste bin; stool for the doc; diagnostic/deck rig, drone arm or cyberware rack for the Shadowrun flavour; cheap variants add a crate of supplies, a folding chair and a tarp floor; the door side is the clean approach with 1 m free around the table. Generated 12 rooms (street_doc, 20x16 and 28x20 cells, seeds 1-6) and compared.
  - [high] Table is oversized and always bare in the centre of the room: a 3x7 cell (1.5x3.5 m) operating table, in a 20x15 cell room (about 75 m2, far above a street-doc OR of 20-30 m2). Real table is about 0.8x2 m; the room is far too large, so 80 % is empty floor. Shrink the table footprint (3x5) and the room area (a back room should be ~24-40 cells... currently `area: [40, 100]` yields 80-250), or fill the rest with a real kit.
  - [high] Staff and kit are missing at the table: no surgeon stool, no instrument tray/Mayo stand, no surgical lamp, no anaesthesia gear; `vital_monitor`/`iv_stand` appear only 50 % of the time, and in several seeds (1, 3, 4 at 14x10, 6) none at all, so the table has nothing next to it. The monitor and IV stand should always flank the head end, a tray at the foot/side, lamp over the table.
  - [high] Wall items are scattered with no logic: medical cabinets in 3x1 strips on random walls, in some seeds three separate groups (seed 1: north, west, south-east), machinery blocks (5x5 cells) in the middle of a wall, crate against wall, sink at 3 cells in a corner or on the opposite wall to the door. Cabinets should be one run on the wall opposite the table's head or beside the door; the sink next to the entrance (scrub zone), not in a far corner; machinery (autoclave/generator) as one unit in a corner.
  - [medium] Table orientation and the door relation: the table is always the same orientation (long axis north-south) regardless of room shape, and the foot points toward the door; it should be oriented along the room's long axis with the head at the far wall and a 1 m aisle all round. It is correct that it is centred, but a clear route door-table-sink is not guaranteed (seed 3: sink, rack, crate and cabinet all in the south).
  - [medium] Counts do not scale with room size: a 28x20 cell room gets the same 1 table, 1-3 cabinets and 0-1 rack as the 20x16 room. Add a second item group when large (instrument table, second cabinet line, deck/diagnostic rig, drone arm) or cap the room size.
  - [medium] Shadowrun flavour is thin: no medical-scanner/deck terminal, no autodoc, no drone arm, no biohazard bin; `cyberware_rack` only 0-1 and often alone on a wall, with no connection to the table. Add a tool/diagnostic rig on a wall with the monitor, a rack of chrome parts beside the cabinet, a biohazard/waste bin next to the sink.
  - [low] Trash bin shows up only 0-1 times and occasionally in a random corner far from the table; a basement back room (`count: [0, 1]` in basement) has no variant for a cheaper look (tarp, crates, folding chair); crates standing in the middle of the empty floor (seeds 1, 3, 6) read like debris.
  - [low] Lighting: single light `radius: 7`, fine; the dark corners of the large variants are not covered.
### shop_floor
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 7x5 m corner shop to 18x12 m small supermarket): door on the street front with the checkout counter right beside it (customer queue lane, cashier stool, lottery/cigarette wall and security mirror behind the till); 2-6 shelf rows (gondolas, 3-4 m long, 1.2-1.5 m aisles) running parallel, perpendicular to the front; wall shelving on side walls; drinks fridges along the back wall (a long continuous run), freezer chest/ice-cream, a hot-food/coffee counter, magazine/newspaper rack at the door, ATM and vending machines in the entrance zone, sale baskets/endcap displays at the aisle ends, a lockable stock door behind the counter, cameras, a shoplifting mirror; Shadowrun flavour: credstick reader, ad holo-screen, armoured counter glass, a guard/sentry drone. Generated 9 rooms (14x10 to 36x22 cells, seeds 1-3, stuffer_shack hall) and compared.
  - [high] The checkout floats in the middle of the room: it sits mid-front 4-5 cells in front of nothing, 7x3 cells with free floor on all sides, not beside the door and not against a wall or with a cashier behind. A till belongs next to the entrance on the side wall (or counter perpendicular to it), with a queue lane in front and the cashier side towards the stock door; no customer should pass behind it. Also no cashier chair/terminal beside it (the cashier_booth is a separate room), so it reads as a bare slab.
  - [high] Shelf layout is a rigid, oversized grid: 3 gondola columns by N rows, each 7x3 cells (3.5x1.5 m), identical, with 4-cell wide cross aisles and only a 2-cell gap in the other direction; in 24x16 and larger floors they fill the whole hall like a warehouse, with islands of the same length everywhere. Real shops use rows parallel to one axis (perpendicular to the front), running from the checkout zone to the back wall, with a clear front zone and a clear main aisle from the door; the gondolas should have varied lengths, sit against the side walls too (wall shelving), end with endcap displays, and leave a 2-3 cell main aisle. A 14x10 shop shows only two rows of gondolas: fine, but they are centred in the room with equal margins instead of leaving the front free.
  - [high] Gondolas are too deep and too tight in places: 3 cells = 1.5 m deep with 2-cell (1 m) aisles gives a cramped, barely wheelchair-passable store (< 1.2 m in DIN 18040 shops); the rows that touch the east/west margin leave 6 cells of dead floor at the side. Use 2-cell depth (1 m double-sided gondolas) with 3-cell aisles, wall shelves 1 cell deep along the side walls, and let the area scale the count of rows.
  - [high] Fridges/freezers are 7x3 cell blocks dropped on random walls (north in one seed, in the east wall, in the south-west corner next to the door in the other): one to two chest freezers of a 3.5 m size. A convenience store has a long refrigerated run (open-front coolers, 4-8 m) on the back wall opposite the door, plus a small ice-cream chest at the till. Put coolers on the wall furthest from the entrance, in one continuous row, and never in the entrance corner.
  - [medium] Entrance zone is empty: ATM, vending machine, newspaper/magazine rack, pay station and security mirror are random wall pieces (ATM in the far corner of the south wall in 24x16 s1, vending machines on the west wall away from the door, pay_station only in some seeds). They should cluster in the door vestibule, ATM on the outside-facing/entry wall within the camera view, vending machines beside the door; trash bin next to the exit, not near the freezer far from the door.
  - [medium] Missing items that make it a convenience store: hot-drinks/snack counter, display stands/endcaps and sale bins, shopping baskets near the door, a stock door behind the checkout, wall-mounted cameras, a shoplifting mirror, a drone/sentry, ad screens; wall decor and a window display at the facade. Add by wealth: squatter/low shops get fewer shelves (some half empty), high shops get more fridges and a cafe corner.
  - [medium] Floor of 14x10 puts the door in the south wall far from the checkout (checkout is 3 cells from the west wall, door at x=11 of 14) and ends with the medkit as a lone item; at 36x22 the floor has about 28 gondolas with no route structure. The count of gondolas should follow an area rule (about 1 row per 3 m of width) rather than a pure grid fill, and a main aisle should connect the door to the back-wall coolers.
  - [low] Medkit and trash bin land on arbitrary walls (trash far from the checkout); a fire extinguisher by the door/checkout (shops with > 100 m2 need one per 200 m2, ASR A2.2) is absent.
### factory_floor
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) production lines instead of scattered single items: conveyors run end to end as long lines with machinery/robot arms at stations along them and workers' benches beside; (2) clear zones: goods-in/goods-out with pallet racks and staging near the roller door, production in the middle, maintenance/tool area at the side; (3) marked walkways (forklift route from the roller door, pedestrian lanes) and safety kit; (4) enough density for the size.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 10x10 m assembly cell to 40x25 m hall; wealth squatter to AAA, incl. a drone/robot cell and a Shadowrun corp line): 2-4 parallel conveyor lines the full hall length, each with a machine every 4-6 m and a worker station beside it; robot-arm cells with a safety cage; pallet racks (2-3 rows) and staging pallets at the roller door; forklift lane 3 m wide from the door along the long axis; workbench/tool wall and parts shelves on a side wall; machinery (CNC/press) along the wall; overhead crane rail; fire extinguishers every 20 m, eyewash/medkit, floor markings; columns on a 6-8 m grid; AAA adds drone docks, quality-check station, charging bays. Generated 4 halls (24x16 to 80x40 cells, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] Conveyors are isolated 15x3-cell strips in a regular grid (rows of 2-6 segments with a gap of 9 cells between them), not lines: they connect to nothing, have no machine at either end, and no worker stands beside them. A conveyor line must be one continuous run (or segments abutting) from an input station to an output station; place machinery/robot arms directly at the line, facing it.
  - [high] Robot arms, pallets and forklifts are scattered at random in the free middle (a lone 3x3 robot between two conveyor rows, pallets as 3x3 blocks floating in the aisle). Robot arms must stand at a conveyor (both sides, evenly spaced, inside a cage); pallets belong in staging zones by the roller door and in rack rows, not in the walkway; one forklift parked at a charging spot next to the loading door, not mid-hall (80x40 shows a 3x7 forklift standing alone in the main axis).
  - [high] Machinery is only wall-hugging blocks (5x3) at the north/south walls and in corners, with `per: 60` giving 2-10 pieces; big halls (80x40 = 3200 cells) feel almost empty: ~70 % bare floor. Density must scale: more machine lines, and machine rows parallel to the conveyors; middle of the hall must not be empty between conveyor groups.
  - [high] No circulation logic: the roller door (11 cells) opens straight onto empty floor and the main axis, but no goods-in staging, no marked forklift lane, no clear connection from the door to the conveyor block. Reserve a lane and a staging area (pallets, racks) at the roller door and keep conveyor rows out of the door line.
  - [medium] Missing storage: no pallet racks / shelving (only floating pallets), no parts bins, no crane/hoist, no tool cabinets or lockers; workbenches (7x3) only against walls at random, often adjacent to machinery with no chair/stool. Add rack rows along a side wall and a tool/maintenance corner with benches plus cabinets.
  - [medium] Safety kit thin: 1-4 extinguishers, a hydrant at most once, medkit mostly absent; add eyewash/shower station, emergency stop panels at lines, a second exit (hall over 600 m2 needs exits at <= 35 m travel distance, ASR A2.3) and fire extinguishers at each door/column row (spacing about 20 m).
  - [medium] Columns missing: halls this large have a column grid; they would also structure the layout (lines between rows of columns) and break the flat emptiness.
  - [low] Windows optional only; high clerestory windows or skylights would suit; wealth differences barely visible (squatter workshop vs AAA clean line look the same); gate drone docks, QC stations and charging bays by security/wealth and add oil stains/grime for low wealth.
### control_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a console bank facing the floor window: 2-4 consoles side by side in one row with an operator chair each, a screen wall above/behind; (2) a supervisor station behind that row, rack/UPS corner, door at the back; (3) fewer bare metres; (4) the rigger cocoon as a deliberate Shadowrun feature, not a random wall item.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x5 m small plant booth to 10x6 m main control room): a long window on the wall facing the factory floor; one row of 2-4 consoles (each 2 m wide, with chair, 2-3 screens) parallel to the window, operators looking at the glass; a second raised row or supervisor desk behind; a wall screen/mimic panel on the side wall; server/PLC rack and UPS on the back wall; coffee machine, shelf with binders/manuals, lockers; E-stop panel and fire extinguisher at the door; cameras; Shadowrun: rigger cocoon with a drone/forklift link, deck jack, a matrix node. Generated 6 rooms (12x10 to 30x18 cells, middle wealth, factory, seeds 1-6) and compared.
  - [high] Consoles are scattered one per wall (2 on opposite sides, one in a corner, 5x3 block in the north-west, a 3x5 block on the east wall) and mostly face nothing: no console bank, no common orientation, none looking at the floor window. A control room's defining element is a row of consoles side by side at the window with the operator chairs lined up behind them; place consoles in one adjacent row, facing the floor side/window, spaced about 1.5-2 m.
  - [high] Chairs do not belong to the consoles: a single chair stands 3-6 cells from a console (seeds 1, 2, 4, 5), several consoles have no chair, and the chair is often on the wrong side (back to the console, e.g. at the cocoon). One chair per console, directly at it, on the operator side facing it; the cocoon needs none.
  - [high] The room is a huge bare hall: 120-540 cells with 3-6 objects (seed 6: 540 cells and only 3 consoles, a rack and 2 screens; 80 % empty floor). The YAML area is 24-70 cells, but when the footprint is bigger the room is simply filled with the same small set. The count must grow with area (second console row, more screens, a supervisor desk, lockers, tables) or the room must be capped and the rest become a lobby/corridor.
  - [high] No window to the factory floor: it is declared `next_to: factory_floor` ("overlooks the floor") but the generated room does not use that wall; consoles and screens are not oriented to it and no glazing is required. Require a window/glass wall on the side shared with the factory floor and orient the console bank to that wall; the other walls stay closed.
  - [medium] Missing standard kit: no supervisor desk, no table with chairs for shift handover, no shelf/binders, no lockers, no coffee machine, no UPS/PLC cabinet (only 0-1 server rack, absent in half of the seeds), no E-stop/alarm panel, no cameras, no fire extinguisher at the door. Add 2-3 of these by size, grouped (rack + UPS at the back wall, coffee/shelf at a side wall).
  - [medium] Wall screens (5x1 strips) sit on random walls, often in a corner away from the consoles (seed 5: south-east corner, 4 cells from the nearest console; seed 3: a vertical strip on the west wall next to nothing). Screens belong on the wall in front of the consoles (above/behind the floor window) or directly above each console; give a mimic panel/status wall on the side wall facing the operators.
  - [medium] Rigger cocoon (5x3 block) appears in 3 of 6 rooms on a random wall with no relation to the consoles; it is the only Shadowrun element, but it stands alone in a corner. Place it beside the console bank with a clear 1 m approach and a deck jack/drone dock next to it, only at corporate/high wealth.
  - [low] Door is at the middle of the south wall (consoles and chairs stand 3 cells from it with the door arc); fine, but no object blocks it. Keep the entry aisle (1.2 m) to the consoles clear and put the fire extinguisher/E-stop by the door.
### paint_booth
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a spray booth that reads as a booth: a vehicle (or part) on stands/turntable in the middle of a clearly delimited, filtered cell, with prep and mixing zones around it; (2) the equipment that makes it a paint shop: mixing bench with paint shelves, compressor, spray-gun hose reels, extraction/filter wall, flammables cabinet, suits/respirator rack; (3) objects arranged by function along walls with a free work ring around the vehicle, not scattered; (4) scale with size and wealth.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x3 m motorbike/drone-shell booth to 12x6 m truck/limo booth with prep bay, low-grade garage spray corner to corp-grade downdraft booth): door/roller door on a short wall, vehicle on stands or turntable in the centre with 1 m work ring, filter wall + extraction duct on the far wall, ceiling lamp banks, mixing bench with paint shelves and sink by the door, compressor, 2 hose reels, flammables cabinet, extinguishers and eyewash, drying lamps/infrared gantry, suit/respirator rack and a bin for masking; Shadowrun flavour: painter drone arm, ID-scrubbing/re-plating for stolen vehicles, camo and plate swaps, hidden stash behind the filter wall. Generated 10 rooms (12x8 to 40x20 cells, wealth low to luxury, seeds 1-10, chop_shop) and compared.
  - [high] Almost empty room: 6-8 objects in total (a 3x6 or 5x3 block of "machinery", 3-5 barrels, one extinguisher) in rooms of 100-800 cells (e.g. 40x20: a single machinery block and three barrels, 90 % bare floor). Nothing says paint: no vehicle or workpiece on stands, no mixing bench, no paint shelves, no compressor, no hose reels, no filter wall, no suits. Number and variety of furnishings must scale with area and wealth.
  - [high] No central subject: the room should be organised around a vehicle (car-sized footprint, about 9x4 cells, on stands/turntable) placed centrally with a 2-cell work ring; instead the only large item is "machinery" shoved against a wall or corner, and a few barrels float in the middle of the floor (sometimes in the doorway line).
  - [high] Barrels are scattered at random in the middle of the floor (3-4 single barrels with nothing between them); paint/solvent barrels belong together on a spill pallet or a flammables cabinet against a wall, clustered, away from the door and any ignition source (machinery).
  - [medium] No separation of zones: a real booth has a prep/masking and mixing zone next to the door and the clean spray cell behind it (partition or curtain/filter wall); the generated room is one open hall. Group mixing bench + shelves + sink near the door, spray cell on the far side.
  - [medium] Extinguisher is a single item in a corner (often the far corner from the door); a paint booth needs one at the door and one at the mixing area, plus an eyewash/safety sign and a clearly marked exit path; cameras are missing in the theft-related chop-shop variant.
  - [medium] Machinery block position is random (against any wall, sometimes beside the door) and shown without orientation; extraction/filter equipment should sit on the wall opposite the door with the compressor in a corner, never in the entry line.
  - [low] No wealth differences (low = single bare spray corner with fans, luxury = dust-free booth with downdraft grate and infrared drying gantry); no Shadowrun touches (painter drone arm, plate-swap rack, stash).


### parking_deck
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a working parking floor: bays in regular rows on both sides of 6 m driving aisles, filled in proportion to the floor with cars, ramp placed so the aisle leads to it; (2) structural and traffic details (column grid, wheel stops, lane arrows, one-way flow, entry and exit barriers); (3) pay station, stairs/lift core and emergency exit on pedestrian routes; (4) EV, disabled, motorbike, reserved bays and cameras by wealth.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 20x12 m street garage level to 60x30 m corp podium deck): bays 2.5x5 m at 90 degrees in double rows (nose to nose) with 6 m aisles between, cars in about 60-80 % of bays (more in the slums, fewer in a corp deck), column grid every 7.5-8 m between bays, ramp at one end with a 1:6 slope and a clear turning zone, entry barrier + ticket machine and an exit pay station near the pedestrian core, stairs and lift core by the walls with a marked pedestrian path, extinguishers/hydrant at the core, disabled bays at the core, EV chargers, motorcycle bays at a column row, cameras, lane arrows and signs, drain gutters; Shadowrun: armed guard, drone patrol, rigger bays, a crashed wreck, gangers' cars, shuttered car-elevator. Generated 10 floors (20x12 to 60x30 cells, middle to high, seeds 1-6, parking_garage) and compared.
  - [high] Small and medium decks hold no cars at all (20x12: only a ramp, a pay station and an extinguisher; 24x16: empty hall), while 30x20 and larger fill only two rows along the north and south walls. Bays must be generated in every deck above a minimum size, in parallel rows over the whole floor (a 20x12 cell = 10x6 m floor still fits one aisle and one bay row).
  - [high] Large decks leave a huge empty middle: cars only in two edge bands (rows at the north and south wall), nothing between them but a 14-row empty strip (30x20, 40x24, 60x30). Real decks use double rows (back-to-back or nose-to-nose) separated by 6 m aisles filling the whole level; fill the middle band with additional car rows and keep only the driving aisle and the ramp strip free. In decks with a large gap in the east (60x30 s4) one whole bay column is missing for no reason.
  - [high] No bay markings: the cars are rendered as 7x8 blocks with a 1.5 cell gap but with no line, number, wheel stop, disabled bay or aisle arrows, so it reads as a car shop. Every bay should show lines, bay numbers on columns, wheel stops and directional arrows; at least a few disabled, EV and motorbike bays near the core.
  - [high] No structure: no columns (a deck of this size is a column grid every 7.5-8 m, normally one column between each pair of bays or at every third bay), no stair/lift core, no emergency exit and walls are dotted with fire-resistant window openings only. Stairs and a lift core with a protected pedestrian path are mandatory for an assembly floor and also the place for pay stations, fire alarm, hydrant.
  - [medium] The ramp is a 14-cell wide, ~10-row block of "ramp" cells attached to the south wall, in line with the doors; it has the same size in a 20x12 and a 60x30 deck, takes much of a small deck and does not connect to the aisles (parked cars end on a 14-row empty strip between them and the ramp). Make the ramp one or two lanes wide (about 3 m per lane), with arrows, barrier gate and a straight approach from the aisle; in small decks use a single-lane (or one-way) ramp.
  - [medium] The pay station / ticket machine is in a random corner or wall edge (seed 4: north wall, south-east corner; seed 5: three stations spread over the floor), usually away from the exit and the stairs. Machines belong at the pedestrian core and at the entry/exit barrier, 1-2 pieces, never far from a stair.
  - [medium] Extinguishers sit at the building edge or inside the car rows (30x20 s2: two extinguishers inside the north car band), not at the columns or the core; hydrant/standpipe is absent. Place them along walls and at stair cores, every ~25 m.
  - [medium] No cameras, no lights/lamp strips, no ceiling signage and no guard post visible; for Shadowrun, wealth should show as camera/drone coverage, barrier gates and an attendant, and low wealth as oil stains, wrecks and graffiti.
  - [low] Car count and variety: all cars use the same sprite/footprint; vary type (sedan, van, bike) and occasionally leave bays empty (about 20-40 %); mixed rows with a few wrecks or gang cars in low wealth.


### attendant_booth
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a tiny booth sized like one (2x2 to 3x3 m, up to ~3x4 m for corp) with one desk and chair arranged for the job: the attendant sits facing the driveway window with the desk in front, the door on the side; (2) the small kit that makes it a booth: barrier controller, monitor/camera feed, cash drawer or ticket printer, key cabinet, heater/fan, kettle, bin; (3) scale and wealth only in extra screens and kit, not in floor area.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 2x2 m ticket kiosk to 4x3 m corp gate house): window wall facing the vehicle lane (large glass, sliding hatch), desk or counter along the window, office chair behind the desk facing the window, door on a side wall away from the lane, PC/monitor bank with camera feeds, barrier-arm control panel and ticket/ID reader on the desk, cash drawer, key cabinet and small shelf on the back wall, heater, fire extinguisher, coat hook, bin, mini fridge/kettle, notice board; larger gate houses add a second desk, locker, small toilet and a weapons cabinet; Shadowrun: panic button, armoured glass, drone dock, deck/RFID reader, guard with shotgun in the corp variants. Generated 8 rooms (4x4 to 12x8 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-8, parking_garage) and compared.
  - [high] Wrong size: the room scales with the footprint (up to 12x8 cells = 6x4 m, 96 cells, mostly empty floor) and it can also be tiny (4x4 cells = 2x2 m) at which it fails the 8 m2 building-code warning on rooms under 8 m2 for a workstation. A booth must be near a fixed size (about 3x3 m or 6x6 cells); big sizes should be capped or grow only as a second room/office, small sizes allowed as a kiosk exemption.
  - [high] Almost nothing in it: one console desk and one chair in every seed (plus 1-5 wall screens in high/luxury, nothing in low). Missing the barrier control, ticket/cash drawer, monitor on the desk, shelf/key cabinet, heater, bin, extinguisher, camera; at least 4-6 pieces of kit even in low wealth.
  - [high] Desk and chair arrangement is not job-oriented: the desk is a 3x5 or 5x3 block pushed into the north-west corner, with the chair 2-3 cells away beside it or at a diagonal, not behind the desk and not facing the window (seed 6, 7, 8: chair sits next to the desk end). The chair must be behind the desk, close, facing the desk and the window wall; the desk should run along the window wall that faces the driveway, not stand in a corner.
  - [medium] Window and door relation: the door is on the opposite or the near edge while the "window" wall (=== segments) is the wall the desk uses only by chance; a booth needs a big glass front toward the vehicle lane, a hatch or window next to the desk, and the entry door on the side or back, never at the window wall and not opposite the desk.
  - [medium] Door width: 8x6 high-wealth booth gets a door the full width of the south wall (13 cells = 6.5 m, seed 3, 12x8 too) like a garage door; a booth has a single 1 m (2 cells) personnel door. Doors of 3 cells (1.5 m) elsewhere are also too wide.
  - [medium] Wall screens in high/luxury sit in a line on the east/west wall or as a 4x1 stack on the north wall (seed 8 top right, seed 4 whole west wall with 14 screens) with no desk relation: it should be a bank of 2-4 monitors over/behind the desk facing the chair, not a screen wall on the far side. Seed 4 is also overdone (a whole wall of screens in a booth).
  - [low] No Shadowrun flavour: panic button, armoured shutter, drone dock, RFID/ID reader, shotgun rack for corporate variants, or - in low wealth - a hand-written sign, space heater and a baseball bat.
  - [low] A toilet is in the building list for the garage but not tied to the booth (no staff toilet next door); and the booth does not have to be a room inside the hall: it could sit at the entry/exit barrier, which is how the garage should place it.


### workshop_floor
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a working garage/machine shop with a clear work flow: vehicle bays (lift or floor spot) in a row with 1.5-2 m between vehicles and a drive lane from the roll-up door, benches and tool racks as a work zone along the walls; (2) scale item count and mix with size and wealth, not just more of the same cars/lifts; (3) tools next to where they are used; (4) safety and flavour details (extinguisher at the door, eyewash, oil/scrap bins, Shadowrun chop-shop kit).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 7x5 m squatter back-yard garage to 25x15 m high-end chop floor): wide roll-up door on one short wall with a free drive lane; 1-6 vehicle bays side by side (car on floor or on a 2-post lift, 2 m gaps for doors and tool carts); workbench with vise and a pegboard tool wall; rolling tool chests, parts shelving and a tire rack; compressor/air hose, welding corner with screens, oil drain and used-oil barrels, scrap/parts bin; fire extinguisher, medkit and eyewash near the door; luxury adds diagnostics terminal, engine hoist, paint/spray booth, clean tile; Shadowrun: stripped cars with ID plates removed, drone/cyberware parts crates, rigger interface bench, cameras and a lookout. Generated 9 floors (14x10 to 40x24 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-7) in the chop shop and compared.
  - [high] Vehicles are packed like a car park, not a workshop: in 24x16 and 30x20 floors cars (7x14-char blocks) stand in solid blocks of 3x4 and 4x5 with a gap of only 1.5 cells and no lane, with car lifts butted against them (seed 3: 20 cars wall to wall, nothing to drive or walk between). A workshop holds a few vehicles, each with 1.5-2 m free around it, in one or two rows parallel to the door axis, plus a free drive lane from the roll-up door to every bay. Cap vehicles by area (about 1 bay per 25-30 m2) and keep door-to-bay aisle clear.
  - [high] The car lifts are far too big (9 chars wide x 19 deep, bigger than a car) and used in rows of up to 6 at the south or west wall, empty, with no car on them (workshop 14x10, 40x24: only lifts, no vehicle at all). A lift is a two-post unit slightly longer than a car; there should be 1-3 of them, and a lift bay must carry a vehicle, or be omitted in a squatter garage that works on the floor. Seeds with 10-18 lifts and no cars read as a lift showroom.
  - [high] The work zones are nearly empty and misplaced: workbenches come as 3x5 / 7x3 strips scattered along random walls with long gaps (seed 4: benches on four walls), tool racks (5x1) lie on the opposite wall from the benches and the vehicles, and there is no pegboard/tool wall behind a bench. Group them: benches in a continuous row on one wall with the tool rack directly above/behind, a stool or rolling chest at each bench, parts shelving and the tire rack on a second wall, away from the vehicle bays.
  - [high] Floor is bare for its size: 600-960 cells hold 6-10 non-vehicle objects (mostly barrels and crates), so 70-80 % is empty after cars and lifts. Add by size and wealth: compressor, welding bay with screen, engine hoist, parts shelving, tire rack, rolling tool chests, spray/paint corner for high wealth, diagnostic terminal; a squatter garage only a bench, a rack and barrels.
  - [medium] Barrels and crates are single objects in the middle of the floor (seed 3: barrels at 4 spots in the open floor, crates 3x3 in the aisle), not stacked against a wall or in a corner. Used-oil barrels belong together near the lift/compressor, crates as pallets in one storage corner; keep the lane free.
  - [medium] Wealth does not change the look: the luxury and squatter floors differ only in object count. Use clean/tiled floor, wall tool cabinets, lighting and an office/glass counter for high/luxury; oil stains, junk and scrap piles for squatter. A dealer-level shop also needs a customer counter or small office corner.
  - [medium] Safety and flavour details are rare and misplaced: the extinguisher stands in the middle of the south wall far from the door (and absent in some), the medkit appears once, no eyewash, floor drain, fire-blanket, cameras or lookout. Put extinguisher and medkit within 3 m of the door; add a camera and a lookout spot; Shadowrun flavour (drone parts crates, stripped chassis, cyberware cooler, rigger jack) is missing.
  - [low] The roll-up door (11 chars) is the whole north wall of the room and the other door a 3-cell one at the opposite end; fine for a through-shop, but the interior makes no use of it: bays do not face it and the drive lane to it is blocked by lifts in several seeds.
### rigger_workshop
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a rigger's den with the cocoon/jack-in chair as centrepiece next to a bench, with drone racks and docks near it and a clear vehicle/drone work area; (2) fill the room by size: more benches, docks, parts shelves, battery chargers; (3) tools and parts grouped by function (soldering bench, parts wall, charging wall); (4) believable power/cable detail and door access.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x4 m squatter drone nook to 12x8 m corp rigger lab): door on one side with free path; one rigger cocoon or control chair with a deck jack, placed against a side wall with 1 m free in front and monitor wall; 2-4 workbenches in a row for drone repair with chairs, tool pegboard above; drone racks / charging cradles on a wall, 2-8 docks on the floor for ground and rotor drones; parts shelves with bins, battery/charger rack, soldering station, 3D printer; a vehicle on a stand for high end; fire extinguisher, medkit, trash, a camera; Shadowrun flavour: Matrix link/antenna, signal-shielding, trode rig, spare drone shells, cables on the floor, weapons mount stock. Generated 8 rooms (10x10 to 24x14 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-6) and compared.
  - [high] The rigger cocoon, the room's defining item, is missing in half of the rooms (seeds 1, 4, and the squatter room) and, when present, is a 3x5 block standing alone in the middle of the south part (seed 2) or in the bottom corner next to a door (seed 3, 5x? block in front of the south wall), facing nothing and with no deck jack, screen or chair beside it. It must always be present (except in very poor ones, where a chair and a deck suffice), put against a wall with a free approach, and flanked by a monitor wall and the drone racks.
  - [high] The room is a nearly empty hall: 200-340 cells hold 6-10 objects (seed 3: 17x12, benches in one strip, 2 racks, a dock, a cocoon and 90 % bare floor; seed 4: 24x14 with 3 benches, 4 racks and crates around the edge). Add content scaling with area and wealth: more benches in rows, parts shelves, battery chargers, a printer, a test area; a floor-standing drone dock group; or fill the middle with a vehicle/drone work area and a lift.
  - [high] Drone docks are missing or too few: only 1 dock in two rooms and none in the other 6, despite being a rigger shop; docks are what the drones park on. Add a row of docks (2-6 by size) on one wall next to the drone racks, with a charging line, and one near the cocoon.
  - [medium] Benches, racks and shelves are spread over all walls without relation (seed 4: bench groups in 3 corners, tool racks at the west wall, drone racks on the north wall, one bench in the south-west). Group by function: soldering/repair benches together in a row, drone racks and docks on one wall, tool racks over the bench, shelves on the back wall; the cocoon area is separate.
  - [medium] No chairs/stools at the benches and no small items (soldering station, oscilloscope, parts bins, 3D printer, cable reels, battery charger); only generic benches. The repair bench needs a stool and an item mix typical for electronics.
  - [medium] Crates (3x3) float in the open floor (seed 1: next to the entrance path, seed 4: beside a rack), partly in the walkway. Stack them in a corner on a pallet or on a shelf.
  - [low] Extinguisher is placed in the far corner (seed 2, 4, 6: east or south-east corner away from the door); put it near the door. Add a camera and a floor drain/ventilation hood over the soldering bench.
### contraband_store
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a hidden stash room that reads as organised smuggler storage: shelf racks in rows with aisles, crates stacked on pallets in groups, a locked cage or locker wall for weapons/drugs, a safe at the back; (2) one counting/packing table with scale and chairs; (3) security (camera, guard post, hidden or heavy door, lock); (4) scale with size and wealth.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m closet stash to 10x8 m smuggler warehouse vault): single heavy door (steel, often with a peephole/camera), 1 m path from it; steel racks in parallel rows 2-4 shelves high with 1 m aisles, crates and barrels in stacks on pallets along walls; locked cage or wire-mesh compartment for weapons and drugs, safe or vault in the back; a packing table with scale, sealing equipment and a laptop, a chair; boxes labelled and sorted by kind (arms, chips, drugs, BTL, cyberware coolers); low wealth: loose crates and a tarp; high wealth: climate-controlled lockers, scanner/jammer, armed guard, cameras; fire extinguisher is absent in dives but trash and a trolley are normal; Shadowrun flavour: BTL chip crates, cyberware coolers, fake manifests, scanner-proof shielding, a hidden second exit/trap door. Generated 9 rooms (6x6 to 30x16 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-6) in warehouses and compared.
  - [high] Storage is a random scatter of 3x3 crates (and 3x1 shelf stubs) in the open floor, not an organised store: 240-480 cell rooms hold 6-12 crates in loose clumps with no aisles or rows, the 30x16 luxury room only 4-5 objects. Arrange shelf racks in parallel rows with 1-1.5 m aisles, crates stacked in blocks on pallets along walls, one clear route door to back, and add more of these by area.
  - [high] The shelves are tiny (3 cells wide, 1 deep, 1-3 per room) and mostly sit in the room's middle/top edge; a contraband store is defined by shelving. Use long shelf units (4-8 cells) against the walls and as back-to-back islands, filling the room by area (about 1 rack per 8-10 m2 of floor).
  - [high] No locked compartment or counting table: the weapon lockers (2x3 blocks along a wall) appear in only some rooms, there is no cage, no packing table with scale, no chair, no desk, no cooler for chips/cyberware, and the safe appears in only one tiny low-wealth room. Add by wealth: a locked cage or locker wall (weapons, drugs), a safe at the back, a packing table with a chair near the door; Shadowrun items (BTL crates, cyberware cooler, scanner) are missing.
  - [medium] Everything drifts in the centre: the crate cluster stands right in line of the door (seed 4: crates directly on the 6x6 room axis, seed 2: stacks in the middle of the walking path) while the walls stay empty, so the door route is blocked or zigzagged. Keep the door route clear (1.2 m), put bulk against walls and corners, and leave the centre as the aisle.
  - [medium] No security: no camera, guard stand, peephole, alarm panel or second hidden exit; at high/luxury wealth a store behind steel doors needs at least a camera at the door and a guard desk/chair. Add by wealth.
  - [medium] Wealth does not show: low and luxury rooms look the same (crate piles and lockers); low should be loose crates, tarps and barrels, luxury climate lockers, scanner/jammer, display racks and a guard.
  - [low] No small details: no pallets under crates, trash, trolley/pallet-jack, boxes on shelves, floor markings, fire extinguisher. Add a trolley and a pallet jack for larger stores.
### decon_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a directional dirty-to-clean flow: undress bench and lockers on the entry side, a row of shower stalls in the middle, clean-side bench and lockers at the far exit; (2) shower stalls grouped as one line, with a drain floor and a few cheap emergency items (eyewash, waste bin) rather than lone stalls scattered; (3) a compact room that scales with size by adding stalls and lockers, not by growing empty floor.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x3 m closet to 10x6 m corp airlock block): door in from the dirty side, a second interlocked door to the clean side; 1-6 chem/UV shower stalls in a row along one wall (a larger room has a walk-through spray tunnel), an undress bench plus hooks/lockers on the dirty side, a clean bench and lockers on the clean side, hazmat-suit rack, contaminated-clothing/waste bin by the entry, eyewash and emergency shower on the wall, floor drain, a scanner arch or wipe-down station before the clean door; cameras; Shadowrun flavour: biohazard, toxic and radiation markings, astral-ward keyed door, drone wash. Generated 6 rooms (10x8 to 26x14 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-6) in corp_lab and compared.
  - [high] Shower stalls are scattered as 1-4 lone 3x3 blocks on different walls and corners (d4: four stalls at four spots; d6: three stalls on two walls), not as one row. A decon line is a row of stalls side by side along one wall with a common walkway in front, same orientation.
  - [high] No flow dirty to clean: benches and lockers stand at random places (d3: bench strip on the east wall, a locker in the north-east corner, stalls far away at the opposite end) and the room has only one door. Put undress bench, lockers and waste bin near the entry, stalls between, and the clean bench and lockers at the far side with a second (clean) exit door; both ends of the line must be on a clear walkway.
  - [high] Rooms are mostly empty floor: 20x12 and 26x14 cells hold 3-6 stalls and 2-3 lockers with 90 % open space. Item counts must scale with area and wealth: more stalls in a line, longer benches, a locker wall (one locker per user), plus a hazmat rack and a wipe-down or scanner station.
  - [medium] Missing kit that says decon: eyewash/emergency shower, hazmat suit rack, contaminated-waste bin by the entry (a lone trash bin sits in a corner far from the door), floor drain, scanner arch, decon spray tunnel for big rooms, cameras and warning signs.
  - [medium] Lockers are single pieces dropped at random (one or two per room, often in corners or by the east wall); lockers should form a bank of 4-10 along the wall near both benches. Benches should be placed along a wall right before the lockers, not in the middle of a long wall without relation to anything.
  - [low] Small low/squatter rooms still get 3 stalls; a minimal decon is 1 stall plus a bench plus a bin, so reduce by wealth and size.
### subject_holding
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a holding block of identical cells or cages in neat rows, each with its cot, toilet and sink, separated from a guard corridor and observation point; (2) restraint and exam kit (restraint chair, exam table, drain) that shows what the room is for; (3) security: cameras, guard desk/station, a locked sally door, no loose furniture; (4) size scales through more cells, not empty floor.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m two-cell holding to 14x10 m corp test block): a guard station by the door with a monitor wall and a clear line of sight along a central aisle; 2-8 cells (2x3 m) on both sides of the aisle, each with a bolted bunk or cot, a steel combined toilet/sink, bars or glass front; a restraint chair or an exam/gurney bay with a drain; a cabinet for sedatives; cameras in each cell and the corridor; magic suppression or astral-ward floor markings, a shock collar rack, a feeding hatch; higher wealth adds observation glass, an interview table and a sterile bay. Generated 6 rooms (10x8 to 26x14 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-6) in corp_lab and compared.
  - [high] There are no cells at all: beds (7x3, 3x7 cells) are scattered over the whole room with no partitions, so it reads as a dormitory with random bunks; beds stand in the middle of the room, in corners and across the aisle. Rooms must be built from enclosed cells (or cages) with the bed inside, in a row on one or both sides of a central guard aisle.
  - [high] Toilets and sinks are not tied to a bed: a wc sits in a far corner (s3: wc in the south-west, beds 6 cells away) and sinks stand alone along the opposite wall (s1, s4). Each cell needs its own toilet/sink pair next to the bed, in the cell corner away from the door.
  - [high] No guard station, observation window or monitor wall and no restraint chair or exam table; a wall_screen appears only sometimes at random walls (s1 bottom, s4). The room needs a guard desk with a view down the aisle, plus a restraint/exam bay for the Shadowrun subjects.
  - [high] Counts do not scale and do not follow a plan: a 26x14 cell room shows 5 beds, 2 wc, 1 sink and 3 screens, a 10x8 room has 6 beds and 1 wc, so the bed:toilet ratio varies from seed to seed. Counts should scale with area in whole units (cell = bed + toilet + sink + camera), identical for all cells.
  - [medium] Orientation is arbitrary: beds are laid both horizontally and vertically in the same room and do not face the corridor or a camera; all beds in one row should be parallel, heads to the wall.
  - [medium] Missing security detail: cameras in every cell, lights/alarm, floor drain for hosing, sedative cabinet, a sally door with a lock; no loose items (wall screens in the room centre area read like entertainment) in the cells. Magic-suppression markings or a ward would add flavour.
  - [low] Squatter and luxury rooms look the same; wealth should change the quality (bare concrete cages versus a clean white observation block with interview table), not the bed count only.

## office.yaml
### office
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m cell to 6x5 m exec office, squatter to luxury): the `office` room is a 1-2 person room: one desk (or L-desk) with the chair behind it facing the door diagonally, 1-2 visitor chairs on the door side, a shelf or filing cabinet on the wall, plant, bin, optionally a small meeting table or sofa group (high), safe, drinks cabinet, wall screen (luxury/corp), 0.9 m aisles and a free door swing. Generated 10 rooms (11x15 to 23x15 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The room is generated as a bullpen, not a private office: 4-6 desk clusters (13x15 cells already has 5 desks, 23x15 has 6-7) with 8-12 chairs. A room called office should hold 1-3 workstations at most; bigger rooms must get other content (meeting corner, sofa group, shelving, cabinets) instead of more desks. If many desks are wanted, that is what `open_office` is for.
  - [high] Desk arrangement reads wrong: desks are pushed together in back-to-back pairs with a chair at each short end, plus a lone desk beside them at 90 degrees (a T/L cluster), with chairs on the far sides. It is unclear who faces whom, seats sit at the sides of the desk instead of on the working long side, and monitors/keyboards are not visible. Each person needs one desk with the chair on the long side facing the desk, and the chair-desk pairs must have one consistent orientation (all facing the same way or mirrored in clean rows).
  - [high] Clusters are scattered around the perimeter with no relation to door, windows or each other: some desks have the chair back to the entrance, others have their chair squeezed between desk and wall; the big middle of the room stays empty (13x15, 19x15, 23x15 are about 40-50 % empty). Desks should sit in one clear zone (against the window wall or in the far half), with a straight route from the door and the chair facing the door or the window, never the wall behind.
  - [high] No visitor chairs, meeting table, sofa or coffee table: an office without any place for a visitor looks like a call centre. Add 1-2 guest chairs opposite the desk, and in rooms above ~15 m2 a small meeting corner or seating group; high/luxury needs a sofa group, drinks cabinet, rug.
  - [medium] Wealth and style barely change anything: squatter, low and luxury look identical (same grey desks, same few shelves). Squatter: mismatched desk, junk boxes, less storage; high/luxury: bigger executive desk, leather chairs, sofa group, art, plants, wall screen, safe; corp: terminal with deck and camera.
  - [medium] Storage is sparse and poorly placed: 1-2 shelf units in corners, sometimes two shelves back to back in a corner (o7) or right beside the door (o1, o3, o9 shelf next to the entrance blocking the first step). Shelves/filing cabinets belong along a wall in a row near the desk, never in the entry zone; add a bin by the desk and a coat stand next to the door.
  - [medium] Missing detail: only 0-2 plants/bins, nothing at the door (coat hook, umbrella stand), no wall screen or whiteboard, no printer/cabinet, no cable/terminal object on the desk. These small items are what make an office look inhabited.
  - [low] Large rooms (20+ cells wide) have long empty walls with no shelf run; spread cabinets and shelves along the wall rhythm rather than single stubs.
### open_office
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m to 30x15 m, squatter / startup / corp call centre / luxury / decker den): desks in pods or rows (two rows facing each other, 4 or 6 per pod, chairs on the outer long sides), 1.2-1.5 m aisles between pods, a 1.8 m main aisle from the door, printer/copier island, kitchenette or coffee corner, filing cabinets along the walls, plants, 1-2 phone booths or a small meeting nook, whiteboard, server/network nook, desks at the windows. Generated 10 rooms (14x16 to 35x17 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Everything is one monotone grid of identical desk pairs, with no zones: no coffee/kitchen corner, no printer island, no meeting nook or phone booth, no lounge. A real open office has support zones (print/copy, kitchenette, 1-2 closed phone/meeting booths) at the edges or the end of the room; with size these should grow (large halls need several).
  - [high] Empty bands and odd leftovers: rows stop after 3-4 pods and leave a gap, then a single orphan column (p1, p3, p5, p9: 4 pods, a wide gap, then 1 pod), and the area between the pods and the walls (2-3 m on every side) stays empty while the door side has a large dead lobby. Fill the space evenly: either an extra pod or a clear cross aisle, not an unexplained gap or a lone pod; let desk blocks reach the window walls.
  - [high] Aisles: pods are spaced very tightly in the row (pods touch each other side by side, almost no aisle between desks of neighbouring pods) while huge empty zones exist elsewhere; there is no obvious main aisle from the door. Real offices: neighbouring workstations in a row touch (benches) but each row/pod has 1.2-1.5 m aisle, and a clear 1.8 m main aisle leads from the door through the room.
  - [high] Single rows (p1 and p8 lower row, a lone row of desks facing the south wall with chairs between desk and wall) have the sitter with the back to the room and the chair squeezed 1 cell from the wall; a desk row should either sit against the wall with the chair on the room side (facing the wall) with an aisle behind, or be one half of a facing pair. Several 4x2 columns (p4, p10) are rotated by 90 degrees to the rest with chairs at the short ends; pick one orientation per room.
  - [medium] Chairs are exactly one per desk but no desk has a monitor or terminal visible, no partitions or screens between rows, no personal clutter (plant, bin, lamp). Add low dividers between desks, a bin per pod and plants at the aisle ends.
  - [medium] Wealth/style are invisible: squatter and luxury look the same dense rows. Squatter: mismatched desks, fewer chairs, cables, junk; high: fewer desks per area, partitions, lounge corner, plants, art, phone booths; corp call centre: very dense long rows plus a supervisor desk at the head of the rows.
  - [medium] Perimeter items are randomly scattered (shelf stubs, 2-3 bins in a line, screens): wall screens/whiteboards hug a wall with no desks facing them, shelves stand in the entry zone next to the door (p5, p10, p1), and the kitchenette-like fridge stands alone against a wall without counter, sink or table. Group service items (counter, fridge, coffee, table with 2-4 chairs) into one corner, keep shelves/cabinets in a row along a wall.
  - [low] Corner plants all in the corners (4 identical green discs) look like markers; use some by the door or at pod ends. Fire extinguisher/exit is fine.
### meeting_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m huddle to 6x10 m boardroom, squatter to luxury): one rectangular table centred with the long axis along the room's long axis, chairs evenly on both long sides (about 0.7 m per seat) plus head chairs, 1 m clearance behind the chairs, screen/whiteboard on one short wall that the seats face, credenza with coffee/water along a wall, bin, plant, conference unit on the table; small rooms a round table with 4-6 chairs; training room as U tables facing a screen; boardroom with art and drinks; Shadowrun: holo-projector table, camera, keypad door. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 25x19 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Table is far too small for the room: 25x19 cells (12x9 m) and 20x16 contain the same U of three tables (about 12 seats) in a sea of empty carpet; the round table with 8 chairs sits in a 10x10 room and in a 15x13 room alike. Table size and seat count must scale with the room (a 5x8 m room needs a 14-20 seat table); otherwise choose a layout that fills the room (two tables, boardroom table plus side seating along walls).
  - [high] Seating is wrong: chairs stand only on the outer sides of the U (the three tables' outsides), nobody sits inside the U or on the open side facing the screen, and the three top chairs are crowded as two chairs touching in the middle, leaving odd gaps. For a U layout the seats are on the OUTSIDE of the U in training rooms, facing a screen at the open end; for a meeting they are on the inside. Either way spacing must be uniform (about 0.7 m per seat, no touching pairs, no gaps) and each chair must face its table edge.
  - [high] Round table: chairs come in pairs (2 per side, 4 pairs), so they touch each other and leave 1 m gaps in between, and chairs press against the table with their backs flush to the table edge; seats should be evenly spaced around the circle with a hand-width gap to the table and no touching pairs.
  - [high] Screen/whiteboard does not belong to the seating: the whiteboard/screen sits at the entrance wall, on a side wall or in a corner, and not at the head of the table facing it (m2, m3, m7). It must be centred on one short wall (or the wall at the head) and all seats must see it; a second whiteboard may sit opposite, not by the door.
  - [medium] Table position ignores the door and the axis: tables float exactly in the centre with the open U or the table long axis not related to the entrance; the door should land at the back/foot of the table so entering people reach the free seats, with the head chair (and screen) at the far end. Keep at least 1 m behind every chair; no object in the door swing (m2/m3 have clear space but a screen right next to the door).
  - [medium] Missing furnishing: no credenza with coffee/water, no bin, no conference phone/holo-projector on the table, no wall art, no coat hooks; walls are empty (m1, m4, m5, m8 have only plants and a lone small box). A meeting room needs a side cabinet or sideboard along a wall, a bin by the door and a power/AV element on the table.
  - [medium] Wealth does not change anything: squatter, low and luxury have the same table and chairs. Squatter: mismatched chairs, folding table, a few empty cans/boxes, no screen; luxury: one big boardroom table with head chairs, rug, art, drinks credenza, two screens.
  - [low] Plants are in corners as green dots; one at a window or beside the sideboard looks more natural. Door width 4 cells in a meeting room is fine.
### focus_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design: 6-16 m2 single-person quiet room; one desk against a wall with a chair facing it, 1 m free path from the door, plus by wealth an armchair/lounge corner with side table and floor lamp, a plant, acoustic panel/whiteboard on the wall, bin; luxury adds sofa and rug; Shadowrun variant is a decker nook with deck, cable rack and recliner. Generated 10 rooms (8x8 to 16x12, low to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The room is nearly empty: every result is one desk with one chair in a north corner plus (sometimes) one tiny corner item next to the door, with 80+ cells of bare carpet in the middle.
