A focus room must read as a furnished retreat: a lounge chair or small sofa with side table and lamp, a plant, a shelf/whiteboard, bin. Furniture count must scale with room size, and wealth must visibly change it (low to luxury looked identical).
  - [high] Rooms come out far too big for the purpose (up to 15x12 cells, 40+ m2); a focus/phone room is 2-4 m x 2-4 m. Either the room should stay small or, when it is large, it needs a second function (lounge zone, two desks as a pair-focus room) instead of one lone desk.
  - [medium] The single desk is oversized (about 5x3 cells = 2.5x1.5 m) and jammed into the corner, with the chair beside it at the end rather than centred in front of it; a real work desk is about 1.2-1.6 m x 0.7 m with the chair centred on the long side, and the desk should not be pushed into the corner so that the window and wall tiles are blocked.
  - [medium] The one other object (a small dark cube/plant, red marker) sits in a far corner by the door and does not belong to any group; small items should sit next to the furniture they serve (side table next to the lounge chair, lamp by the chair, bin by the desk).
  - [low] Orientation is always the same (desk north corner, door south); the desk should sometimes sit on the wall facing away from the door or with its back to the window, and the seat should not look straight at the door.
### huddle_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design: 8-20 m2 small meeting room for 4-6 people; table centred (round, or 1.2x2 m rectangle) with chairs on all long sides and the ends, 0.9 m clearance behind each chair, a wall screen on a short wall that every chair can see, whiteboard on another wall, conference phone/cable box on the table, plant, credenza/coffee for high wealth, soft-seating variant (two sofas, coffee table, screen). Generated 10 rooms (8x8 to 16x12, low to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] Every result is identical: a small square table in the exact room centre with 4 chairs as two pairs (north and south), and nothing else varies with room size or wealth. A 16x12-cell room still holds just that, floating in a sea of carpet. The table and seat count must scale with the room (6-8 seats, longer table), and the table should be a proper meeting table, not a centred pedestal.
  - [high] The screen/whiteboard (blue panel) sits on the west wall but all chairs sit north and south of the table, so nobody faces it; the seats must be oriented toward the display, with the screen on a short wall at the head of the table, and seats arranged along the long sides plus one at the end.
  - [high] Chairs stand about one cell away from the table edge and are not tucked in, and there are no chairs on the east/west sides; chairs must sit directly at the table (tucked in) and distributed around its perimeter, not only at two sides.
  - [medium] Table floats in the exact centre regardless of door and screen position; it should be aligned with the screen axis, leave 1 m walkway behind the chairs, and not block the door side (the door is always at the south and the south chairs sit in the entry line).
  - [medium] Missing details: no whiteboard on a second wall, no credenza/coffee station for higher wealth, no conference phone or cable box on the table, no bin; only occasionally a lone plant in a corner.
  - [low] Rooms far larger than a huddle (up to about 40 m2) leave the walls empty; either keep the room small or add a second zone (sofa corner, standing bar table).
### balcony
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design: an outdoor strip on the facade, 1-1.5 m deep and 2-8 m long, with a door from the room, a continuous railing on the outer edge, and light outdoor furniture only: a bistro table with 2 facing chairs (or two lounge chairs with a side table), planter boxes along the railing, ashtray/bin, laundry rack or AC unit at one end; luxury adds loungers, parasol, dining set; Shadowrun adds antennas, dishes, drone perch, neon sign, fire-escape ladder. Always a clear 0.9 m path from the door. Generated 10 rooms (10x6 to 30x8, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] It reads as an indoor lounge, not a balcony: padded armchairs, a sofa and a wooden coffee table, i.e. living-room furniture, many of them standing in the middle of the floor. Outdoor furniture should be a bistro table with chairs, loungers, benches or plastic chairs, planters, ashtray, and everything must sit against the wall or railing side, leaving the middle free as the walkway.
  - [high] Armchairs are scattered at random spots and orientations (balcony_4, 6, 10: three or four chairs facing different directions, none facing a table, the view or each other), and the sofa often stands apart from its coffee table. Seating must form groups: chairs facing each other across a table or lined up side by side facing the railing/view, sofa facing its table.
  - [high] Walkway and door: furniture, plants and armchairs are placed right beside or in front of the door (balcony_9: two armchairs directly in front of the opening; balcony_4: chairs and plants at the entrance), so the entry is blocked. A free aisle of 2 cells in front of the door and along the length is required; nothing in the door line.
  - [medium] Plants are scattered one by one all around the edge, including the door corners and the middle of the room, 5-8 of them on short balconies, which looks like confetti. Plants must be grouped in planter boxes along the railing side or at the ends, in rows, and their number must fit the length.
  - [medium] Missing balcony details: no railing or parapet visible as the edge (standalone it is a closed box with four walls), no ashtray/cigarette stand, no AC condenser, satellite dish, laundry rack, bin, drain, grill, parasol; cyberpunk flavour (antennas, neon sign, drone perch, fire-escape ladder) is absent. Wealth hardly changes the result: low and luxury balconies carry the same padded seating.
  - [medium] Furniture is far too large for a 1-1.5 m strip: the armchairs/sofas are 2-3 cells deep and the room is up to 6 cells deep, so it behaves like a small living room; a balcony should be shallow, with furniture occupying one side only.
  - [low] Very long balconies (30 cells) have a single small group at one end and a bare stretch elsewhere; either repeat groups (table-chairs sets) at regular spacing or fill the stretch with planters and benches along the wall.
### training_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x4 m to 14x9 m): classroom rows of double desks (2 chairs per desk, chair pull-out 1 m between rows, 1.2 m centre aisle) all facing a front wall with whiteboard + screen + instructor desk/lectern; door at the back corner so latecomers do not cross the front; U-shape/board-table for small rooms, workshop clusters, computer-lab rows along walls, luxury tiered auditorium; coat rack/cabinet/trash by the door. Generated 10 rooms (12x10 to 30x18 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] There are NO tables or desks at all: the room is only dense columns of bare chairs (every result). A training room is desks/tables with chairs, not a chair-only lecture hall. Give each seat group a table (1 table per 2 chairs, or long tables for 3-5 seats) in front of the chairs.
  - [high] Seating is one chair per cell in a 1-cell-wide line, 5-6 chairs touching each other, with a 3-cell gap to the next column (all results, worst in 24x16+ rooms with 12-13 columns x 6 chairs, 150+ seats). It reads as a theatre/church, not as training; chairs touching in a long file have no pull-out space and no cross aisle. Break the file into short rows (max 2-3 seats per desk block) with a cross aisle of at least 1 m and a clear lane to the door.
  - [high] Orientation does not make sense: the screen sits on a SIDE wall (west or east, seeds 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8) while the door is in the south wall and the chairs are lined up in north-south columns, so there is no clear front. Chairs/desks must all face the screen/whiteboard; the screen wall should be the short wall opposite (or at least away from) the door, rows perpendicular to it, so people enter from the back.
  - [high] The screen is a thin blue strip on one wall only, with no whiteboard, no instructor desk/lectern/podium and no free teaching zone in front of it. Needs a front zone: whiteboard and screen side by side, instructor table or lectern, 2+ m free in front, a projector (ceiling or cart) for the wealthier rooms.
  - [medium] Room is filled edge to edge uniformly with the same grid regardless of wealth or size; middle and luxury rooms look identical except for seat count. Vary by size and style: small (< 40 m2) a single conference/U table, 40-80 m2 classroom rows, big hall rows with a centre aisle, wealthy gets a lectern, stage and AV rack; low wealth fewer, mismatched tables.
  - [medium] Large rooms (24x16, 30x18 cells) have the rows terminating around 2/3 of the width leaving an empty east strip, and the rows are not centred; the seat block should be centred or aligned to the screen axis with a main centre aisle in line with the door.
  - [medium] Missing supporting furniture: no coat rack/cabinet by the door, no storage cupboard for materials, no flipchart, no trash bin, no water/coffee table, no side table for the instructor. Plants (2-3) sit in corners, fine. Add 1-2 cabinets/shelf on a side wall and a trash bin.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour absent: no matrix terminals/jack-in chairs, holo projector, cameras or a locker for trainee gear; add for high/luxury and corporate settings.
### nap_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x2.5 m single-bed cell to 6x5 m pod room): beds head-to-wall in rows with a ~0.8-1.2 m aisle, a nightstand/lamp per bed or between two beds, curtain dividers or pods, bunk beds and recliners as variants, lockers/coat hooks and bench by the door, luxury with sofa/wardrobe/rug/tv, Shadowrun flavour with jack-in beds and medkit; never a bed in the door swing. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 16x12 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] Every result is the same recipe: a full row of identical beds on the north wall touching almost edge to edge (1 cell-wide beds with a hairline gap), plus one or two beds on each side wall and two or three beds left and right of the door. No variety by size, wealth or style (luxury 16x12 looks like squatter 10x10 with more beds). Needs variants: pod/cot rows, bunk beds, recliner loungers, a couple of large beds, sofa corner.
  - [high] Bed density is a dorm/barracks rather than a nap room: 8 to 14 beds with a shared room and no privacy. Real nap rooms have 2-4 beds/pods with dividers or curtains or one bed per small cell; if more, add partition screens between beds and a clear aisle at the foot of every bed (at least 1 m).
  - [high] Beds directly beside the door: the two beds on the entrance wall sit hard against the door jambs, so the head end is next to people entering and the door aisle is only 2-3 cells wide. Keep the entrance zone free (1.2 m either side of the door), put lockers/hooks/bench there rather than beds.
  - [medium] Side-wall beds (head to the side wall, foot into the room) sit across the middle aisle at mid-room, so the passage to the north row zigzags; the long north row beds are separated from the door by the 3-cell free zone with side beds in it. Use one consistent bed direction (all heads to the same wall, or all to the long walls) with a straight aisle from the door.
  - [medium] Missing bedside details: nightstands/lamps only appear as a few grey squares on the side walls, not one per bed or per pair, and none for the north row. Add a nightstand or shelf between each pair of beds, a lamp, coat hooks/lockers by the door, a blanket/linen shelf, a trash bin and a clock/alarm panel.
  - [medium] No sense of dim/quiet room: no curtain, no divider screen, no sofa or chair to wait on, no water dispenser; add for middle+ wealth. Low/squatter should be cots or mattresses on the floor with fewer extras, not identical beds.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour absent: a jack-in bed with trode rack and a medkit/wall panel would fit a corp nap room; windows on the long wall would not hurt realism but are not needed.
### copy_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x2 m closet to 6x4 m print centre): copier/MFP against a wall with 1 m standing space in front and kept clear, printer/shredder/scanner next to the copier in a row, paper/supply shelves directly beside the copiers, recycling and trash bin and shredder within reach, a collating work table (centre or opposite wall) in larger rooms, toner cabinet, notice board; bigger rooms get plotter, guillotine cutter, 2-3 copiers in a row; Shadowrun flavour with data printer, trid scanner. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 16x12 cells, squatter to luxury) and compared.
  - [high] Objects are scattered one by one around the walls and corners with no grouping: copier in the NE corner, shelf in the SW corner, a second copier by the door, etc. (all results). A copy room is one working cluster: copiers in a row on one wall with the paper shelf directly next to them and the bin/shredder at their end; the other wall carries storage. Group them.
  - [high] Rooms are mostly empty floor (10x10 to 16x12 cells with only 2-5 items; seed 1, 7, 5, 9): 25-50 m2 with one copier. Either the room is much smaller or it must hold a real equipment set: 2-3 copiers/printers, plotter, shredder, a central collating table with paper stacks, supply racks. Scale item count with room area.
  - [high] No work table at all; a copy room needs a table to collate, sort, staple and put jobs down (centre or along a wall); add a table with a stack of paper and a stapler/cutter.
  - [medium] Shelves with paper are far from the copiers or on a different wall (seeds 3, 5, 8, 10: copiers north-east, shelf south-west); paper supply must be adjacent to the machines, and a toner/supply cabinet next to them.
  - [medium] Orientation of the copier: units stand with the long side to the wall in random corners/sides; a copier should face into the room with its operating side open and 1 m clear, and nothing (shelf, bin) in that zone. Copiers jammed into corners (seed 5 SE, seed 7) leave no service access at the side.
  - [medium] Entrance zone: shelves/units sit directly beside the door (seed 1, 6, 9, 10: shelf at the door jamb, bin in the entry corner), blocking the swing and the first step. Keep 1.2 m beside the door free.
  - [medium] Missing details: shredder with locked bin, recycling/paper waste bin (small round items in corners are the only bins and appear in a few rooms only), notice board, plant/coffee not needed; add a poster/notice board wall, a second waste bin type, a stool/step for stacks. For Shadowrun: data printer/3D printer, trid scanner, a locked shredder for corp secrets, a camera.
  - [low] Wealth has no effect: luxury looks as sparse as squatter; low wealth could be a lone old copier plus a shelf, wealthy more units and a dedicated print room look.
### kitchenette
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x2.5 m pantry nook to 8x4 m tea kitchen): one continuous counter run (sink, dishwasher, base cabinets, coffee machine, microwave) with the fridge at its end, in a single line or an L/U along one or two walls, 1-1.2 m walkway in front, bin beside the counter; small bistro/high table with 2-4 chairs or stools in a corner away from the door line; water dispenser, extinguisher, notice board; vending machine in bigger ones. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] There is no kitchen counter: stove, sink/coffee unit, fridge and a bar-like shelf are single small items dropped on different walls and corners (k2, k3, k8: hob top-left, coffee top-right, fridge east wall, a long counter west or south). A real kitchenette is one connected work run: sink, hob/dishwasher, coffee machine, microwave and fridge side by side along one wall (or an L), so the user sees a kitchen. Group all cooking/cleaning appliances into one contiguous run on a single wall.
  - [high] Rooms are far too empty and the contents do not scale: a 20x12 or 30x20 cell room still holds 4-5 small items and one table, 80-90 % bare floor (k4, k5, k9, k10). Either shrink the room, or grow the contents with area (longer run, second run opposite, more tables, vending machine, water cooler, shelves, bins); a break kitchen of 60 m2 would have 3-6 tables.
  - [high] Table and stools float in the middle of the room with no relation to the kitchen run: one small table with two chairs diagonally (not opposite each other, k2/k3/k7/k9) in the room centre, far from everything. Seating should be one cluster near the run but outside the working walkway, chairs opposite or on three sides of the table; in larger rooms several tables in a tidy row or grid.
  - [high] Fridge is not next to the counter: it stands alone in a corner or mid-wall (k3, k4, k6, k8, k10) and in k1 it is the only item next to the door. Fridge, sink and coffee unit must be adjacent to each other, and never directly beside the door where it blocks traffic.
  - [medium] Some low-wealth rooms are nearly empty: k1 (squatter) has only a fridge by the door, a bench-like counter on the north wall and a tiny item in the corner, no table or sink at all. Even a squatter kitchenette needs hotplate, sink, a table and a bin.
  - [medium] Vending machine (magenta) is stuck in the room edge or south-west corner beside the door in a few results (k5, k7); it should stand against a wall in the walking zone near the entry, with free space in front, and only in rooms big enough for several users.
  - [medium] Missing details: no bin next to the counter (a lone circle appears in some corners), no water dispenser, no microwave visible, no extinguisher, no notice board or wall cabinets; coffee and microwave should sit on the counter, bin beside it.
  - [low] Counter orientation is inconsistent: a long counter hugging the south wall right beside the door (k10) or the west wall in the middle (k4) with nothing around; the counter should have the open front facing the room and sit on the wall away from the door.
### server_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m closet to 10x6 m corporate hall): one locked door (reader, camera) in a short wall; racks in straight rows with cold aisle (front-to-front) about 1.2 m and hot aisle behind, 1 m free at row ends and along walls; rows parallel to the long axis or perpendicular to the door; cooling (CRAC/split units) at the wall ends of the aisles, UPS/battery cabinet along a wall; a small desk/KVM console or crash cart and workbench just inside the door; fire extinguisher/gas-suppression bottle, EPO button, cameras, sensors; low-wealth rooms are messy (a rack plus loose towers on a shelf). Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 11-20) and compared.
  - [high] Support equipment is missing: apart from one red cooling/UPS-like block (0-1 per room) and a corner circle, there is no desk or console at the door, no workbench, no UPS bank, no second cooling unit, no extinguisher/suppression cylinder, no cameras, no cable tray; large halls (24x16, 30x20, 14x10 cells) have one such block or none (r14, r16, r20). Needs cooling units at the ends of aisles (1 per ~8 racks), a UPS/battery cabinet row on a wall, a small desk and chair or crash cart by the door, an extinguisher and a camera, all scaling with rack count.
  - [high] Rack rows read as a flat grid of identical bars: every row is the same, with the same gaps and all facing the same way. Real halls alternate row orientation (front-front cold aisle, back-back hot aisle) so aisles are visibly different widths (about 1.2 m cold, 1 m hot) and rows are paired; also vary one or two racks (network/patch rack, storage array) so the room does not look like a parking lot.
  - [high] Size does not change the character: squatter, low, middle and luxury rooms all show the same rows of identical racks, only the length differs. A low-wealth server room should be small and messy (one or two racks, loose towers, shelf, fans, cables), a luxury one cleaner with closed rows, cooling and UPS rooms; a 10x10-cell room (5x5 m) holding 12 racks (r11) is a data centre, not an office server room.
  - [high] Clearances at the door and walls: rows run flush from one wall region to the other, with a narrow 1-cell strip at the side walls and only a bare strip in front of the door (r11, r13, r14, r16, r20). A real room has a clear entrance zone with the console and a main aisle from the door to the rear, and rack ends stay at least 1 m from walls so racks can be pulled out; the central gap in wide rooms (r14, r16, r20) should line up with the door as a deliberate main aisle rather than appear as a random hole in some rows.
  - [medium] Counts are too high for an office server room: 20-60 racks in 30-100 m2 is dense data-centre territory and leaves no room for service items; an office has 2-10 racks. Cap racks by area and add free floor, a service area and storage (spare parts shelf, tape/cable cart).
  - [medium] Row ends are ragged in some rooms (r14, r18: last column has only one or two racks; r11: three even rows but an empty last column), which looks like the generator ran out of space. Complete rows to the same length or fill the remainder with cooling/UPS units.
  - [medium] The red/round item in the corner (smoke detector or sensor) is tucked into a corner next to nothing; the fire panel/extinguisher/EPO should be beside the door and a camera should cover the entrance.
  - [low] Rack orientation does not relate to the door: in the narrow room (r19) racks run front to back along the entrance axis, which reads fine, but in the others rows block the view of the door and the side aisles are the only way round; rows perpendicular to the door (parallel to the entry axis) with a central main aisle look more natural.
### archive
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 3x3 m cupboard to 10x6 m climate-controlled archive): shelving in parallel rows with ~0.9-1 m aisles, long axis perpendicular to the entry wall or along the long wall, shelving also lining the perimeter walls, a cross aisle just inside the door, a small reading/work table with a chair or two (and lamp) and a filing-cabinet bank near the door, step stool/ladder, trolley, bin/shredder; larger or richer archives add mobile compactus blocks, scanner/copier, safe or vault cabinets, microfilm reader, camera; low-wealth versions use steel racks with loose boxes. Generated 10 rooms (10x10 to 30x20 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 21-30) and compared.
  - [high] The room contains only shelves: no table or desk to read or work at, no filing cabinets, no step stool/ladder, no trolley, no bin, no safe, no copier/scanner, nothing at the door. A real archive is defined by its work place and cabinets next to the shelving; add a table with chairs and a cabinet bank near the entrance, plus small cart/stool/box details.
  - [high] The shelf layout is a copy of the server room: identical short 4-cell segments, in evenly spaced rows floating in the middle of the room, with bare floor all around (a, b, c all). Real archive shelves are long continuous runs (or double-sided back-to-back rows) perpendicular to the long wall, with perimeter shelving along the walls and no empty band between wall and first shelf; small gaps every 4 cells make every row look like separate boxes.
  - [high] Aisles: every row is a single-sided shelf with the same 1 m gap, so the aisle between two rows has shelf faces on one side only and a blank back on the other. Shelves should be paired back to back (double-sided units) with aisles between the fronts, giving visibly different aisle and back-to-back widths, a main cross aisle by the door, and a straight clear route from the door to the rear wall.
  - [high] Rooms scale only by repeating more rows: a 30x20-cell archive (150 m2) has 36 identical shelves, a 10x10 one 12. Large archives should split into zones (reading area at the door, compactus/shelf block, perimeter cabinets) and keep the aisle layout legible; the central gap in wide rooms (a5, a6, a10) should line up with the door as a main aisle, and incomplete row ends (a5, a8: short last column) should be filled or removed.
  - [medium] No difference between wealth levels: squatter/low and luxury have identical shelves. Low should be steel racks with loose boxes and stacks on the floor, some uneven spacing and a bin; high/luxury climate-controlled with compactus, locked cabinets, a safe, camera and reading desk.
  - [medium] Clearances to walls look arbitrary: a ~1-cell strip at the side walls and a wide empty band at the entrance, but nothing uses the space; either place shelving flush to the walls (no dead strip) or leave a proper 1 m aisle everywhere, and keep the door area free for the table and cabinets.
  - [low] Orientation in the narrow room (a9) is good (rows along the long axis, aisles from the door); the same logic (rows parallel to the entry axis in rooms deeper than wide) should apply in all rooms so the first aisle starts in front of the door.
### executive_office
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x4 m startup office to 12x8 m mega-corp suite, plus a yakuza and a Shadowrun matrix/safe variant): desk with its back to the window wall (or in a corner facing the door) with the executive chair behind it, 2 guest chairs facing the desk 0.8-1 m away, credenza/bookcases along walls, a sofa + armchair + low table group on a rug in the free half, plants in corners, wall screen/art, bar/drinks cabinet and coat stand at higher wealth, 1 m free path from door to desk. Generated 10 rooms (16x12 to 30x20 cells, middle to luxury, office and corp_office, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] Rooms do not fill with size: a 30x20 cell (15x10 m) or 24x16 office holds the same desk, one sofa, one cabinet and two plants as a 12x10 one, so 70 % of the floor is empty carpet. Bigger or richer offices need more content: a conference nook (table + chairs), a second seating group, bookcase wall, bar/drinks cabinet, wardrobe/coat stand, safe, art/screens. Or keep the room smaller.
  - [high] The desk floats in the middle of the room (seen from all sides, 3+ m from every wall in most results). An executive desk stands in a deliberate spot: back to a (window) wall or in a corner, with the executive chair between desk and wall and the guest chairs on the door side. At minimum the desk should be on the room's long axis, parallel to a wall, not free in the centre with the chair floating behind it.
  - [high] Seating group is not a group: the sofa is pushed against a wall, the coffee table touches it directly (no knee space) and there are no armchairs opposite, no rug. A lounge needs sofa + 1-2 armchairs around a low table with about 0.5 m between, a rug underneath, and it should face away from the desk area, e.g. near the door side or in a clear zone, not facing the wall corner. The sofa side tables are tiny discs at the ends, fine.
  - [high] Wall furniture is too sparse and weak: the cabinet/bookshelf reads as a 1x4 grey strip (radiator-like), appears once or twice, sometimes in the middle of a long wall; no real bookcase wall, credenza behind the desk or display case. Executive offices are wall-lined with shelves/credenza behind the desk and a bookcase on a side wall; shelves must not stand in front of windows and should be grouped, not scattered.
  - [medium] Missing typical items: wall screen is rare (only 2 of 10), no bar/drinks cabinet even at high/luxury, no coat stand/wardrobe by the door, no rug under the desk, no wastebasket, no desk items, no meeting table. Missing Shadowrun flavour: deck/holo projector on the desk, safe, panic button, security drone dock, matrix terminal.
  - [medium] Guest chairs: two chairs sit 1 cell from the desk, evenly spaced in a row, fine, but they are the only chairs; the executive chair is a standard round chair. Higher wealth should have a bigger high-back chair, and guest chairs should be a pair slightly offset, facing the desk (they do face it), never behind the desk.
  - [medium] Door relation: the door sits in the corner and the guest chairs face away from it, so a visitor walks round the desk. Place the desk so that the visitor path door to guest chairs is straight and 1 m wide; keep the area in front of the door clear (it is, but only because the room is nearly empty).
  - [low] Windows: windows appear as blue strips in the interior in only 2 of 10 rooms (south or north wall), while an executive office is the one room that should have a window wall behind the desk; rooms with a long exterior wall and no window or blind look dead. Also the plants sit in the corners only; fine, but add a tall plant by the window/sofa.
### board_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m huddle to 14x8 m luxury, plus U-table and Shadowrun holo variants): one table centred on the long axis with chairs evenly on both long sides opposite each other plus a head chair at each end, table length about room length minus 2.5 m, 0.9 m aisle behind the chairs, head end opposite the door with a wall screen/whiteboard, a credenza/sideboard with coffee and water along a side wall, plants, cameras, optional observer chairs along the walls. Generated 10 rooms (14x10 to 30x20 cells, middle to luxury, office and corp_office, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The table and chair count never scale with the room: the same 11x5-cell table with 12 side chairs + 2 end chairs appears in 14x10 and 30x20 rooms. In large rooms (24x16, 30x20, 26x14) it is a small island in a huge empty carpet field, with 5+ m free on all sides. Table length and chair count must follow the room (a 15 m room has a 9-10 m table with 18-24 chairs, or additional seats along the walls); otherwise the room is wrong-sized.
  - [high] The room is empty apart from the table and 2-4 corner plants: no credenza/sideboard with coffee and water, no whiteboard or second screen, no cabinet, no coat rack, no cameras. A boardroom always has a catering/credenza wall and a screen wall; at high/luxury also a bar, art, a lectern. These are the main missing details.
  - [high] The wall screen is a small blue strip always on the west (left, door-side) wall, a third of the way up, not at the head end of the table and not centred. The screen must be centred on the short wall at the head end of the table, opposite the door (the door is at the foot), and be as wide as about a third of the wall (large screens/video wall for luxury). When the table runs east-west, the screen end walls must be the west or east wall at table centre axis.
  - [medium] Chairs sit with the seat touching each other in an unbroken row of 6, with the end chairs flush; real chairs have about 0.6 m pitch with a little gap, 3-6 per long side matched to table length, and the table surface should reach beyond the outer chair on each side (the table ends flush with the end chairs here). Chairs should be pushed in slightly (touching the table edge), which they are, but the chair rows need a visible gap to the table edge.
  - [medium] The table is centred in the room but not aligned to the door: the door is in the south-west corner, so people enter at the long side near the foot; fine, but the table should stay clear of the door swing by 1 m+ and the table should be oriented along the room's longer axis (it is) and offset slightly towards the head end/screen so the foot end has circulation near the door.
  - [medium] Plants occupy 2-4 corners only, evenly mirrored (e.g. 30x20 with 3 plants in three corners), which reads as stamped. Use 1-2 plants at the window/credenza, not every corner; add a planter or art instead.
  - [low] No observer/second-row chairs along the walls, no side tables, no table-top items (speakerphone, water carafes, holo projector at the table centre for a Shadowrun room), no ceiling projector/camera marks. Add at higher wealth.
### executive_quarters
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m studio to 12x8 m penthouse, plus Shadowrun and minimalist variants): bed with the headboard on a wall, a nightstand each side, wardrobe/dresser on a wall away from the door swing, a seating group (sofa, armchairs, low table, rug, TV opposite), a desk near the window, bar/mini fridge and dining table for rich suites, a walk-in closet and an enclosed bathroom behind its own door, 0.6-0.9 m free on 2-3 bed sides; Shadowrun details: safe, panic button, drone dock, trid. Generated 10 rooms (14x10 to 30x20 cells, middle to luxury, office and corp_office, seeds 1-10) and compared.
  - [high] The suite is just a bed: every result contains one bed, two nightstands, a wardrobe strip, and loose bathroom fixtures, and nothing else, regardless of size (30x20 and 24x16 rooms are 90 % empty carpet). A living suite needs a sitting group (sofa/armchairs/low table on a rug, TV or screen opposite), a desk with a chair, a bar or mini fridge and a dining or coffee table at higher wealth, plus bookshelf, plants and art. Content must scale with area and wealth.
  - [high] Bathroom fixtures stand free in the room: a round shower tray in a corner (sometimes next to the door), a wash basin on the wall and a bath/wc piece in other spots, all with no enclosure or walls, sometimes 8+ m from each other. A bathroom is a compact enclosed zone (partition walls plus its own door), with WC, basin and shower together; an open bedroom must not have a toilet or shower at all. Either build a separate en-suite room or remove these.
  - [high] Bed placement is random: it is in a different corner each time, headboard at the wall (good), but far from the door and sometimes pushed into a corner so only one side is free (eq1, eq5, eq7 one nightstand reaches the corner with the other side free). The bed should sit with the headboard centred on a wall, nightstands on both sides, 0.6+ m free at the foot and both sides, and not directly in line with the entry door. The foot of the bed should have a bench or chest at higher wealth.
  - [high] The wardrobe is a 1x4 cell strip with no relationship to the bed and sits on the door wall, 1 cell from the door (eq2, eq6, eq9): doors of a wardrobe would swing into the entry. Wardrobes belong on a wall with 0.7 m clearance in front, beside or opposite the bed foot; an exec suite should have a walk-in or two wardrobes and a dresser.
  - [medium] Room is entered straight into the empty middle and there is no zoning: no entry vestibule, coat stand, or clear path to a sleeping zone. Large suites should divide into sleeping, sitting and work zones with furniture groups separated, the bed on the quiet far side from the door, the seating group near the door/window.
  - [medium] No Shadowrun/executive flavour: no safe, panic button, trid/wall screen (only 3 of 10 have a small blue screen, always on a wall unrelated to anything), no drone dock, no cyber/med corner. A big TV should face the bed or sofa; the screen should be centred on the wall opposite the bed or sofa.
  - [medium] Rooms are mostly without windows treatment or decorations: no rug under the bed (a rug under the bed's foot half is usual), no lamps, no plants at all. Add a rug, a plant, art.
  - [low] Nightstands are tiny and sit exactly flush with the bed (good), but come without lamps and there is a gap of one cell beside them to the wall in some results; fine, only cosmetic.

## prison.yaml
### prison_lobby
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a real security entry sequence in a compact zone: door, lockers/search, walk-through scanner or airlock, reception behind glass, waiting area; (2) the room sized to its job, not a 600-cell hall; (3) waiting seats in rows facing the desk; (4) wealth/flavour (corporate prison, Shadowrun scanners, drone and sentry presence).
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m county lock-up to 18x10 m corporate-run facility): street door into a small hall; metal-detector gate or airlock with a guard post beside it; locker wall for phones/weapons/personal items; a bullet-proof glazed reception/registration counter with a clerk; 6-20 fixed benches or chairs in rows facing the counter, notice boards, a visitor-rules screen; toilet and vending; a second door (steel, buzzer) into the visiting room/prison; cameras at the ceiling; luxury (private corp prison) adds a visitor lounge, plants, a security lobby with turnstiles; Shadowrun flavour: full-body scanners, sniffer drones, armed guards in armour, ID-chip readers, a Matrix-blocking cage for commlinks. Generated 7 rooms (14x10 to 36x24 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-7) and compared.
  - [high] The lobby grows into an empty hall: 268-588 cells (20x14 to 36x24 cells) with 6-12 objects and about 85 % empty floor (seeds 4, 7). A prison entrance is a compact checkpoint; the extra area must be filled (more benches in rows, a second scanner lane, kiosks, a guard area) or the room capped, the rest becoming corridor or admin rooms.
  - [high] No guard presence at the checkpoint: no guard desk or guard post at the gate or in the lobby (it exists in the visiting room but not here) and no cameras. A security gate without a guard beside it is meaningless. Put a guard station beside the scanner on the inner side and cameras covering door and queue; scale the number of lanes and guards with size and wealth.
  - [high] Security gates are not a walkthrough scanner: a single 1-cell-wide column, 5 cells deep (seeds 1-3, 5), or two columns 2 cells apart (seeds 3, 6, 7) that stand in the middle of the floor. They do not span the room or connect to a wall, so one can simply walk around them. The scanner lane(s) must close the room width (wall to wall or with a barrier), have a funnel of 1.2 m, sit between the door and the reception/inner door, and the queue should form in front of them.
  - [high] Wrong sequence: the reception desk (9x3 cells) stands in the open in the room centre, not behind the gates on the inner side and without glazing/barrier toward the visitors. The order must be door, lockers/waiting, check-in counter, scanner, inner door. The desk should be against a wall, behind glass, with the clerk side not reachable from the visitor side.
  - [medium] Benches are a single 7-cell strip or a vertical strip against a wall, lone and not facing the desk (seed 1: 7 benches north-west along the wall; seed 6: along the west wall; seed 3: one in the north and one in the south-west corner). Waiting seats should be several rows of benches/chairs, facing the counter, with an aisle and grown by room size (~1 seat per 2 cells of lobby above a threshold).
  - [medium] Lockers (10-15 along the north wall) are the far end from the entrance, at the opposite end from the door and far from the scanner (visitors would walk through the whole lobby past the desk to put their belongings away). Put lockers directly beside the entrance or the scanner, before the check, a short 5-8 cell block, not a whole wall.
  - [medium] The visiting-room connection and the staff side are missing: the lobby has only the street door; no door into the prison (steel buzz door after the scanner) is visible, the toilet (wc) sits in the north-west corner inside the room (a 12-cell block carved out of the lobby with a corridor door) far from the visitor flow. Place the wc by the waiting area and the inner door at the end of the scanner lane.
  - [medium] Chair at the desk is a single chair in front of the desk for visitors, 1-3 cells from the desk, not behind it; the clerk chair belongs behind the counter. The wall screens (rules/queue display) sit in odd places (south-east corner near nothing, seed 3 none) and should be above the desk or on the wall the queue faces.
  - [low] Missing small detail: notice board, vending machine, water cooler, clock, fire extinguisher, plants for high/luxury, trash bins are two tiny cells in corners (fine). Luxury (seed 4) looks identical to squatter apart from size: corp prisons should have a lounge, plants, rugs, turnstiles, a drone dock.
  - [low] Shadowrun flavour is absent: no commlink/weapon locker cage, no sniffer drone or sentry turret, no Matrix-null scanner marker. Gate these on wealth/security level.
### visiting_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Realism goal, priority order: (1) a clear two-sided partition: inmates on one side of glass, visitors on the other, each booth with its own stool/phone and an aisle behind each row; (2) guard supervision with an overview from one end; (3) separate entries for inmates and visitors; (4) size and count that match the prison, and (for lower security) a table area; (5) cameras and details.
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x5 m 4-booth cell to 12x10 m mixed hall): a row of 4-12 booths (each ~1 m wide with a glass partition and phone handset, stool on both sides) running along a long wall or as a back-to-back island, visitors on the open side, inmates on the secured side; a guard desk at the end with a view over all booths; two doors, the visitors' door from the lobby and the inmates' door from the corridor, never joined; lower-security prisons have contact visiting with 6-10 fixed tables, benches and a vending machine, children's corner, guard on a raised chair; locker/search table near the door; cameras and a panic button; luxury/corp: private rooms with a screen link (video visits); Shadowrun flavour: Matrix-null cage, AR-blocking, drones in the corners, cyberware scanners at the inmate entrance. Generated 7 rooms (14x10 to 36x22 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-7) and compared.
  - [high] Booths are laid out as a freestanding 5x3 block grid in the middle of the room, with a stool row above and below and 3-4 cells between the rows, i.e. seats on both sides of every block, and no separation of inmate and visitor side: there is no partition wall or glass between the two stool rows and no barrier between the two halves of the room. The visitor and inmate zones must be distinct, a booth row with a glass screen between stools, e.g. booths in one line along a wall with the visitor stools on the room side and the inmate stools behind a secure back corridor, or two sides of a continuous glass counter.
  - [high] Only one door, in the middle of the south wall, serves everybody (seeds 1-7); the YAML intent of "inmates from the corridor" is not visible. Add a second, secured inmate entrance on the opposite side of the partition (and a guard door), so inmates and visitors never cross. Room walls with a visitor door only on the lobby side.
  - [high] Booths are too few for the size and too sparsely placed on very large floors: 600-cell rooms (30x20 cells) have 8-16 booths in 4 rows with 3-4 cell gaps and 25 % empty floor at the north and south ends; luxury (36x22) gets 20 booths and a bare strip of 5 rows at the top. Count must scale with the area and be arranged compactly in a few long rows, with a 1.5 m aisle behind each and fewer blank gaps; a middle gap of 5 cells splits each row in two groups without a purpose (a walkway to nowhere; seed 2: a column of nothing between 4 and 1 booths). Keep one central entry aisle only if it leads to the guard.
  - [high] The guard desk is missing in about 4 of 7 rooms (seeds 1, 2, 5 none), and where it exists it is placed in a corner (seed 6: 3 cells wide, along the west wall; seed 7: a 7x3 desk in the south-east corner behind the door; seed 3: at the top of the room, behind the booths, with a chair 2 cells in front of it). A supervised visit needs a guard with an overview of all booths from the end of the rows or from a raised platform; always at least one guard post, one more per 8-10 booths, and cameras (none present).
  - [medium] Stools are far from the booths (1-2 cells above and below the block) and are unevenly counted: the booth blocks have stools at both the north and south side, so the visitor side and inmate side are not distinguishable, and a different object (chair vs stool) is chosen by wealth with no logic (seed 1-2 stools, seed 3 stool, seed 4 chair, seed 6 stool). Visitors should sit at the room side with a stool bolted to the floor, inmates inside the booth, one per booth on both sides; use bolted furniture throughout.
  - [medium] The door arc opens straight into the blocks of booths on small rooms (seeds 1, 5): the aisle in front of the door is 2-3 cells to the first row. Keep a free 2 m entrance area near the door (and a locker or search table), before the first row of booths.
  - [medium] Missing contact-visit elements for lower security wealth levels: no table group, benches, vending machine, water or toilet, a children's corner, or a search/frisk station at the entry; no phones/handsets (as a separate object) and no screens. At least one variant (squatter/low = cheap bolted tables and benches) should be a table hall.
  - [low] Details: cameras in the corners, wall clock, rules sign, panic button, trash bin, mirror. Luxury (seed 4) and squatter (seed 5) are the same layout at different scale; corp prisons would use video-visit pods with screens and a Matrix-null warning plate.
### prison_block
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 16x14 cell squatter block to a 50x30 cell max-security unit): cells on one or two tiers along both long sides of an open dayroom (the "pod"), cell doors facing in, all visible from one elevated guard station; bolted steel tables with fixed stools in the middle in neat rows; a phone/commcall bank and a trid on the wall; showers at the end, in one run of 3-6 stalls behind a half-wall; a sallyport with two interlocked doors at the entrance; a control bubble with windows to the pod, a second door to the yard/exercise cage, a cleaning closet, a fire exit. Generated 4 blocks (16x14 to 50x30 cells, squatter to luxury, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] Dayroom is empty at larger sizes: a 40x24 block has 5 tables in one top row, 2 stray tables and a handful of loose chairs, 70 % bare floor (seed 3); the 50x30 floor is the same. The dayroom must scale with the number of cells (about 1 table seat per 2 inmates): tables in several even rows, all with the same chair pattern, with a 2 m aisle between rows and a clear way from every cell door to the tables, showers and the exit.
  - [high] Tables are laid out in rows of identical sets but the leftover sets (seed 3: one table at the west wall with a chair above and below, another chair floating in the middle) are dropped in random bays with chairs that belong to no table. Rows must stay complete (fill rows, drop the remainder) and chairs must sit only at the long sides of a table.
  - [high] No guard sight line and no control point: the guard post/desk is missing in most seeds, and where it exists (seed 3) it is a 7x3 desk in the middle of the south part with one chair behind it, no sight to the cell doors, unrelated to the control room. A block always needs one elevated guard position or the control bubble with windows onto the dayroom, centred on the long axis so every cell door is visible; cameras in the corners.
  - [high] Cell doors face the dayroom but all cells on one side open onto a 3-cell-wide strip directly in front of the door and then the dayroom floor without any walkway: tables or showers can stand right in front of the cell doors on narrow blocks. Keep a continuous 2 m wide walk (the tier) along the cell fronts, free of furniture, and put furniture only beyond it.
  - [medium] Showers are a single 3x3 block in a corner (seeds 2, 3: one stall block at the south-west wall, also a lone block in the middle of the south wall) and not every block has them. A block needs a run of 4-6 shower stalls along one end wall, with a drying/changing strip and a half-height partition, and a wall of phones (3-4 commcall terminals, one in every seed) rather than one random terminal.
  - [medium] Wall screens are in short 5-cell strips at random walls (one above the cell row, one at the bottom corner), not bracketed to a viewing area. A trid belongs to the TV zone: bolted benches or tables facing it, on the end wall opposite the guard post, at one height.
  - [medium] Cells are all the same (4x6 cells = 2x3 m) and always one door per cell; no wealth difference (squatter and luxury look the same apart from scale). Squatter/low should be dormitory-like (bunk rows, no cell doors, an open barrack) and high/luxury more single-occupancy cells, a second tier with a gallery and stairs, cell numbering.
  - [medium] The entrance door (3 cells, the sallyport) is centred in the south wall right next to the table rows or the shower block, no airlock zone and no search table; keep 3 m free in front, with a guard station beside it. A second door (fire exit to the yard) at the opposite end is missing in every block.
  - [low] Details: trash bins and a mop/cleaning closet, a notice board with the schedule, a water cooler, wall clock, a lone chair at the middle (seed 3: chair at the guard desk is the only chair). Fire extinguishers, emergency stop button and a megaphone/alarm panel at the guard post.
### dayroom
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x10 cell low-security common room to 20x30 cell high-security pod floor): bolted tables in rows (4 stools each, fixed), trid on a wall with a benches row facing it, a phone bank (2-4 handsets with a divider and a queue line), a guard desk or elevated post at the entry or centre, water fountain, a shower run at one end, a small book shelf/rec corner, a trash bin; the rest is open floor for cell traffic. Generated 3 rooms (10x8 to 30x20 cells, low to high, seeds 1-3) and compared; the room is the free part of a prison block so it is generated alone as an open hall.
  - [high] Large floors are mostly bare: 30x20 cells has one row of 6 table sets at the top and a 20 row void between them and the guard desk (seed 3); there is nothing in the middle, no second table row. The tables must fill the area in several rows (1 set per ~20-25 cells of floor) with 2-3 cell aisles, a central aisle between entry and guard post; leave the walking zone only in front of the cell doors.
  - [high] Guard desk at the door is a 7x3 slab straight in front of the entrance with one chair 2 cells above it (seed 3), jammed against the south wall, facing the wrong way (the chair is at the north side, the room side, the desk behind it); the guard must see the whole hall, so the post goes at the opposite end or elevated in the middle, with its chair behind the desk facing the tables. It is also missing in the small rooms (seeds 1, 2).
  - [high] Showers placed beside the table rows (seed 2: 3x3 block between table sets at the south wall, another at the east wall; seed 1: top right) with chairs directly beside the wet area. Showers belong together on one wall (a run of 3-4 stalls) away from the tables, never in the table grid; the dayroom is a clean, dry zone.
  - [medium] Table sets are made of 3x3 canteen tables with exactly 2 chairs, always at the short top/bottom edge (c c above and below), so a table seats 4 on the long edges. Chairs should be along the long sides (4 per table) and be stools bolted to the table; the pattern is mirrored vertically, which is fine, but the sets start 2 cells from the wall and ignore the door side.
  - [medium] Wall screens in the corner at the east wall in a vertical 4x1 strip with nothing facing it, trash bin in the north-west corner away from tables, terminal next to the screen at the bottom (seed 3): no viewing area. Needs a TV corner with benches in rows facing the screen, a phone bank with 3-4 handsets side by side on a wall with dividers (not one terminal), a trash bin per table group.
  - [medium] Missing the usual things: a water fountain, book/game shelf, notice board, a cleaning closet, fire extinguisher, cameras; no windows is correct. The dayroom has no wealth variation: low/high look the same, luxury has no softer furniture (couch, exercise corner, game tables) and squatter no ruin.
  - [low] The room is only the leftover of a block and has no door on its own; as a standalone it gets one door in the middle of the south wall which is right in the walkway of the table rows.
### prison_cell
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x6 to 6x8 cells = 2x3 to 3x4 m): single and double occupancy; a bunk (or two) along one long wall, a steel toilet-basin unit in the far corner opposite the door, a small fixed steel desk and stool under the window slot or on the wall, a shelf above the bunk, a mirror (polished steel), a door with a food slot and a small observation window, a ceiling light and a call button; the bed head is at the back wall, the bed does not touch the toilet; a dorm cell for 3-4 with two bunk stacks. Generated 4 cells (4x6 to 6x8, low to luxury, seeds 1-4) and compared (the standalone room uses the free-form furniture list; in a prison block the template with the door in the south wall is used, the standalone ASCII cells show how it looks when the template does not fit).
  - [high] Standalone cells are almost empty and wrong: in a 5x6 cell the bunk and toilet are only some of the elements but in a 4x6 cell only a bunk (seed 1) and not even a toilet; the 6x8 luxury cell has a lone bunk in the middle of the room with the toilet in a corner at the front (seed 4), i.e. the bunk is free-standing and 3 cells away from every wall. A bunk always stands with its long side against a wall and its head to the back wall; the toilet in the back corner, away from the door.
  - [high] Toilet next to the door or on the wrong side: in seed 3 the bunk is at the door end, the toilet at the back; in seed 4 it is the other way round. A toilet unit always in the corner farthest from the door (or at the head end), the bunk along the other wall; there must be a free standing zone of about 1 m x 1 m just inside the door.
  - [high] Cells are too large and empty for their content: up to 25 % of the floor is used by two objects; add what makes a cell: a bolted desk with a stool (or a shelf unit), a steel mirror/sign, a shelf above the bunk, and for double cells a second bunk. For high/luxury (a privileged ward) add a locker, a trid, a rug.
  - [medium] The door is 3 cells wide (1.5 m) in the large cells and 2 cells (1 m) in the standard: a cell door is one narrow door of 0.8-1 m and opens outward; keep 2 cells everywhere and avoid a door in the middle of the wall of the larger variant (seed 4).
  - [medium] In template cells (block) the bunk is always 3 wide at the left of the door and the toilet 2 wide: good, but the 5x6 variant has the toilet and a shelf in the right column directly beside the bunk and nothing for the standing space, so the whole cell is a 2 m x 1.5 m slit. Fine for a cell, but a second cot or a desk should appear in a double cell.
  - [low] Details: wall-mounted fold-down table, a call button/camera in the corner, a bunk numbering plate, a pin-up on the wall, a window slit in the outer cells (windows: forbidden here: inner blocks have none, but a cell on the outer wall of a block should have a small barred window).
### block_sallyport
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x4 to 14x8 cells): a small vestibule between two interlocked heavy doors (only one open at a time, both visible from the control bubble), a metal detector or pat-down zone, a guard desk or window of the control room right beside it, a camera in each corner, intercom panel and door-release buttons beside each door, a floor mark/yellow line, a bench for waiting visitors or an inmate-transfer hold (a cage) in the larger variants, a fire extinguisher, a drain in the floor. Generated 3 rooms (6x4 to 14x8 cells, low to high, seeds 1-3) and compared.
  - [high] The room is empty: seeds 1 and 2 contain at most a fire extinguisher, and seed 2 (10x6) nothing at all, just a big empty box with a door. A sallyport must read as a controlled airlock: a security gate/detector across the room at the middle, a guard window or desk beside it, a camera and intercom at each door; no room should be left without anything.
  - [high] Only one door in the standalone room (south wall in the middle); the whole point of a sallyport is two doors on opposite walls (or at least on different sides), interlocked. The second door must be present and placed so that the path crosses the room (not both on the same wall), and the room should be a narrow passage (2-4 m wide), not a hall (14x8 cells, 7 m x 4 m, seed 3 is a hall with a gate in the corner).
  - [high] Security gate is a 5-cell strip pushed into the east corner of the room (seed 3: bottom right, along the wall), so it neither blocks the route nor works as a checkpoint; in a real sallyport the detector stands in the middle of the passage and everyone walks through it; with a table for items, a tray and a guard next to it.
  - [medium] Fire extinguisher (seed 1) stands in the corner next to the door: fine, but the wall is not visible in a sallyport without cameras and an alarm panel; add them always, plus a wall-mounted intercom beside each door and a door-release panel inside the guard window.
  - [medium] No wealth or security variation: a corp prison sallyport would have two scanners, drones and turrets; a low one a plain door with a bench. Scale the checkpoint (1 gate to 3 gates) with the size of the room.
  - [low] Details: a floor drain, light strips, "one door at a time" sign, a bench for transfers, shackle rail along the wall.
### block_control
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 to 14x10 cells): the guards' bubble beside a cell block, glazed on the block side with a window wall; a long console desk with 2-4 monitors and door/lock controls facing the glass, 1-2 swivel chairs, a weapon locker and key cabinet on the back wall, a coffee machine and a small table, a camera wall of 4-8 screens, a phone/radio charger, a door to the block and a second to the corridor, cell-door release panel and an alarm panel, a fire extinguisher. Generated 4 rooms (8x6 to 14x9 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] The console desk is not facing anything: it stands in the room middle (seed 3: 5x3 desk at the top, chairs 5 cells below, in front of it) or as a 3-wide column on the west wall (seeds 1, 4) with the chair beside it, and the chairs are 3-6 cells away in the open (seed 4: two chairs a column away from the desks). A console has one chair directly behind it at the working side, and the whole row faces the window/door to the block (the long wall), all consoles in one line.
  - [high] Wall screens as strips of 5 on the walls opposite the consoles or in the room's corner (seed 2 top-left, seed 4 two on the north wall and a column of 5 at the left), which do not form a monitor wall behind or above the consoles. Group the screens in one wall as a camera wall facing the consoles' chairs.
  - [high] The window to the block is not modelled: the room has no windows (windows: forbidden for the whole prison) and the door (3 cells, in the south wall) is the only link, so the room reads as a normal office; at least the wall facing the cell block should be glass (an interior window or a long window opening). This is the defining feature of a control bubble. (Door to dayroom via `connect` is only in a block.)
  - [high] Room is half empty: a 14x9 cell room has one console, a locker, a screen and the rest bare (seed 3); small rooms (8x6) have one console and one screen. Fill with key cabinet, weapon locker, coffee machine and a small table, a second console desk for large ones, phone/charger, a first-aid box; scale with area.
  - [medium] Coffee machine and weapon locker in single seeds only (locker at the north-west corner in seed 3, with nothing near it); the weapon locker should be near the door to the corridor (so that the guards can arm themselves), and a key box by the console. The coffee machine goes beside a small table, not a lone item.
  - [medium] The route door-to-block is blocked in some layouts by the console desk in front of the door (seed 2: desk lines at the south wall right next to the door, with the door arc of 3 cells almost touching it). Keep a 1.5 m path from each door to the consoles.
  - [low] Details: cameras, intercom, a second chair, fire extinguisher, panic buttons, notice board with shift roster. Higher wealth: more screens, a drone dock/turret panel, a second operator.
### segregation_unit
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 12x8 to 30x14 cells): one row of 4-12 single cells along one long wall (or two rows on both sides of a central walk), each cell with a heavy solid door with food slot, a narrow 1.5-2 m walk in front, a guard desk with a monitor wall at the end of the walk with sight along all cell doors, a shower cell and a phone cage at the other end, a sallyport at the entrance, cameras every 3 cells, a recreation cage on the unit's end for 1-person yard time, no windows. Generated 4 units (12x8 to 30x14 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] The walk in front of the cells is a big empty hall: the cells fill one row of 3 cm at the north (about 7 cells deep) and the whole rest of the floor, 9-12 cells deep (e.g. 20x16: 7 rows of cells and 9 of empty floor in the south part, seed 4; 30x14: the rest is empty), is bare. A segregation walk is a corridor in front of the cells (2-3 m), not a room of 12 x 8 m; the cell rows should be allowed to grow deeper (two rows of cells on both sides of a narrow walk) or the unit must be compact: cell row plus a 3-cell walk plus the control point, and drop the excess area to other rooms.
  - [high] The unit is a single row of cells on one side only (every seed), with all the doors on one wall; a walk with cells on both sides is the standard layout (a linear block) and doubles the capacity. At least when the room is wider than 10 cells and deep enough (d >= 14) place a second row on the opposite wall.
  - [high] Guard post missing in most seeds and where present (seed 3: a 7x3 desk at the south wall, with the first cell 14 cells away) it is far from the cells and facing the wrong way; for solitary the guard station sits at the end of the walk looking down the whole line of doors, with a monitor wall and a camera at each cell; always at least one.
  - [medium] Cell count is capped (6 here) while the room is larger (seed 3: 6 cells and a big empty rectangle at the east end of 11 cells x 9, unused), so the east end is dead space; better to distribute the extra cells or add shower/rec cage/phone cage rooms in this space (the yard cage is the signature element of a solitary unit: a 2x3 m outdoor cage).
  - [medium] No wealth variation: all four seeds are the same row of identical cells; high/luxury (corp detention) would get glass-fronted cells with cameras and a sensor field; squatter would have simple cells with one bucket toilet and rusty doors.
  - [low] Details: a shower stall (one), phone cage, cameras along the walk, a floor drain, a wall clock, an intercom beside the entrance, a sign. The entrance (door in the middle of the south wall) is far from the cells; put it near one end so that the guard post sits beside it.
### segregation_walk
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 12x4 to 24x4 cells): a narrow 1.5-2 m corridor with the cell doors on one or both sides, bare painted concrete floor with a yellow stripe, a guard chair or desk at one end with a window/monitor, cameras on the ceiling every 3-4 m, a wall phone cage and a trolley with meal trays, intercom and fire extinguisher; with extra width a bench for escorted moves. Generated 1 room and tried a second one (20x4 cells; the smallest allowed depth is 4 cells, 12x3 is refused with "building must be at least 4 cells on each side") and compared.
  - [high] The room is completely empty in the standalone generation (20x4 cells with nothing but the door, seed 2): the YAML only has an optional guard desk (count 0-1). A walk is a bare corridor but still has a guard post, cameras, an intercom and a meal cart; with no furniture at all it reads as unused space. Make the guard desk (or chair) always present at one end, facing the length of the walk, with a camera and intercom at the entrance.
  - [medium] The smallest depth in the standalone building is 4 cells (2 m), which is the real width of the walk; the room in a unit is the free area and can become wide as the room grows (up to 160 cells): then it is a hall, not a walk (see segregation_unit). Rooms of more than ~5 cells depth should be split off or filled with other items (shower cell, phone cage, yard cage).
  - [medium] The door is a 3-cell door in the middle of the long wall, so every route splits the walk into two halves; the entry belongs at one end of the walk, the guard post at the other end.
  - [low] Details: floor stripe, wall numbers, a bench, fire extinguisher, cart. Wealth differences are not visible.
### solitary_cell
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x5 to 5x6 cells = 2x2.5 to 2.5x3 m): the same as a cell but stripped: a concrete or steel bunk shelf along one wall, a steel toilet-basin unit at its foot, no desk, no shelf, a thick door with food slot and observation port, a single recessed light and a camera in the ceiling; padded variant for a suicide-watch cell with no fixtures; a floor drain. Generated 2 cells (4x5 and 5x6, low and middle, seeds 1-2) and compared (standalone free-form placement, the block template is the stand-in).
  - [high] The cot is free-standing in the middle of the 4x5 cell with the toilet missing (seed 1: cot 3 cells wide, centred, 1 cell from either side wall, no toilet at all); a solitary cell without toilet makes no sense, and the cot must have its long side against the wall. The toilet unit must always be there, in the back corner.
  - [high] 5x6 cell (seed 2) is exactly the normal prison cell: a cot plus a toilet at the east wall, and the cell's door in the south wall facing the room with the cot against the back wall; fine as the layout, but the cell is the same size as a normal cell, so solitary and ordinary cells look alike; the solitary cell must be smaller and plainer, with no shelf or other extras.
  - [medium] Door: a 2-cell door (1 m) is right but the arc opens into the walk; the door should be a heavy door with a food slot (a hatch object or a marked part of the wall) and the cell has a camera in the corner; both are missing.
  - [low] Details: floor drain, a ceiling camera and recessed light, the bare walls. Higher security levels: a padded cell (no objects) or a cage front instead of a door.
### intake_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 to 20x12 cells): a processing line along the room: a counter/desk with a terminal and a guard at the entry, a bench for the waiting inmates (bolted, along a wall), a scanner/body scanner in the middle of the route, a strip-search booth or curtain, a fingerprint/photo station (a stand in front of a measuring wall), a shelf/rack with jumpsuits and sandals, a lockers row for personal items, a shower/delousing and a drain, a door into the block and one to the sallyport (the line flows from the entry door through the scan to the issue counter and out); cameras. Generated 4 rooms (8x6 to 20x12 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] There is no flow: the counter, bench, scanner and shelves are scattered on random walls (seed 1: counter at the top-left, a bench column on the east wall, shelves on the west wall; seed 2: a bench at the top, shelves at the west wall and a counter plus shelves in the south, beside the door), and there is only the one door through which everyone enters and leaves. A real intake room is a line: door in, bench, scan, search/photo, issue counter with jumpsuits, door out to the block; the objects must be arranged in that order along the room and the room needs a second door (to the block) at the other end.
  - [high] The scanner (seed 3) is a 7x11 block (3.5 x 5.5 m) in the middle-left of the room; a body scanner is a 1 m frame that people walk through (an arch 1 x 2 m); here it is a huge slab that takes half the room and cannot be walked through. It must be small, stand across the route (or against the wall as a baggage scanner) with a free approach of 1.5 m.
  - [high] Rooms are very bare at larger sizes: the 20x12 cell room (seed 4) has a bench, a counter, two terminals and two shelves, the whole middle is empty (about 80 % empty floor); the 16x10 room has a counter, a scanner and a bench; there is no search area, no photo/print station, no lockers or the weighing scale, no shower. Scale the contents with the area (several processing stations), and with large size add a holding cell/bench pen for the arrivals.
  - [high] Benches in a single 7-cell strip by the door or the east wall (seeds 1-4), unrelated to the queue: waiting bench along a wall facing the counter, or a bolted bench pair in the middle; a second bench inside the strip-search zone is common. The bench by the entry in the south-east corner at the door arc blocks the route (seed 4: bench next to the door). Keep 2 m free in front of the door.
  - [medium] The counter is never a processing counter with a guard side: a 7-cell counter alone on the wall with its terminal 5-10 cells away (seed 4: terminal at the opposite side, a second terminal in the south). Terminal(s) must stand on the counter, one per clerk, with a chair behind and the customer side facing the queue.
  - [medium] Shelves for jumpsuits are in three disconnected groups on three walls (seed 4: three shelves in the south at the door, one in the middle) and the stack for sandals/boots is missing; put them in one run behind the issue counter, the shelves aligned in a row, with the property bins nearby.
  - [low] Details: camera, a floor mark, rules sign, a scale/height chart, a drain in the shower, a toilet for urine tests. Wealth: a corp intake has a body scanner and biometric stand; squatter just a table and bench.
### property_room
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 to 20x14 cells): a window counter at the entry (the clerk side with a terminal and a scale), behind it rows of shelving or steel racks with numbered sealed bins and hanging garment bags, a trolley for deliveries, a label printer on the counter, a lockable cage for valuables (weapons, cyberdecks, commlinks), a safe, a camera and a card-reader door; the rows run perpendicular to the counter with a 1 m aisle between them, often with a mobile rack on rails; for high security a Faraday cabinet for electronics. Generated 4 rooms (6x5 to 20x14 cells, low to luxury, seeds 1-4) and compared.
  - [high] The shelf rows are very regular (good), but they run parallel to the door wall as long rows of 3-cell shelves with a gap between each pair of shelves in the same row and nothing else in the room: seed 2 is two rows of four shelves, seed 4 four rows of eight shelves with a hole in one place in each row, and a free zone of 6 cells in front of the door. Real racks form a continuous run along the rows with no gap between neighbours, are deeper (shelf units with 2+ cells depth) and are accessed from aisles of at least 1 m; the row direction should lead away from the door so you see down the aisles.
  - [high] There is no counter or it is in the wrong place: in seed 3 the counter is an 11-cell strip along the east wall, which does not face the door and has the shelf rows directly in front of it (the row at the counter's south end touches it); seeds 1, 2, 4 have none. The clerk's counter must stand at the entrance, across the room's width, with the shelves behind it, a terminal and a chair at the clerk's side, and the customer side towards the door.
  - [high] No other equipment: no terminal, scale, label printer, cart, safe or lockable cage for valuables, no camera: it reads as a storeroom (an archive) and not as a property room. Add a counter with terminal, a cart, a safe/lockable cage with the weapons and electronics, cameras, a bench for the inmate to check his items.
  - [medium] The room has a bare strip at the entry and the rows start 2 cells from the side walls but leave a 5-6 cell dead strip at the north end (seeds 1-3: the top 4-5 rows empty). Shelves should fill the room from the back wall, with the front part used for the counter.
  - [medium] Door in the middle of the south wall; with the counter at the door the clerk side must have its own door (a second door into the prison corridor) or a pass-through hatch. Fine for small rooms, but at least a window/hatch on the wall to the intake room is needed.
  - [low] Details: bin labels, garment racks, a mop/cleaning cart, a fire extinguisher, an alarm panel. Wealth changes nothing visible; high/luxury could have an electronic locker wall and a robot picker.
### infirmary
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 6x5 m to 15x9 m): 2-8 beds in a row along one long wall with 0.9-1.2 m between them and the heads against the wall, a curtain/rail between beds, a nurse station or desk with a terminal at the door side with sight on all beds, an exam table in the middle or a treatment corner with a light, a locked medicine cabinet next to the station, a sink at the entrance, a crash cart by the door, a quarantine/isolation bay in bigger ones, cameras; the bed count and the second (secure) door scale with the prison size. Generated 5 rooms (10x8 to 30x18, low to luxury, seeds 1-5) and compared.
  - [high] Beds are scattered as isolated 3x7 blocks on random walls and in the middle (10x8: one bed in the middle of the north half, one against the south wall, one east; 24x14: three beds in a block at the north-west with gaps and two floating beds in the south with nothing around them). A ward is a regular row: beds side by side along one wall (heads to the wall) at equal spacing, or two facing rows with a central aisle. Same orientation for all beds, no bed in the middle of the room.
  - [high] No exam table and no nurse/doctor desk or terminal (seeds 1-5: only beds, a cabinet, a cart and sinks); the room reads as a dormitory. An infirmary needs a staff station near the door, an exam/treatment table with free access on three sides, a medicine cabinet beside the station, a sink and a crash cart at the entrance.
  - [high] The room does not scale: the 30x18 room has 8 beds and 80 % bare floor, the 10x8 room nearly the same list; counts should follow the area (beds per area, a second ward bay, an isolation room, a waiting bench) and wealth (a corp prison has a surgery bay, a squatter lockup a cot and a first-aid box).
  - [medium] Sinks stand alone as 3-cell blocks on the side walls (10x8: west wall, 24x14: far west) or clustered at the north; a hand sink belongs next to the door and next to the station/exam area, not at random walls. Medicine cabinets stand in the middle of the south wall at the door, in the entry line (seeds 1, 2, 5: right in front of the door); keep the area in front of the door free and put them behind the station.
  - [medium] No separation or security: no curtains/screens between beds, no restraint beds or a barred isolation cell for dangerous inmates, no guard post or camera, and no second door; an infirmary behind a locked door should have a guard desk or window at the entrance.
  - [low] Details: IV stands, privacy curtains, bin, a scale, a wall chart, oxygen panel; the wealth differences (clean beds, extra equipment) are not visible.
### mess_hall
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m to 24x14 m): long bolted tables with fixed benches in parallel rows with a 1.5-2 m aisle, the rows run along the axis from the door to the servery, a serving counter (hatch) with a tray rail and a queue lane along one short wall next to the kitchen, a tray return window or rack, a drink/water station, guard posts on a raised spot or in two corners with a view of all rows, cameras, trash and tray carts at the exit; the seating is spaced to hold 1 inmate per 1 m of bench. Generated 5 rooms (10x8 to 30x18, low to luxury, seeds 1-5) and compared.
  - [high] Tables are good as aligned rows, but each is a 3x3 block with only two single chairs 1 cell away on one side (10x8: `c c   c c` above and below, the middle chairs not touching the table; 24x14: chair rows floating 1 cell away). A mess hall table has benches or stools along both long sides, touching the table, one per ~1 m. Chairs must be adjacent to the table and on both sides; also bolted stools, not loose chairs.
  - [high] Rows leave dead lanes: the 30x18 hall has the same 4+4 table grid twice with a huge empty band along the south and north, the counter is a short 7-cell strip in a corner or on the west wall with no queue lane (seed 5: counter far north-west, 3 rows from the door; seed 1: counter on the west wall behind the tables). The counter must be a long serving line on the wall next to the kitchen, all tables facing/leading to it with a free queue lane (2 cells) in front, and the grid should fill the free area evenly, centred, not offset.
  - [high] The guard desk is a 3x7 slab at the south-east corner by the door or a 7x3 block in the middle of the north area (seed 2: guard desk in the north-west, standing in the free space behind the table rows with chairs touching it), occupying the door route. Guards stand at a wall in two corners or on a platform with a sight line over all tables, not in the entry line.
  - [medium] No tray return, drink dispenser, water cooler, or condiments; trash bins are a single 1-cell U in a corner far from the exit. Add a tray return window/shelf near the exit and a trash/tray cart next to the door, and a camera in every corner.
  - [medium] The door is on the south wall in the middle, tables begin 4 cells north of it, but the hall looks like a school canteen; no wealth variation (squatter and luxury differ only by count). Squatter: fewer long tables and plain; corp: smaller round tables, vending machines.
  - [low] Details: floor line markings, a menu board, serving trays, cleaning trolley, fire extinguisher, a windows-free wall.
### prison_kitchen
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m to 20x12 m): a cooking line (stoves/ranges and fryers in one run on one wall, hood above, 1 m prep counter in front, 1.2 m aisle behind), a central prep island, a dish-wash station with sinks and a rack at a different wall, a walk-in fridge/freezer and dry store on the back wall, shelving for goods, a pass-through window to the mess hall, a delivery door, a hand sink at the entrance, a drain, knife cabinet locked; only trusty inmates are in and the guard looks in. Generated 5 rooms (10x8 to 30x18, low to luxury, seeds 1-5) and compared.
  - [high] Equipment is scattered on separate walls in single blocks (10x8: sinks at the north next to one stove, a counter column on the west and east walls, stoves in the south east corner and east wall; 28x16: stoves on both side walls, sinks on both sides, counter pieces at north and south). A kitchen is a workflow: stoves in one continuous run with the work counter facing them, a prep table island in the middle with a 1.2 m aisle all round, sinks grouped at one end, fridges in a row beside the delivery/store door. Sort by function and place each group on one wall.
  - [high] No central island or prep table (all seeds): the middle of the room is empty (18x12: 80 % bare), the counters stand against walls without relationship to the stoves. Counters must be in front of the stove run (work triangle: fridge, sink, stove) and a free-standing prep island added from medium sizes up; stove runs must have a hood and a 1 m free workspace.
