  - [high] Fridges and shelves are lone single objects (a fridge in the south at the door, 2 fridges apart at the south wall, shelf at the west wall); a walk-in/cold-store should be a room or a row of 2-4 fridges and freezers together, shelving in rows behind them, not next to the entrance. Missing: dish-wash station with a rack, trolleys, pots, bins, knife locker, floor drain, extinguishers in the right spot (near the stoves, not in the corner at the door).
  - [medium] Missing the serving hatch to the mess hall and a delivery door; the door sits in the middle of the south wall facing the room, and the objects start 4-5 cells away, so the entry is empty, the working zone is up north. Doors should be near the serving side, with the cooking line against the opposite wall.
  - [medium] Scale and wealth: the 28x16 kitchen has about the same objects as 10x8 with more empty floor; the count must scale (more stoves, a second island, a bigger dish station). Squatter vs. corporate differences are not visible.
  - [low] Details: hand sink, apron hooks, a blackboard menu, vent hoods, cleaning cart, trash bins (none at all in the generated rooms).
### prison_laundry
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x6 m to 18x12 m): rows of industrial washers on one wall, dryers on the opposite or the same wall, each with 1 m free in front, a central folding table (long counter) with a sorting bin cart, rolling carts for dirty and clean linen near the door (dirty side and clean side separated), shelves for clean stacks, an ironing/mangle station, a detergent store, a floor drain and a sink, a counter for issue; two doors in a bigger plant (dirty in, clean out). Generated 5 rooms (10x8 to 30x18, low to luxury, seeds 1-5) and compared.
  - [high] Machines are not grouped: washers and dryers are single machines at random wall spots and corners (10x8: washers in the four corners and along the north, a dryer at the top; 28x16: a lone washer at the west wall and a row of three at the south). A laundry has a bank: all washers side by side in a continuous row against one wall with the dryers in a row opposite or beside, each of the same orientation with the doors facing the working aisle.
  - [high] Too few machines for the area (28x16: five machines, the 18x12: four); a prison laundry serves hundreds of inmates and needs 6-20 big machines in banks. The count must follow the area, and with the machines lined up a 1.5 m work aisle in front of them.
  - [high] The counter is a 7-cell piece floating in the middle of the room (10x8: counter in the room centre, 18x12 a vertical column in the middle beside a cart column) and not related to the machines; it should be a long folding table in the centre between the machine banks with carts at its ends, or the issue counter at the door. Laundry carts stand in columns in the middle of the room; they must stay in groups next to the doors or at the machine banks (dirty at one end, clean at the other).
  - [medium] Missing: shelves for the clean piles, ironing/pressing station, sink/drain, detergent store, a table for sorting, trash bin, a drying rack and a camera. The room is laid out as a hall with no zones (dirty / washing / drying / folding).
  - [medium] The room has no windows and a single door in the south; a bigger laundry should have a second door for the clean-linen exit and a vent; the door arc and the first row start close together, fine.
  - [low] Details: sinks, a bucket, wall clock, safety sign, floor drain; wealth is not visible.

Add: a rack/dumbbell shelf along one wall, a pull-up frame, a mat zone, a boxing bag in higher variants; the types should depend on the size (small cell yard: only a bar and benches).

Benches along the walls (q) are single segments in the corner or 11 cells long (28x16: a column on the west wall); keep them for waiting/spotters at the sides.

The warden's desk should stand in the room, facing the door, the warden's chair behind (towards the back wall), the guest chairs on the door side opposite the warden and facing the desk. Here the guest chairs are on the same side as the warden chair.

Filing cabinets are spread as singles at three walls; they should be in a continuous run on one wall behind the desk or beside it.

## public.yaml
### squad_room
- Status: done
- Findings:

  - [low] Details: umbrella stand, shoe mat, candles at a statue, a plant; luxury differs from squatter only by size.

- Status: done
- Findings:

  - [high] The loft is just a block of pews in 3-wide strips, even in the small variant, with no relation to the open edge: pews must face the balustrade/the altar direction, in 2-3 stepped rows with a gap behind for walking, instead of a floating grid in the middle of the room. The 12x8 squatter room is a plain pew field with no organ at all.

  - [medium] The room scales by tripling the pew grid (30x16: 12 pew blocks in 4 rows, no choir space, no walkway to the organ); a loft is a narrow room, so cap the size or add a clear gallery edge, music stands, a hymn cabinet.

### crypt
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 5x4 m to 14x8 m): a vault below the church: sarcophagi or wall niches (loculi) along the walls, a central row of tombs with a walkway all round, an altar or small chapel niche opposite the stair, candle stands at the corners of the walkways, ossuary shelves with skulls, a grave slab with an inscription, pillars supporting the vault; luxury has carved tombs with statues, a reliquary, an iron gate.

Real crypts have tombs in short rows with walkable aisles of 2+ cells on all sides, aligned with the room axis, or tombs set in wall niches; the empty half of the room should hold the altar, ossuary, pillars.
  - [medium] Candle stands are scattered at wall spots at random (12x8: three in the corners, 24x14: three along the south wall far from the tombs); put them beside the altar and at the head of the tomb rows, and add wall sconces.

### sally_port
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 8x5 m to 16x8 m): the secured vehicle lock at the entrance: a big roll-up gate at the outside (and a second gate on the inner side so only one is open), 1-2 cars parked in the middle in a line or at an angle, a guard booth with window beside the lane, weapon lockers for checking in weapons, a bench for those waiting, cameras, floor drain, a bollard, fire extinguisher, barrier arm; prison variant has a body-search area and a cage.

Group them: sinks and fume hoods together (wet wall), lockers and the exam table at the door, computers with the microscopes on one dry wall.

Rooms ought to scale with the number of rooms, not with the floor area: hallway 84 cells is a hall not a corridor.

### butchery
- Status: done
- Findings:
  - Blind design (10 sketches, 4x3 m to 8x6 m): a butcher block or stainless table in the middle, a meat rail/hooks along a wall (or cold room next door), a counter and a sink along the wall with a handwash sink by the door, a band saw/grinder, a scale, a fridge, a floor drain, a bin, an apron hook. Generated 3 sizes and compared.
  - [high] The butcher blocks form a 3x6 or 6x3 block in the middle of the room in the high case and the meat rail runs along other walls; counter and sink are at yet other walls: butcher block, rail and sink must be a working line: rail above or beside the block, the sink within 2 m, the counter on one wall.
  - [high] Does not scale: 384 cells with two butcher blocks, a rail, a counter and a sink; the room is 90 % empty. A big butchery has multiple blocks in a row, a saw, grinder, scale and cold storage; small rooms need only one.
  - [medium] No band saw, scale, floor drain or bin; the sink alone at the south wall away from the block; no wealth difference.
  - [low] Details: apron hooks, a knife strip, a hose, a drain.
